```
1	---
2	name: quality-analysis
3	description: Comprehensive quality analysis for BMad workflows and skills. Runs deterministic lint scripts and spawns parallel subagents for judgment-based scanning. Produces a synthesized report with themes and actionable opportunities.
4	menu-code: QA
5	---
6	
7	# Quality Analysis
8	
9	Communicate with user in `{communication_language}`. Write report content in `{document_output_language}`.
10	
11	You orchestrate quality analysis on a BMad workflow or skill. Deterministic checks run as scripts (fast, zero tokens). Judgment-based analysis runs as LLM subagents. A report creator synthesizes everything into a unified, theme-based report.
12	
13	## Your Role: Coordination, Not File Reading
14	
15	**DO NOT read the target skill's files yourself.** Scripts and subagents do all analysis.
16	
17	You orchestrate: run deterministic scripts and pre-pass extractors, spawn LLM scanner subagents in parallel, then hand off to the report creator for synthesis.
18	
19	## Headless Mode
20	
21	If `{headless_mode}=true`, skip all user interaction, use safe defaults, note any warnings, and output structured JSON as specified in the Present Findings section.
22	
23	## Pre-Scan Checks
24	
25	Check for uncommitted changes. In headless mode, note warnings and proceed. In interactive mode, inform the user and confirm before proceeding. In interactive mode, also confirm the workflow is currently functioning.
26	
27	## Analysis Principles
28	
29	**Effectiveness over efficiency.** The analysis may suggest leaner phrasing, but if the current phrasing captures the right guidance, it should be kept. Over-optimization can make skills lose their effectiveness. The report presents opportunities — the user applies judgment.
30	
31	## Scanners
32	
33	### Lint Scripts (Deterministic — Run First)
34	
35	These run instantly, cost zero tokens, and produce structured JSON:
36	
37	| #   | Script                           | Focus                                   | Output File                |
38	| --- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
39	| S1  | `scripts/scan-path-standards.py` | Path conventions                        | `path-standards-temp.json` |
40	| S2  | `scripts/scan-scripts.py`        | Script portability, PEP 723, unit tests | `scripts-temp.json`        |
41	
42	### Pre-Pass Scripts (Feed LLM Scanners)
43	
44	Extract metrics so LLM scanners work from compact data instead of raw files:
45	
46	| #   | Script                                  | Feeds                        | Output File                       |
47	| --- | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
48	| P1  | `scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py` | workflow-integrity scanner   | `workflow-integrity-prepass.json` |
49	| P2  | `scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py`     | prompt-craft scanner         | `prompt-metrics-prepass.json`     |
50	| P3  | `scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py`     | execution-efficiency scanner | `execution-deps-prepass.json`     |
51	
52	### LLM Scanners (Judgment-Based — Run After Scripts)
53	
54	Each scanner writes a free-form analysis document (not JSON):
55	
56	| #   | Scanner                                     | Focus                                                                     | Pre-Pass? | Output File                             |
57	| --- | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------- | --------------------------------------- |
58	| L1  | `quality-scan-workflow-integrity.md`        | Structural completeness, naming, type-appropriate requirements            | Yes       | `workflow-integrity-analysis.md`        |
59	| L2  | `quality-scan-prompt-craft.md`              | Token efficiency, outcome-driven balance, progressive disclosure, pruning | Yes       | `prompt-craft-analysis.md`              |
60	| L3  | `quality-scan-execution-efficiency.md`      | Parallelization, subagent delegation, context optimization                | Yes       | `execution-efficiency-analysis.md`      |
61	| L4  | `quality-scan-skill-cohesion.md`            | Stage flow, purpose alignment, complexity appropriateness                 | No        | `skill-cohesion-analysis.md`            |
62	| L5  | `quality-scan-enhancement-opportunities.md` | Edge cases, UX gaps, user journeys, headless potential                    | No        | `enhancement-opportunities-analysis.md` |
63	| L6  | `quality-scan-script-opportunities.md`      | Deterministic operations that should be scripts                           | No        | `script-opportunities-analysis.md`      |
64	
65	## Execution
66	
67	First create output directory: `{bmad_builder_reports}/{skill-name}/quality-analysis/{date-time-stamp}/`
68	
69	### Step 1: Run All Scripts (Parallel)
70	
71	Run all lint scripts and pre-pass scripts in parallel:
72	
73	```bash
74	python3 scripts/scan-path-standards.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/path-standards-temp.json
75	python3 scripts/scan-scripts.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/scripts-temp.json
76	uv run scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/workflow-integrity-prepass.json
77	python3 scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/prompt-metrics-prepass.json
78	uv run scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py {skill-path} -o {report-dir}/execution-deps-prepass.json
79	```
80	
81	### Step 2: Spawn LLM Scanners (Parallel)
82	
83	After scripts complete, spawn all applicable LLM scanners as parallel subagents.
84	
85	**For scanners WITH pre-pass (L1, L2, L3):** provide the pre-pass JSON file path so the scanner reads compact metrics first, then reads raw files only as needed for judgment calls.
86	
87	**For scanners WITHOUT pre-pass (L4, L5, L6):** provide just the skill path and output directory.
88	
89	Each subagent receives:
90	
91	- Scanner file to load
92	- Skill path: `{skill-path}`
93	- Output directory: `{report-dir}`
94	- Pre-pass file path (if applicable)
95	
96	The subagent loads the scanner file, analyzes the skill, writes its analysis to the output directory, and returns the filename.
97	
98	### Step 3: Synthesize Report
99	
100	After all scanners complete, spawn a subagent with `./report-quality-scan-creator.md`.
101	
102	Provide:
103	
104	- `{skill-path}` — The skill being analyzed
105	- `{quality-report-dir}` — Directory containing all scanner output
106	
107	The report creator reads everything, synthesizes themes, and writes:
108	
109	1. `quality-report.md` — Narrative markdown report
110	2. `report-data.json` — Structured data for HTML
111	
112	### Step 4: Generate HTML Report
113	
114	After the report creator finishes, generate the interactive HTML:
115	
116	```bash
117	python3 scripts/generate-html-report.py {report-dir} --open
118	```
119	
120	This reads `report-data.json` and produces `quality-report.html` — a self-contained interactive report with opportunity themes, "Fix This Theme" prompt generation, and expandable detailed analysis.
121	
122	## Present to User
123	
124	**IF `{headless_mode}=true`:**
125	
126	Read `report-data.json` and output:
127	
128	```json
129	{
130	  "headless_mode": true,
131	  "scan_completed": true,
132	  "report_file": "{path}/quality-report.md",
133	  "html_report": "{path}/quality-report.html",
134	  "data_file": "{path}/report-data.json",
135	  "warnings": [],
136	  "grade": "Excellent|Good|Fair|Poor",
137	  "opportunities": 0,
138	  "broken": 0
139	}
140	```
141	
142	**IF interactive:**
143	
144	Read `report-data.json` and present:
145	
146	1. Grade and narrative — the 2-3 sentence synthesis
147	2. Broken items (if any) — critical/high issues prominently
148	3. Top opportunities — theme names with finding counts and impact
149	4. Reports — "Full report: quality-report.md" and "Interactive HTML opened in browser"
150	5. Offer: apply fixes directly, use HTML to select specific items, or discuss findings
151	
```

> AGENT

**Grade: Good** — 6 themed opportunities, 0 broken items, 12 strengths.

skf-brief-skill is one of the most determinism-disciplined and structurally-sound workflows in the repo: structural pre-passes return zero issues, headless and interactive paths are co-designed, and seven shared scripts already absorb heavy deterministic work. Remaining opportunities cluster around step-01's accumulated breadth, residual deterministic seams the LLM still walks in prose, and a first-timer experience gap.

**Top opportunities (ranked by impact)**

1. **Step-01 has outgrown its name** — 334 lines, 8 sections, 12 sub-branches; conditional sub-flows inlined. Extract headless source-authority detection, portfolio-similarity, and draft-checkpoint into lazy refs.
2. **Residual deterministic seams** (8 findings) — language detection, scope-type heuristics, brief-context JSON assembly walked in prose. ~1,200–2,300 tokens/invocation savings if scripted; eliminates interactive/headless drift on scope-type.
3. **Repeated HALT envelope pattern** (2 findings) — same envelope-emission contract restated at 5 sites with wording drift. Promote step-05 §4b to canonical, link from the rest.
4. **First-timer archetype under-served** (5 findings) — open intent prompt with no examples, [F][M][P][C][R] abbreviations, no description-purpose microcopy, no "did you mean to brief a skill?" rail. Content edits only.
5. **Investment / failure timeline asymmetry** (3 findings) — gh api access probe lives at step-02 §1 but cheap signals at step-01 §3.3 could surface it; placeholder versions silently default; no description sentence-count check.
6. **Redundant network probes & lazy-load gap** (2 findings) — step-03 §2b re-HEADs URLs already probed; step-04 §1 implicitly invites re-loading the 17 KB schema.

**Reports**
- Markdown: `skills/reports/skf-brief-skill/quality-analysis/20260502-222251/quality-report.md`
- Interactive HTML: `skills/reports/skf-brief-skill/quality-analysis/20260502-222251/quality-report.html` (opened)
- Data: `report-data.json`

Want me to apply any of these (e.g., Rank 5 envelope dedupe and Rank 7 On Activation reorder are both low-effort wins), or do you prefer to drive selection from the HTML report's "Fix This Theme" buttons?

1	---
2	name: skf-brief-skill
3	description: Design a skill scope through guided discovery. Use when the user requests to "create a skill brief" or "brief a skill".
4	---
5	
6	# Brief Skill
7	
8	## Overview
9	
10	Helps the user define what to skill — target repo, scope, language, inclusion/exclusion patterns — and produces a `skill-brief.yaml` that drives create-skill. This is the first step in the skill creation pipeline; the brief is the input contract for create-skill, which performs the actual compilation.
11	
12	A good skill brief sets a tight, cohesive boundary: one capability with 3-8 primary functions, an unambiguous public API surface, and a description short enough to fit in a registry row. Briefs that try to cover several unrelated concerns (e.g. authentication *and* data visualization) compile into skills that no agent can route to confidently — a brief covering too much is a worse failure mode than a brief covering too little, and this workflow steers toward the smaller, sharper version when scope is unclear.
13	
14	Brief-skill is split from create-skill so the scoping conversation runs *once*, on cheap signals (manifests, top-level exports, intent), without paying for AST extraction. Compilation is expensive; scoping decisions are cheap to revise. Keeping them in separate workflows lets a user iterate on the brief, share it for review, and re-run create-skill against the same brief whenever the upstream version moves.
15	
16	## Role
17	
18	You are a skill scoping architect collaborating with a developer who wants to create an agent skill. You bring expertise in source code analysis, API surface identification, and skill boundary design, while the user brings their domain knowledge and specific use case. Work together as equals.
19	
20	## Workflow Rules
21	
22	These rules apply to every step in this workflow:
23	
24	- Read each step file completely before taking any action
25	- Follow the mandatory sequence in each step exactly — do not skip, reorder, or optimize
26	- Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps
27	- **Lazy-load references and assets:** `references/*.md` and `assets/*.md` files are loaded inside the section that needs them, not at step entry. If a section is skipped (e.g. `version-resolution.md` when `{extractPublicApiScript}` already returned a version, `scope-templates.md` for the `docs-only` branch that bypasses §2c), do not load that file. Each unnecessary load costs context (~5-10 KB per reference) and biases the LLM toward consulting material the current path does not need.
28	- Always communicate in `{communication_language}` (the language for user-facing prose). Written artifact text — the `description`, `notes`, and other free-form fields persisted into `skill-brief.yaml` — is in `{document_output_language}`; per-step rules call this out where it applies (see step-05). The two values may be the same.
29	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
30	
31	## Stages
32	
33	| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
34	|---|------|------|--------------|
35	| 1 | Gather Intent | steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md | No (interactive) |
36	| 2 | Analyze Target | steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md | Yes |
37	| 3 | Scope Definition | steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md | No (interactive) |
38	| 4 | Confirm Brief | steps-c/step-04-confirm-brief.md | No (confirm) |
39	| 5 | Write Brief | steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md | Yes |
40	| 6 | Workflow Health Check (terminal) | steps-c/step-06-health-check.md | Yes |
41	
42	## Invocation Contract
43	
44	| Aspect | Detail |
45	|--------|--------|
46	| **Inputs** | `target_repo` [required], `skill_name` [required], `scope_hint` [optional], `language_hint` [optional], `target_version` [optional], `source_authority` [optional: official/community/internal, default community], `source_type` [optional: source/docs-only, default source], `doc_urls` [optional: list of `url[,label]` for source_type=docs-only or supplemental], `scope_type` [optional: full-library/specific-modules/public-api/component-library/reference-app/docs-only], `include` [optional: comma-separated globs], `exclude` [optional: comma-separated globs], `scripts_intent` [optional: detect/none/free-text, default detect], `assets_intent` [optional: detect/none/free-text, default detect], `intent` [optional: free-text used to derive description], `force` [optional: overwrite existing brief without prompting] |
47	| **Gates** | step-01: Input Gate [use args] | step-03: Confirm Gate [C] | step-04: Confirm Gate [C] |
48	| **Outputs** | `skill-brief.yaml` at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml`; final `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` line on stdout when `{headless_mode}` is true |
49	| **Headless** | All gates auto-resolve with heuristic-driven or default action when `{headless_mode}` is true; pre-supplied inputs consumed at the gates that would otherwise prompt; absent `source_authority` and `scope_type` are resolved by signal-driven detection (see `references/headless-args.md`); existing briefs are preserved unless `--force` was supplied (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` otherwise) |
50	| **Exit codes** | See "Exit Codes" below |
51	
52	## Exit Codes
53	
54	Every HARD HALT in this workflow exits with a stable code so headless automators can branch on the failure class without grepping message text:
55	
56	| Code | Meaning              | Raised by                                                                                  |
57	| ---- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
58	| 0    | success              | step-06 (terminal)                                                                         |
59	| 2    | input-missing / input-invalid | step-01 GATE — required headless arg absent (`target_repo`, `skill_name`, or `doc_urls` when `source_type=docs-only`) → `input-missing`; enum violation, malformed semver, non-kebab `skill_name`, or step-05 brief-context schema validation failure → `input-invalid` |
60	| 3    | resolution-failure   | step-01 §1 (`forge-tier.yaml` missing); step-02 §1 (target inaccessible / `gh auth` fails) |
61	| 4    | write-failure        | step-01 §1 pre-flight write probe (data folder unwritable: read-only mount, disk full, permissions denied); step-05 §4 (write to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` failed) |
62	| 5    | overwrite-cancelled  | step-05 §2 (existing brief, `force` not supplied)                                          |
63	| 6    | user-cancelled       | any interactive menu in step-01/03/04 (user selected `[X]` Cancel and exit)                |
64	
65	## Result Contract (Headless)
66	
67	When `{headless_mode}` is true, step-05 emits a single-line JSON envelope on **stdout** before chaining to step-06, and every HARD HALT emits the same envelope shape on **stderr** with `status: "error"`:
68	
69	```
70	SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"success|error","brief_path":"…|null","skill_name":"…","version":"…|null","language":"…|null","scope_type":"…|null","exit_code":0,"halt_reason":null}
71	```
72	
73	`status` is `"success"` on the terminal happy path, `"error"` on any HALT. `halt_reason` is one of: `null` (success), `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`, `"user-cancelled"`. `exit_code` matches the table above.
74	
75	## On Activation
76	
77	1. Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/config.yaml` and resolve:
78	   - `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `forge_data_folder`, `sidecar_path`
79	
80	2. **Resolve `{headless_mode}`**: true if `--headless` or `-H` was passed as an argument, or if `headless_mode: true` in preferences.yaml. Default: false.
81	
82	3. Load, read the full file, and then execute `./steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md` to begin the workflow.
83	
```

1	---
2	briefSchemaFile: 'assets/skill-brief-schema.md'
3	versionResolutionFile: 'references/version-resolution.md'
4	qmdRegistrationFile: 'references/qmd-collection-registration.md'
5	nextStepFile: './step-06-health-check.md'
6	writeSkillBriefScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-write-skill-brief.py'
7	emitBriefEnvelopeScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py'
8	forgeTierRwScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-forge-tier-rw.py'
9	forgeTierFile: '{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml'
10	---
11	
12	# Step 5: Write Brief
13	
14	## STEP GOAL:
15	
16	To generate the complete skill-brief.yaml from the approved brief data and write it to the forge data folder, completing the brief-skill workflow.
17	
18	## Rules
19	
20	- Focus only on writing the file — all decisions have been made
21	- Do not change any field values without user request — the brief was already approved
22	- Create the output directory if it doesn't exist
23	- Chains to the local health-check step via `{nextStepFile}` after completion — the user-facing success summary is NOT the terminal step
24	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`; written artifact (`description`, `notes`) in `{document_output_language}`
25	- **Determinism delegation:** YAML rendering, version-precedence, atomic write, the headless result envelope, and the QMD-collection registry mutation are all delegated to shared SKF scripts. The LLM's job in this step is to assemble inputs, branch on script results, and surface user-facing prose — not to render YAML, JSON envelopes, or YAML-mutation diffs in the model.
26	
27	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
28	
29	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
30	
31	### 1. Reference the Schema (LLM context only)
32	
33	`{briefSchemaFile}` and `{versionResolutionFile}` document the brief contract for human readers. The deterministic enforcement of that contract lives in `{writeSkillBriefScript}` and its JSON Schema artifact at `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skill-brief.v1.json`. Load `{briefSchemaFile}` only if you need to explain a specific field to the user during inline adjustments — otherwise skip the read; the script is the source of truth.
34	
35	### 2. Resolve Output Path
36	
37	Resolve the target write path:
38	- Primary: `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml`
39	- Fallback (when `{forge_data_folder}` is not set or doesn't exist): `{output_folder}/forge-data/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` and inform user "**Note:** forge_data_folder not configured. Writing to {output_folder}/forge-data/{skill-name}/ instead."
40	
41	The script's atomic-write helper creates parent directories as needed (`mkdir -p`) — no separate mkdir call required.
42	
43	### 2b. Existing Brief — Overwrite Policy
44	
45	Before writing, check whether the resolved target path already exists.
46	
47	**Interactive (`{headless_mode}` is false):**
48	
49	If the file exists, present:
50	
51	"**An existing brief was found at `{path}`.**
52	Overwrite it with the brief you just approved? [Y/N]"
53	
54	- **[Y]** Overwrite — proceed to §3.
55	- **[N]** Cancel — emit a single-line stderr log `brief-skill: overwrite-cancelled at {path}` and HALT with exit code 5 (do not chain to step-06; the run produced no new artifact).
56	
57	**Headless (`{headless_mode}` is true):**
58	
59	If the file exists:
60	
61	- If `force` was supplied as a headless argument: log `"headless: force-overwriting existing brief at {path}"` and proceed to §3.
62	- Otherwise: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript}` to emit the error-variant envelope on stderr (see §4b) with `halt_reason: "overwrite-cancelled"`, then HALT with exit code 5.
63	
64	If the file does not exist, proceed normally.
65	
66	### 3. Write the Brief
67	
68	Assemble the brief context as a JSON object containing the approved field values from steps 01-04:
69	
70	```json
71	{
72	  "name":             "{approved skill name}",
73	  "target_version":   "{target_version from step 01, or null}",
74	  "detected_version": "{auto-detected version from step 02, or null}",
75	  "source_type":      "{source or docs-only}",
76	  "source_repo":      "{approved source repo or doc site URL}",
77	  "language":         "{approved language}",
78	  "description":      "{approved description}",
79	  "forge_tier":       "{Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep}",
80	  "created":          "{current ISO date YYYY-MM-DD}",
81	  "created_by":       "{user_name}",
82	  "scope": {
83	    "type":    "{approved scope type}",
84	    "include": ["{approved include patterns}"],
85	    "exclude": ["{approved exclude patterns}"],
86	    "notes":   "{approved scope notes or empty string}"
87	  },
88	  "doc_urls":         [{"url": "...", "label": "..."}],   // omit when not applicable
89	  "scripts_intent":   "{detect|none|free-text}",          // omit if "detect"
90	  "assets_intent":    "{detect|none|free-text}",          // omit if "detect"
91	  "source_authority": "{official|community|internal}"     // optional; defaults to "community" and is forced to "community" when source_type=docs-only
92	}
93	```
94	
95	Pipe it into the writer script:
96	
97	```bash
98	echo '<context-json>' | uv run {writeSkillBriefScript} write --target {resolved-target-path}
99	```
100	
101	The script:
102	- Validates the context against `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skill-brief.v1.json`
103	- Applies the version-precedence rule from `{versionResolutionFile}`
104	- Enforces the `target_version == version` invariant (refuses to write a brief that violates it)
105	- Renders YAML in canonical key order (byte-stable across runs)
106	- Atomically writes the file via temp + fsync + rename (no half-written file ever visible)
107	- Emits a JSON success envelope on stdout: `{"status":"ok","brief_path":"…","version":"…","bytes":…,"warnings":[…]}`
108	
109	**On script failure (non-zero exit):**
110	- Exit 1 (validation/invariant): The error JSON on stderr names the offending field. This indicates a context-assembly bug, not a user error — surface the message to the user, log it, then HALT.
111	  - Interactive: **HALT** — display the error JSON's `message` field.
112	  - Headless: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr` with envelope-context `halt_reason: "input-invalid"` (the script derives `exit_code: 2` automatically — do NOT pass `exit_code` in the JSON payload), then `exit 2`.
113	- Exit 2 (I/O failure): The atomic write failed (target unwritable, disk full, etc.).
114	  - Interactive: **HALT** — "**Error:** Failed to write skill-brief.yaml. Check that the directory is writable and try again."
115	  - Headless: invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr` with envelope-context `halt_reason: "write-failed"` (script derives `exit_code: 4` automatically), then `exit 4`.
116	
117	**On success:** capture `brief_path` and `version` from the response envelope — both are needed for §4b and §6.
118	
119	**Draft cleanup.** After a successful write, remove `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/.brief-draft.json` if it exists (`rm -f` — silent on absent). The draft was a step-01 §7 checkpoint covering the in-flight workflow window; once the brief is written it is no longer meaningful. In headless mode this rm is a no-op (drafts are only written interactively).
120	
121	### 4b. Headless Result Envelope
122	
123	If `{headless_mode}` is true, emit the success envelope on **stdout** immediately after the write (before §5 / §6 / §7):
124	
125	```bash
126	echo '{"status":"success","brief_path":"<from §3 response>","skill_name":"<name>","version":"<from §3 response>","language":"<language>","scope_type":"<scope.type>","halt_reason":null}' | \
127	  uv run {emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit
128	```
129	
130	The script derives `exit_code` deterministically from `halt_reason` (the canonical mapping is null→0, input-missing/input-invalid→2, forge-tier-missing/target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed→3, write-failed→4, overwrite-cancelled→5, user-cancelled→6 [interactive-only — headless never raises this]), validates against `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-brief-result-envelope.v1.json`, and prints the prefixed `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {…}` line.
131	
132	The envelope shape on HARD HALT (any phase) is the same call with `--target stderr`, `status: "error"`, and the matching `halt_reason` (one of `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`) — see the §3, §2b, §5 (no-halt) branches above and the §1/§2 HALTs in step-01/step-02 for invocation sites. The script enforces the success/error halt_reason invariant (success requires null halt_reason; error requires non-null). The `user-cancelled` halt_reason is also accepted by the script for completeness (interactive `[X]` Cancel sites in step-01/03/04 use it) but never appears on the headless code path.
133	
134	When `{headless_mode}` is false, skip this section silently — no envelope is emitted.
135	
136	### 5. QMD Collection Registration (Deep Tier Only)
137	
138	**IF forge tier is Deep AND QMD tool is available:** load `{qmdRegistrationFile}` and follow the procedure there to index the brief into a QMD collection and update the forge-tier registry.
139	
140	**IF forge tier is NOT Deep OR QMD is not available:** skip this section silently — do not load `{qmdRegistrationFile}`. No messaging.
141	
142	### 6. Display Success Summary
143	
144	"**Skill brief written successfully.**
145	
146	---
147	
148	**File:** `{brief_path from §3 response}`
149	**Skill:** {name}
150	**Language:** {language}
151	**Scope:** {scope type}
152	**Forge Tier:** {forge tier}
153	
154	---
155	
156	## Next Steps
157	
158	Your skill brief is ready. To compile the actual skill from this brief, run:
159	
160	**create-skill** — Reads your skill-brief.yaml and compiles a complete SKILL.md with AST-backed analysis.
161	
162	After compilation, you can:
163	- **test-skill** — Validate the compiled skill
164	- **export-skill** — Package the skill for distribution
165	
166	---
167	
168	**Brief-skill workflow complete.**"
169	
170	### 7. Chain to Health Check
171	
172	ONLY WHEN the brief file has been written and the success summary displayed will you then load, read the full file, and execute `{nextStepFile}`. The health-check step is the true terminal step — do not stop here even though the summary reads as final.
173	
174	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
175	
176	This step chains to the local health-check step (`{nextStepFile}`), which in turn delegates to `shared/health-check.md`. After the health check completes, the brief-skill workflow is fully done.
177

3	forgeTierFile: '{sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml'
4	descriptionVoiceExamplesFile: 'assets/description-voice-examples.md'
5	headlessArgsFile: 'references/headless-args.md'
6	validateBriefInputsScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-validate-brief-inputs.py'
7	emitBriefEnvelopeScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py'

9	
10	# Step 1: Gather Intent
11	
12	## STEP GOAL:
13	
14	To initialize the brief-skill workflow by discovering the forge tier configuration, then gathering the user's target repository, intent, and any upfront scope hints for skill creation.
15	
16	## Rules
17	
18	- Focus only on gathering intent — do not analyze the repo yet (Step 02)
19	- Do not examine source code or list exports in this step
20	- Open-ended discovery facilitation — collect target repo, user intent, scope hints, skill name
21	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
22	
23	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
24	
25	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
26	
27	### 1. Discover Forge Tier
28	
29	**Pre-flight write probe.** Before any conversational state accumulates, verify `{forge_data_folder}` is writable. A read-only mount, full disk, or permissions-denied path otherwise only surfaces at step-05's atomic write — by then the user has invested 5–15 minutes. Run a single-byte write-and-remove probe:
30	
31	```bash
32	mkdir -p "{forge_data_folder}" && \
33	  printf 'probe' > "{forge_data_folder}/.skf-write-probe" && \
34	  rm "{forge_data_folder}/.skf-write-probe"
35	```
36	
37	`mkdir -p` succeeds on a pre-existing read-only mount, but the `printf > file` redirect actually attempts a write — that catches read-only, disk-full, and permissions-denied uniformly. **On any non-zero exit:** HALT (exit code 4, `halt_reason: "write-failed"`) — `"**Error:** {forge_data_folder} is not writable: {captured stderr}. Verify the path exists, the mount is writable, and there is free disk space, then re-run."` In headless mode, invoke `{emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr` with envelope-context `{"status":"error","skill_name":"<name-or-pre-resolution-placeholder>","halt_reason":"write-failed"}` before exiting (matches the §8 GATE / step-05 emit pattern — never hand-assemble the envelope JSON). On success, continue silently to the forge-tier load below.
38	
39	Attempt to load `{forgeTierFile}`:
40	
41	**If found:**
42	- Read the tier level (quick, forge, forge+, or deep)
43	- Note available tools for scoping guidance later
44	
45	**Apply tier override:** Read `{sidecar_path}/preferences.yaml`. If `tier_override` is set and is a valid tier value (Quick, Forge, Forge+, or Deep), use it instead of the detected tier.
46	
47	**If found but the YAML cannot be parsed (corrupted or truncated):**
48	- Display: "**Cannot read forge-tier.yaml** at `{forgeTierFile}` — the file exists but failed to parse: `{parser error message}`. The setup workflow can rewrite it cleanly. Until then, the brief workflow falls back to **Quick** tier (no extra tools assumed)."
49	- Continue with `tier = "Quick"` and `tools = {}` — do not HALT. Record `tier_source: "fallback-corrupted-config"` for later diagnostics.
50	
51	**If not found:**
52	- "**Cannot proceed.** forge-tier.yaml not found at `{forgeTierFile}`. Run the **setup** workflow first to configure your forge tier (Quick/Forge/Forge+/Deep)."
53	- HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "forge-tier-missing"`) — do not proceed.
54	
55	### 2. Welcome and Explain
56	
57	"**Welcome to Brief Skill — the skill scoping workflow.**
58	
59	I'll help you define exactly what to skill and produce a `skill-brief.yaml` that drives the create-skill compilation workflow.
60	
61	We'll work through this together:
62	1. **Now:** Understand what you want to skill and why
63	2. **Next:** Analyze the target repo structure
64	3. **Then:** Define scope boundaries
65	4. **Finally:** Confirm and write the brief
66	
67	{If tier override was applied:}
68	**Your forge tier:** {override tier} (overridden from {original tier}) — {tier_gloss}
69	{Else:}
70	**Your forge tier:** {detected tier} — {tier_gloss}
71	
72	(Substitute `{tier_gloss}` with the matching one-liner so the user knows what the tier label means: `Quick` → "text-only extraction"; `Forge` → "AST-grep on, semantic discovery off"; `Forge+` → "AST-grep + ccc semantic discovery"; `Deep` → "full pipeline — AST + ccc + qmd portfolio search + LLM re-ranking". The tier sets the ceiling for what the downstream create-skill workflow can do; you can re-run setup later to change it.)
73	
74	Let's get started."
75	
76	### 3. Gather Target Repository
77	
78	This section has three sub-flows. Execute exactly one branch — 3.2 *or* 3.3 — based on the user's response in 3.1, then end with the shared confirmation. Do not mix branches.
79	
80	#### 3.1 Collect target
81	
82	"**What repository or documentation do you want to create a skill for?**
83	
84	Provide one of:
85	- A **GitHub URL** (e.g., `https://github.com/org/repo`)
86	- A **local path** (e.g., `/path/to/project`)
87	- **Documentation URLs** for a docs-only skill (e.g., `https://docs.stripe.com/api`) — use this when no source code is available (SaaS, closed-source)
88	
89	**Target:**"
90	
91	Wait for user response. Branch on the response:
92	
93	- Documentation URLs only (no source location) → §3.2
94	- GitHub URL or local filesystem path → §3.3
95	
96	#### 3.2 Branch — Documentation URLs (docs-only)
97	
98	- Set `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief data
99	- Collect one or more doc URLs with optional labels
100	- HEAD-check the collected URLs in parallel — do not loop sequentially. Issue all N `curl -sI {url}` (or equivalent) calls in a **single message with N parallel Bash calls**, then process the responses together. Each call must use a 5-second timeout (`curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`) to bound worst-case wall-time on hung hosts. Per response:
101	  - On 2xx/3xx: silently accept.
102	  - On 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout: warn `"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}. Confirm the URL is correct, or proceed anyway."` Interactive: re-prompt for a corrected URL or `[K] Keep anyway`. Headless: keep the URL and log the warning — the brief still records it but the failure is now visible at brief-creation time instead of materializing hours later in skf-create-skill.
103	- Set `source_authority: "community"` (forced for docs-only — T3 external documentation; the §3.3 source-authority prompt is skipped)
104	- Note: `source_repo` becomes optional (can be set to the main doc site URL for reference)
105	
106	Skip §3.3 and continue at "Confirm the target" below.
107	
108	#### 3.3 Branch — Source (GitHub URL or local path)
109	
110	- Set `source_type: "source"` (default)
111	- **Pre-validate the target before continuing.** Issue these probes in a single message with parallel Bash calls:
112	  - **GitHub URL:** `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`. On a 4xx (typically 404 for a typo'd repo or org), warn `"GitHub returned {status} for {url} — confirm the URL is correct."` and re-prompt. On 2xx, accept. (The full `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` check still runs in step-02 §1 to catch private-repo access issues — this HEAD probe is just for typo catch.)
113	  - **GitHub URL, in parallel with the above:** `gh auth status` — if it reports unauthenticated or the binary is missing, warn `"GitHub CLI not authenticated; step-02 will HALT when it tries to fetch the tree. Run 'gh auth login' before continuing, or supply a local clone path instead."` (Do not HALT here — let the user choose to fix or proceed; the canonical HALT still happens in step-02 §1's failure-class triage.)
114	  - **Local path:** verify the directory exists (`test -d {path}`). If not, warn `"Local path {path} does not exist."` and re-prompt.
115	- Optionally ask: "Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)"
116	- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`
117	
118	**Source authority (this branch only — docs-only forces `community` in §3.2):**
119	
120	**Interactive only** — skip this prompt entirely when `{headless_mode}` is true; the GATE in §8 resolves source_authority headlessly via the detection branch documented there.
121	
122	"**Are you the maintainer of this library, or creating a community skill?**"
123	- If maintainer: set `source_authority: "official"`
124	- If community user: set `source_authority: "community"` (default)
125	- If internal/proprietary: set `source_authority: "internal"`
126	
127	Default to `"community"` if user does not specify or skips.
128	
129	---
130	
131	Confirm the target.
132	
133	### 3b. Gather Target Version
134	
135	This step only collects `target_version` and validates its shape with the regex below — auto-detection runs in step-02 and precedence/invariant resolution lands in step-05's writer script. The canonical precedence rules live in `references/version-resolution.md`; load it from step-02 / step-05 only when the relevant section needs it.
136	
137	**Headless:** if `target_version` was supplied as an argument, store it and skip the interactive prompt below. If `doc_urls` were also supplied, treat the version-vs-doc-URL confirmation prompt as auto-confirmed (Y).
138	
139	"**Are you targeting a specific version of this library?**
140	(Leave blank to auto-detect from source)"
141	
142	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
143	"Since this is a docs-only skill with no source code, specifying the version is recommended — otherwise it defaults to 1.0.0."
144	
145	Wait for user response.
146	
147	**If user provides a version:** Validate the shape against `^v?\d+\.\d+\.\d+([.\-+][0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.\-+]*)?$` (full X.Y.Z form, with optional `v` prefix and pre-release / build suffix; CalVer like `2024.04.01` accepted; partial forms like `1`, `1.2`, `v2`, `latest` rejected). On a match, store as `target_version` and set `version` to this value. On a non-match, warn `"'{value}' doesn't look like semver — write the explicit triple (e.g. 1.0.0). Fix it now or skip auto-detection?"` and re-prompt for a corrected value or blank to fall through to step-02 auto-detection.
148	**If blank:** Proceed without `target_version` — version will be auto-detected in step 02.
149	
150	{If target_version was set AND doc_urls are being collected (either docs-only primary or supplemental):}
151	
152	"**You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these documentation URLs correspond to that version?** [Y/N]"
153	
154	- **If Y:** Proceed.
155	- **If N:** "Provide the correct documentation URLs for version {target_version}." Re-collect doc_urls.
156	
157	### 4. Gather User Intent
158	
159	"**What's your intent for this skill?**
160	
161	Help me understand:
162	- **What** specifically do you want to skill from this repo?
163	- **Why** — what's the use case? How will an AI agent use this skill?
164	- **Any initial thoughts** on scope? (Full library? Specific modules? Public API only?)
165	
166	Take your time — the more context you share, the better the brief."
167	
168	Wait for user response. Ask follow-up questions if intent is unclear.
169	
170	### 5. Capture Scope Hints
171	
172	If the user mentioned scope preferences in their intent response, acknowledge them:
173	
174	"**I noted these scope hints from your response:**
175	- {list any scope hints mentioned}
176	
177	We'll refine these after analyzing the repo structure in the next step."
178	
179	If no scope hints were mentioned, that's fine — skip this acknowledgment.
180	
181	### 6. Derive Skill Name
182	
183	Based on the target repo and intent, propose a skill name:
184	
185	"**Suggested skill name:** `{derived-name}` (kebab-case)
186	
187	This will be used for the output directory and file naming. Want to use this name or suggest something different?"
188	
189	Wait for confirmation or alternative.
190	
191	**Collision check (interactive and headless):** before locking the name, check whether `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/skill-brief.yaml` already exists. If it does:
192	
193	- Interactive: generate 1–3 non-colliding candidate alternates by scanning sibling directories under `{forge_data_folder}/`. Apply each rule that fires; skip rules whose precondition isn't met:
194	  1. `{name}-v{N}` where `N` is the smallest positive integer that doesn't collide (e.g. `{name}-v2`, `{name}-v3`) — always applies
195	  2. `{name}-{target_version}` if `target_version` is set and the suffix wouldn't collide (e.g. `marked-1.2.3`)
196	  3. `{name}-{source_authority}` if `source_authority` is not `community` (e.g. `marked-internal` for an internal fork)
197	
198	  Number the surviving alternates `[1] [2] [3]…` in the order produced (1 alternate for a community-authority brief with no `target_version`; 2–3 otherwise). Then present:
199	
200	  ```
201	  **Heads up — a brief for `{name}` already exists at `{path}`.**
202	
203	  Suggested alternates (none collide):
204	    [1] {alternate-1}
205	    {if a second alternate was produced:} [2] {alternate-2}
206	    {if a third alternate was produced:} [3] {alternate-3}
207	
208	  Pick a number to use that name, type a different name, or press Enter to keep `{name}` and let step-05 §2b handle the overwrite prompt.
209	  ```
210	
211	  On a numbered choice, replace `{name}` with the chosen alternate. On Enter, fall through to step-05's overwrite gate. On any other input, treat as a new candidate name and re-run the collision check against it.
212	
213	- Headless: log `"warn: skill name '{name}' collides with existing brief at {path}"` and proceed; the existing-brief overwrite policy in step-05 §2b is the canonical gate (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` unless `force` was supplied).
214	
215	**Portfolio-similarity check (Deep tier with QMD only, interactive only).** When the forge tier is `Deep` AND `tools.qmd` is true in `forge-tier.yaml`, the brief portfolio is already indexed in QMD collections (one `{skill-name}-brief` collection per existing brief, registered by step-05 §5 of every prior Deep-tier run). Run a similarity search against that portfolio so near-duplicates are caught before the user invests scope-definition time. The qmd CLI does not support glob-style collection selection, so enumerate first then query per collection in **a single message with N parallel Bash calls**:
216	
217	```bash
218	# 1. Enumerate brief collections (one per existing brief)
219	qmd collection list | awk '/-brief$/{print $1}'
220	
221	# 2. For each collection, query the proposed name + intent text:
222	qmd query "{name} {synthesized-or-intent-text}" -c {collection-name} -n 1 --min-score 0.6
223	```
224	
225	Aggregate the top hits across all `-brief` collections; keep the 3 highest-scoring across the union. If any results come back, surface them as a heads-up — *not* a HALT. Exact-name collision is already handled above; this check catches semantic near-duplicates that exact-match misses (e.g. `markdown-renderer` proposed when `marked` already exists, or `auth-gateway` when `auth-middleware` already exists). Present:
226	
227	```
228	**Heads up — these existing briefs look semantically close to `{name}`:**
229	  1. {existing-name} (similarity: {score})  — {existing-description}
230	  2. {existing-name} (similarity: {score})  — {existing-description}
231	
232	Continue with `{name}`, or pick a different name?
233	```
234	
235	On any QMD failure (binary missing, collection list empty, any per-collection query times out): log `"warn: portfolio-similarity check skipped — qmd query failed: {error}"` and continue silently — never HALT. Quick / Forge / Forge+ tiers do not run this check (qmd is Deep-tier-only per the canonical tier definition: `Deep = + ast-grep + gh + QMD` in `skf-forge-tier-rw.py`). Headless does not run this check either — it would either need to HALT on duplicates (over-aggressive for an automator) or silently log (no operator to act on it); leaving it interactive-only keeps the headless path side-effect-free against the QMD index.
236	
237	**Draft-resume check (interactive only).** After the name is confirmed, also check for an in-progress draft at `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/.brief-draft.json` (a step-01 §7 checkpoint from a previous run that did not reach step-05). If the file exists AND no `skill-brief.yaml` sits beside it (a finished brief uses the same dir), present:
238	
239	```
240	**An in-progress draft for `{name}` was found** (last updated: {mtime}).
241	  [Y] Resume from the saved draft (jump to §8 with prior answers restored)
242	  [N] Start fresh (delete the draft and re-gather)
243	```
244	
245	On `[Y]`: load the JSON, restore the captured fields (target_repo, source_type, source_authority, target_version, doc_urls, intent, scope_hint, description), and jump directly to §8 — **skip the rest of §6, §7, and §7b**. The restored `description` is authoritative and does not need re-synthesis (re-running §7b would overwrite the user's previously accepted description with a fresh candidate). The user can still revise any field at step-04. On `[N]`: delete `.brief-draft.json` and continue with §6 normally. In headless mode this check is skipped — drafts are an interactive-only convenience and the headless path runs to completion in a single invocation.
246	
247	### 7. Summarize Gathered Intent
248	
249	"**Here's what I've captured:**
250	
251	- **Target:** {repo URL or path}
252	- **Intent:** {user's intent summary}
253	- **Scope hints:** {any hints, or "None — we'll define scope after analysis"}
254	- **Skill name:** {confirmed name}
255	- **Source type:** {source or docs-only}
256	- **Source authority:** {official/community/internal}
257	{If target_version set:}
258	- **Target version:** {target_version} (user-specified)
259	{If doc_urls collected:}
260	- **Doc URLs:** {count} supplemental documentation URLs
261	- **Forge tier:** {tier}
262	
263	Ready to analyze the target repository?"
264	
265	**Draft checkpoint (interactive only).** After the user confirms the summary, persist the captured state to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/.brief-draft.json` so a session interruption between here and step-05 doesn't force a full re-gather. Write a single JSON object with the captured fields (target_repo, source_type, source_authority, target_version, doc_urls, intent, scope_hint, description-after-§7b, the inferred forge_tier and tier_source) atomically (write to `.brief-draft.json.tmp` then rename). The file is removed by step-05 §4 after the final brief writes successfully (a draft only exists while the workflow is in flight). In headless mode skip this checkpoint — the run completes in a single invocation, no resume is meaningful.
266	
267	### 7b. Synthesize Skill Description
268	
269	The schema's `description` field is 1-3 sentences and surfaces in skill registries — it must exist by the time step-04 presents the brief. Synthesize it explicitly here, while the user's intent is fresh, instead of letting it fall out implicitly later.
270	
271	Compose a candidate 1-3 sentence description from the gathered material. **Write like a human library maintainer would** — what does an agent get from this skill, and when should it route here? Two facts must come through (what the skill is, when to use it); everything else is voice. Resist filling in the same skeleton every time.
272	
273	Load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` for the five voice examples (range of acceptable leads, structures, and trigger phrasings) and the "do not template-stamp" guidance, then compose in that spirit. The asset documents what "in that spirit" means; the gathered material to draw on is the target repo, the user's intent, the version if set, and any scope hints.
274	
275	Present:
276	
277	"**Proposed skill description:**
278	
279	> {synthesized description}
280	
281	This is what shows up when agents discover the skill. Edit it, replace it, or accept as-is."
282	
283	Wait for user confirmation or alternative. Store the accepted text as the brief's `description` field. The same field is re-presented in step-04 §3 for a final review pass — refinements there flow back to this value.
284	
285	**Headless:** if the `intent` argument was supplied, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and run the same synthesis against it (in `{document_output_language}`), then store the result. If `intent` was not supplied, fall back in priority order:
286	
287	1. **GitHub repo description** — when `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, fetch `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} --jq .description` (5-second timeout). If a non-empty description comes back, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and synthesize using the GitHub description as the seed in place of `intent`. Write the synthesized description in `{document_output_language}` regardless of the seed's language (the seed may be in any language; the output's language is dictated by the workflow's document-output configuration). Log `"info: description seeded from GitHub repo description"`. (The full `gh api repos` response is fetched again in step-02 §1; this lightweight `--jq .description` call only retrieves the one field.)
288	2. **Generic stub** — when no GitHub description is available (local-path target, GitHub repo with empty description, or `gh api` fails): derive from `target_repo` + `skill_name` (`"Use the {skill_name} skill to work with code or content from {target_repo}."`) — the generic fallback does not need the asset — and log `"warn: description synthesized without intent or repo description — narrow registry text."`
289	
290	### 8. Present MENU OPTIONS
291	
292	Display: "**Select:** [C] Continue to Target Analysis · [X] Cancel and exit"
293	
294	#### Menu Handling Logic:
295	
296	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
297	- IF X: Treat as user-cancellation. Display `"Cancelled — no brief was written."` and HALT (exit code 6, `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"`). When `{headless_mode}` is true the GATE auto-proceeds and never reaches this branch — `[X]` is interactive-only. Cancellation here is non-destructive: no files have been written yet by step-01.
298	- IF Any other: Help user, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#8-present-menu-options)
299	
300	#### EXECUTION RULES:
301	
302	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
303	- **GATE [default: use args]** — If `{headless_mode}`, consume pre-supplied arguments and auto-proceed. The full argument set (required/optional, defaults, halt codes, enum values) is documented in `{headlessArgsFile}` — load it now if you need to look up a specific argument. Validation is delegated to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`; the table is the canonical operator-facing documentation, the script enforces it.
304	
305	  **Preset merge (before validation).** If the headless args include a `preset` field, load `{sidecar_path}/brief-presets/{preset}.yaml` and merge its contents as defaults — explicit args override preset values, key by key. The preset file is YAML; if it does not exist, log `"warn: preset '{name}' not found at {path} — proceeding without preset"` and continue (do not HALT). If it parses but contains unknown fields, log per-field warnings and pass through unchanged (the validator's KNOWN_FIELDS check will catch any that survive). Drop the `preset` key itself from the merged dict before passing to the validator (it is consumed at this level and is not a brief field).
306	
307	  **Delegate validation to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`** instead of reasoning through the table rules in prose:
308	
309	  ```bash
310	  echo '<headless-args-as-json>' | uv run {validateBriefInputsScript}
311	  ```
312	
313	  The script returns a JSON envelope: `{valid, errors[], warnings[], normalized, halt_reason}`. Apply the result deterministically:
314	
315	  - **`valid: false`** — emit the error-variant `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelope on stderr with `exit_code: 2` and the script's `halt_reason` (`"input-missing"` for absent required args / docs-only without doc_urls; `"input-invalid"` for enum violations, malformed semver, malformed kebab-case skill_name). Surface `errors[]` to the operator log so the failure is debuggable. HALT.
316	  - **`valid: true`** — consume the `normalized` object as the source of truth (it has defaults applied per the table). Surface `warnings[]` to the operator log but do not HALT. Auto-proceed.
317	
318	  The script's `KNOWN_FIELDS` set must stay in sync with the table in `{headlessArgsFile}`.
319	
320	  **Headless source-authority detection** — the validator intentionally leaves `source_authority` ABSENT from `normalized` when not supplied (so detection can run here). After consuming `normalized`, if `source_authority` is absent AND `source_type=source` AND `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, run signal-driven detection:
321	
322	  ```bash
323	  gh api user --jq .login
324	  ```
325	
326	  Compare the result to the `owner` segment of `target_repo` (URL pattern `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>`) — **lower-case both values before comparing** (GitHub owner matching is case-insensitive but the API preserves case in responses). If they match, set `source_authority: "official"` — the operator is the repo's GitHub owner. Otherwise set `source_authority: "community"`. On any error from `gh api user` (unauthenticated, network failure, missing binary), log `"warn: source-authority detection skipped — gh api user failed"` and fall back to `"community"`. For local-path targets the comparison cannot apply; set `"community"` directly. (When `source_authority` was already supplied in `normalized`, the supplied value wins — no detection runs.)
327	
328	
329	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
330	
331	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
332	
333	ONLY WHEN C is selected and target repository is confirmed will you load and read fully `./step-02-analyze-target.md` to execute target analysis.
334	
335	
```

7	
8	# Step 3: Scope Definition
9	
10	## STEP GOAL:
11	
12	To collaboratively define the skill's inclusion and exclusion boundaries using the analysis findings from step 02, scope templates, and the user's intent from step 01.
13	
14	## Rules
15	
16	- Focus only on defining scope boundaries — do not write the brief yet (Step 05)
17	- Do not make scope decisions unilaterally — user drives all scope choices
18	- Produce: scope type, include patterns, exclude patterns
19	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
20	- **Re-entry from step-04 [R] revise:** prior selections (`scope.type`, `scope.include`, `scope.exclude`, `scope.notes`, `scripts_intent`, `assets_intent`, supplemental `doc_urls`) are preserved as the current state. Re-present them at each section as the existing answer; the user only re-confirms or overrides. Do not reset to the §2c template menu unless the user explicitly asks to start scope over.
21	
22	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
23	
24	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
25	
26	### 1. Present Scope Context
27	
28	"**Let's define the scope for your skill.**
29	
30	Based on the analysis, here's what we're working with:
31	
32	- **Target:** {repo}
33	- **Language:** {detected language}
34	- **Modules found:** {count} — {list names}
35	- **Your intent:** {user intent from step 01}
36	{If scope hints from step 01:}
37	- **Your initial scope hints:** {hints}"
38	
39	### 2. Handle Docs-Only Mode (if applicable)
40	
41	**If `source_type: "docs-only"`:**
42	
43	"**Docs-only mode — scope is defined by documentation pages.**
44	
45	You've provided these documentation URLs:
46	{numbered list of doc_urls with labels}
47	
48	Which pages should be included in the skill? (Enter numbers, or 'all')
49	Any additional documentation URLs to add?"
50	
51	Wait for confirmation. Then skip to section 5 (Summarize Scope Decisions) with:
52	- `scope.type: "docs-only"`
53	- `scope.include`: confirmed doc URLs
54	- `scope.notes: "Generated from external documentation. All content is T3 confidence."`
55	
56	**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue to scope templates below.
57	
58	### 2b. Confirm Supplemental Documentation (if doc_urls collected)
59	
60	**If `source_type: "source"` AND supplemental `doc_urls` were collected in step 01:**
61	
62	"**Supplemental documentation URLs:**
63	{numbered list of collected doc_urls with labels}
64	
65	These will be included as T3 external references in the skill brief.
66	Add, remove, or confirm these URLs."
67	
68	Wait for confirmation. Record any changes to `doc_urls`.
69	
70	HEAD-check the URLs in parallel — issue all N `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}` calls in a **single message with N parallel Bash calls**, then process the responses together. On a 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout per URL, warn `"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}."` and offer the same correct/keep choice as step-01 §3. The check is best-effort — never HALT on a failed HEAD — but the failure must surface here so it is not discovered downstream during compilation.
71	
72	**If no supplemental doc_urls were collected:** Skip this subsection.
73	
74	**Scope guidance for first-time users:** A well-scoped skill covers one cohesive capability with 3-8 primary functions. If the scope includes unrelated concerns (e.g., authentication AND data visualization), suggest splitting into separate briefs. If the scope is too narrow (single utility function), suggest expanding to the surrounding capability surface.
75	
76	### 2c. Offer Scope Templates
77	
78	Load `{scopeTemplatesFile}` for the scope type options ([F], [M], [P], [C], [R]) and their descriptions.
79	
80	**Recommend a scope type — don't present the five options as equal weight.** SKILL.md states this workflow "steers toward the smaller, sharper version when scope is unclear" — surface that opinion at decision time. Use the analysis from step-02 and the user's intent from step-01 to pick the best-fit recommendation, then present the menu with that option marked as the suggested default.
81	
82	Heuristics for the recommendation (apply in order, pick the first that matches):
83	
84	- **Component registry detected** (file matching `registry.ts` / `components.ts` with 10+ entries, or `Component[]` type annotation) → **[C] Component Library**
85	- **User intent mentions "wiring", "integration example", "starter", "lifecycle", "build config"** OR the analysis identified the source as an example/demo app → **[R] Reference App**
86	- **Intent names specific module(s)** (e.g. "just the auth module", "only the streaming part") OR the analysis surfaced a large library (≥6 top-level modules with weakly-related concerns) → **[M] Specific Modules**
87	- **Library has a clear, narrow public API** (≤8 named exports from the manifest, intent points at "the API" / "the SDK") → **[P] Public API Only**
88	- **Otherwise** (small focused library, intent says "everything", or no strong signal either way) → **[F] Full Library**
89	
90	Present:
91	
92	"**Recommended scope type: [{letter}] {Name}** — {one-sentence rationale tying the recommendation to a specific signal from step-01/02, e.g. 'because the analysis found a 47-entry component registry under src/components/registry.ts'}.
93	
94	How broadly should this skill cover the library?
95	
96	{full menu from `{scopeTemplatesFile}` with the recommended letter marked, e.g. '[F] Full Library', '[M] Specific Modules', '[P] Public API Only ← recommended', '[C] Component Library', '[R] Reference App'}
97	
98	Press Enter to accept the recommendation, or pick a different letter."
99	
100	Wait for user selection. Empty input or just Enter accepts the recommendation; any of the five letters overrides.
101	
102	**Headless:** if `scope_type` was supplied, use it (consumed at the GATE in §6). If not supplied, run the same five heuristics above against the data already gathered (intent text + scope_hint from step-01, module count + exports + file paths from step-02) and use the first matching recommendation as the scope_type. Log `"headless: scope_type auto-selected as {value} from {heuristic-name}"` so the choice is debuggable.
103	
104	For the **component-registry** heuristic, the entry-count threshold (`10+ entries`) and `Component[]` type-annotation check require file *contents* — those are only available when step-02 §4.1 included `registry.ts` / `components.ts` as an entry point in the script payload (rare for the public-API mode), or when the target is a local path. In headless mode without that visibility, treat **the presence of `registry.ts` or `components.ts` anywhere in the file tree** as a sufficient match for the component-library recommendation — content inspection is interactive-only.
105	
106	The `docs-only` short-circuit still applies when `source_type=docs-only` (heuristic ranking is skipped — there is no source surface to scope). When no heuristic matches, fall back to `full-library` and log `"headless: scope_type defaulted to full-library — no signal matched"`.
107	
108	### 3. Define Boundaries Based on Selection
109	
110	Using the boundary definitions from `{scopeTemplatesFile}`, present the appropriate flow for the user's selected scope type ([F], [M], [P], [C], or [R]). Follow each type's prompts and wait for user input at each phase before proceeding.
111	
112	### 4. Handle Language Override
113	
114	{If language detection confidence was low from step 02:}
115	
116	"**Language confirmation needed.**
117	
118	The analysis detected **{language}** with low confidence. Is this correct, or should we set a different primary language?"
119	
120	Wait for confirmation or override.
121	
122	### 5. Summarize Scope Decisions
123	
124	"**Scope Summary:**
125	
126	**Type:** {Full Library / Specific Modules / Public API / Component Library / Reference App}
127	
128	**Include:**
129	{bulleted list of include patterns}
130	
131	**Exclude:**
132	{bulleted list of exclude patterns}
133	
134	**Language:** {confirmed language}
135	
136	{If any scope notes:}
137	**Notes:** {scope notes}
138	
139	Does this look right? You can adjust before we continue."
140	
141	Wait for confirmation. Make adjustments if requested.
142	
143	### 5b. Scripts & Assets Intent (Optional)
144	
145	**Only ask when `scope.type` is `full-library`, `specific-modules`, `component-library`, or `reference-app` (skip for `public-api` and `docs-only`). Reference apps routinely ship wiring scripts and build-config assets — prompt for them.**
146	
147	"Does this library include executable scripts (CLI tools, validation scripts, setup helpers) or static assets (config templates, JSON schemas, example configs) that should be packaged with the skill?"
148	
149	- **[D] Auto-detect** from source (default) — SKF will scan for `scripts/`, `bin/`, `assets/`, `templates/`, `schemas/` directories
150	- **[N] None expected** — skip script/asset detection
151	- Or describe what you expect (free text)
152	
153	Record the response as `scripts_intent` and `assets_intent` in the brief. Default to `detect` if user does not respond or skips.
154	
155	### 6. Present MENU OPTIONS
156	
157	Display: **Select an Option:** [A] Advanced Elicitation [P] Party Mode [C] Continue to Brief Confirmation [X] Cancel and exit
158	
159	#### Menu Handling Logic:
160	
161	- IF A: Invoke {advancedElicitationSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
162	- IF P: Invoke {partyModeSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
163	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
164	- IF X: Treat as user-cancellation. Display `"Cancelled — no brief was written."` and HALT (exit code 6, `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"`). Cancellation here is non-destructive — no files have been written yet. `[X]` is interactive-only; the headless GATE never reaches this branch.
165	- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond then [Redisplay Menu Options](#6-present-menu-options)
166	
167	#### EXECUTION RULES:
168	
169	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
170	- **GATE [default: C]** — If `{headless_mode}`: consume the headless inputs from step-01 in priority order:
171	  - If `scope_type` was supplied, use it (must match one of the six valid types) and skip the §2c template menu.
172	  - Otherwise auto-select: `source_type=docs-only` → `scope.type: "docs-only"`; `source_type=source` → run the §2c heuristic-driven recommendation (component-registry / reference-app keywords / specific-modules / public-api / full-library) against the step-01/02 signals and use the first match. Falls back to `full-library` only when no heuristic matches.
173	  - If `include`/`exclude` were supplied, use them verbatim (split on comma) instead of running the boundary prompts in §3.
174	  - If `scripts_intent`/`assets_intent` were supplied, record them and skip §5b; otherwise default to `detect`.
175	  - Log: `"headless: scope_type={value} include={n} exclude={n} scripts_intent={value} assets_intent={value}"`.
176	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
177	- After other menu items execution, return to this menu
178	- User can chat or ask questions — always respond and then redisplay menu
179	
180	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
181	
182	ONLY WHEN C is selected and scope boundaries are confirmed will you load and read fully `./step-04-confirm-brief.md` to present the complete brief for confirmation.
183	
184	
```

+When the HALT fires before `skill_name` has been resolved (step-01 §1 pre-flight write probe, step-01 §8 input-missing on a malformed args bundle), pass the partially-gathered value or the literal `"unknown"` — the script accepts any non-empty string at this position.
+
+The script derives `exit_code` deterministically from `halt_reason` (null→0, input-missing/input-invalid→2, forge-tier-missing/target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed→3, write-failed→4, overwrite-cancelled→5, user-cancelled→6 [interactive-only — headless never raises this]), validates against `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-brief-result-envelope.v1.json`, and prints the prefixed `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {…}` line.
+
+The script enforces the success/error halt_reason invariant (success requires null halt_reason; error requires non-null). The `user-cancelled` halt_reason is accepted for completeness (interactive `[X]` Cancel sites in step-01/03/04) but never appears on the headless code path.
 
-The envelope shape on HARD HALT (any phase) is the same call with `--target stderr`, `status: "error"`, and the matching `halt_reason` (one of `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`) — see the §3, §2b, §5 (no-halt) branches above and the §1/§2 HALTs in step-01/step-02 for invocation sites. The script enforces the success/error halt_reason invariant (success requires null halt_reason; error requires non-null). The `user-cancelled` halt_reason is also accepted by the script for completeness (interactive `[X]` Cancel sites in step-01/03/04 use it) but never appears on the headless code path.
+Invocation sites (each pointed at this block, not duplicated): step-01 §1 (write-failed pre-resolution), step-01 §8 (input-missing/input-invalid GATE), step-02 §1 (target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed/forge-tier-missing), step-05 §2b (overwrite-cancelled), step-05 §3 (input-invalid/write-failed from script).
 
 When `{headless_mode}` is false, skip this section silently — no envelope is emitted.
```

---

Reviewing `refactor/skf-brief-cleanup-quick-wins` vs. `main` — scope: `src/skf-brief-skill/`.

## Critical

### 1. §4b invocation-sites registry claims step-02 §1 is a converted pointer — it is not

**Confidence: 95**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md`, line 145

The new §4b closes with:

> Invocation sites (each pointed at this block, not duplicated): step-01 §1 (write-failed pre-resolution), step-01 §8 (input-missing/input-invalid GATE), **step-02 §1 (target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed/forge-tier-missing)**, step-05 §2b (overwrite-cancelled), step-05 §3 (input-invalid/write-failed from script).

But `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md` lines 47-49 have three HALT entries that contain no envelope emission instruction and no pointer to §4b:

```
- … HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"`) — "**Error:** …"
- … HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "target-inaccessible"`) — "**Error:** …"
- … HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"`) — "**Error:** …"
```

Additionally, step-02's frontmatter defines no `emitBriefEnvelopeScript` variable, so there is no mechanism to invoke the emit script even if a pointer were added. The result: in headless mode, a `target-inaccessible` or `gh-auth-failed` HALT in step-02 §1 produces no `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` line on stderr. A pipeline greping for that prefix would see nothing and time-out or misinterpret the run as still-in-flight.

The PR description accurately describes converting 4 sites (step-01 §1, step-01 §8, step-05 §2b, step-05 §3). Step-02 §1 was apparently added to the §4b site registry without the corresponding edits to step-02 itself. Either remove step-02 §1 from the registry list (if step-02 is intentionally deferred to a later PR), or add the pointer and the frontmatter variable to step-02 now.

---

### 2. step-01 §1 forge-tier-missing HALT has no envelope emission

**Confidence: 85**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 53

The write-probe HALT on line 37 correctly says "emit the error envelope per **step-05 §4b**". But the forge-tier-missing HALT six lines later is:

```
- HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "forge-tier-missing"`) — do not proceed.
```

No headless envelope emission instruction. Yet §4b's invocation sites registry attributes "forge-tier-missing" to step-02 §1, not step-01 §1. Since step-01 is where `forge-tier-missing` actually fires, headless runs hitting this branch also emit no `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON`.

This may be a pre-existing gap rather than a regression introduced in this PR — but by adding a comprehensive invocation-sites registry in §4b, the PR implicitly claims all HALT sites are covered. The forge-tier-missing site in step-01 §1 line 53 is neither in the registry nor has a pointer, so the registry is incomplete.

Fix: add `halt_reason: "forge-tier-missing"` to the step-01 §1 entry in the §4b registry comment (change from "write-failed pre-resolution" to "write-failed/forge-tier-missing pre-resolution"), and add a headless emission line to step-01 §1's forge-tier-missing HALT at line 53.

---

## Important

### 3. step-03 §2b HEAD-recheck guard states supplemental URLs from §3.3 "already passed" — they did not

**Confidence: 83**

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md`, line 70

The new text reads:

> HEAD-check only newly-added or modified URLs. URLs carried over unchanged from step-01 §3.3 already passed their HEAD-check there…

However, step-01 §3.3 (`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, lines 115-116) collects supplemental doc_urls with no HEAD probe:

```
- Optionally ask: "Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context?"
- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`
```

The HEAD-check block in §3.2 (docs-only branch, line 100) applies only when `source_type=docs-only`, and §3.3 has no equivalent. So supplemental doc_urls from §3.3 have never been probed. An LLM following step-03 §2b literally will skip probing them because the guard says they "already passed," which is false.

The practical consequence is low-severity: the check is best-effort and headless keeps bad URLs anyway. But the guard text is factually wrong and could cause missed warnings in interactive mode for every supplemental URL supplied via §3.3.

Fix: change the guard to reference only §3.2 URLs ("URLs carried over unchanged from step-01 §3.2 docs-only primary collection already passed their HEAD-check there"), or drop the reference to where they previously passed and instead say "URLs that were user-confirmed in step-01 without modification" if you want to still exempt them, but don't claim §3.3 ran a probe.

---

## No Issues Found

- **Envelope contract correctness (§4b error block):** The minimal error payload `{"status":"error","skill_name":"<name>","halt_reason":"<reason>"}` is correct. `assemble()` in the emit script builds all envelope keys via `ctx.get(...)`, so absent keys become `None`/null. The JSON Schema accepts null for `brief_path`, `version`, `language`, and `scope_type`. The `exit_code` is derived from `halt_reason`, never caller-supplied. No schema gap here.

- **§4b cross-reference resolution:** The renamed heading "4b. Headless Result Envelope (Canonical)" is the only `###`-level heading in step-05 with that label. The four converted pointer sites (step-01 §1 line 37, step-01 §8 line 315, step-05 §2b line 62, step-05 §3 lines 112/115) all reference "step-05 §4b" or "§4b" — unambiguous in context. No other doc file or test file references the old or new heading text in a way that would break.

- **SKILL.md On Activation block move:** The new order (Workflow Rules → On Activation → Stages) correctly positions `{headless_mode}` resolution before the stage table. The Invocation Contract and Result Contract sections follow the stage table unchanged. No rendering issues.

- **step-04 §1 anti-load guard:** The instruction ("do not re-load `{briefSchemaFile}` here…the schema is only consulted in §4") is precise and consistent with the §4 conditional load already present in that step.

- **step-05 §4b `skill_name` placeholder convention:** "pass the partially-gathered value or the literal `"unknown"`" — the emit script validates `not skill_name or not isinstance(skill_name, str)`, so `"unknown"` passes. Correct.

- **No broken anchor links** introduced within the changed files.

+### 4b. Headless Result Envelope (Canonical)

+This section is the canonical envelope-emission reference for the workflow. Every headless emission — the success terminal here and every HARD HALT in step-01/02/05 — uses this contract. Remote sites point here instead of restating it.
+
+**Success (this call site only — emitted from §3 directly):**
 
 ```bash
 echo '{"status":"success","brief_path":"<from §3 response>","skill_name":"<name>","version":"<from §3 response>","language":"<language>","scope_type":"<scope.type>","halt_reason":null}' | \

+**Error (used by every HARD HALT site):**
+
+```bash
+echo '{"status":"error","skill_name":"<name>","halt_reason":"<reason>"}' | \
+  uv run {emitBriefEnvelopeScript} emit --target stderr
+```
+
+When the HALT fires before `skill_name` has been resolved (step-01 §1 pre-flight write probe, step-01 §8 input-missing on a malformed args bundle), pass the partially-gathered value or the literal `"unknown"` — the script accepts any non-empty string at this position.
+
+The script derives `exit_code` deterministically from `halt_reason` (null→0, input-missing/input-invalid→2, forge-tier-missing/target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed→3, write-failed→4, overwrite-cancelled→5, user-cancelled→6 [interactive-only — headless never raises this]), validates against `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-brief-result-envelope.v1.json`, and prints the prefixed `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {…}` line.
+
+The script enforces the success/error halt_reason invariant (success requires null halt_reason; error requires non-null). The `user-cancelled` halt_reason is accepted for completeness (interactive `[X]` Cancel sites in step-01/03/04) but never appears on the headless code path.
 
-The envelope shape on HARD HALT (any phase) is the same call with `--target stderr`, `status: "error"`, and the matching `halt_reason` (one of `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`) — see the §3, §2b, §5 (no-halt) branches above and the §1/§2 HALTs in step-01/step-02 for invocation sites. The script enforces the success/error halt_reason invariant (success requires null halt_reason; error requires non-null). The `user-cancelled` halt_reason is also accepted by the script for completeness (interactive `[X]` Cancel sites in step-01/03/04 use it) but never appears on the headless code path.
+Invocation sites (each pointed at this block, not duplicated): step-01 §1 (write-failed pre-resolution), step-01 §8 (input-missing/input-invalid GATE), step-05 §2b (overwrite-cancelled), step-05 §3 (input-invalid/write-failed from script). Step-01 §1 forge-tier-missing and step-02 §1 (target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed) currently HALT without emitting an envelope — pre-existing gaps tracked separately, not addressed by this canonicalization.
 
 When `{headless_mode}` is false, skip this section silently — no envelope is emitted.
```

Reviewing `src/skf-brief-skill/` diff between `main` and `refactor/skf-brief-cleanup-quick-wins`.
