## Fix 1 Verification — §4b invocation-sites registry (step-05-write-brief.md line 145)

The current text at step-05.md:145 reads:

> Invocation sites (each pointed at this block, not duplicated): step-01 §1 (write-failed pre-resolution), step-01 §8 (input-missing/input-invalid GATE), step-05 §2b (overwrite-cancelled), step-05 §3 (input-invalid/write-failed from script). Step-01 §1 forge-tier-missing and step-02 §1 (target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed) currently HALT without emitting an envelope — pre-existing gaps tracked separately, not addressed by this canonicalization.

Cross-checking against the actual step files:

- **step-01 §1 write-failed**: step-01.md:37 — "emit the error envelope per **step-05 §4b**" — confirmed pointer present.
- **step-01 §8 input-missing/input-invalid**: step-01.md:315 — "emit the error envelope per **step-05 §4b**" — confirmed.
- **step-05 §2b overwrite-cancelled**: step-05.md:58 — "emit the error envelope per §4b with `halt_reason: 'overwrite-cancelled'`" — confirmed.
- **step-05 §3 input-invalid/write-failed**: step-05.md:112,115 — both branches cite §4b — confirmed.
- **step-01 §1 forge-tier-missing**: step-01.md:52-53 — "HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: 'forge-tier-missing'`)" with no §4b pointer — correctly documented as a pre-existing gap.
- **step-02 §1 target-inaccessible/gh-auth-failed**: step-02.md:47-49 — halts with exit codes and halt_reasons but no §4b pointer — correctly documented as a pre-existing gap.

Fix 1 is accurate. The registry lists exactly the 4 converted sites and correctly attributes the pre-existing gaps.

---

## Fix 2 Verification — step-03 §2b re-entry caching logic (lines 70-72)

The corrected text at step-03.md:72:

> **On re-entry from step-04 [R]:** if `doc_urls` is byte-identical to the list that was probed on the previous pass through this subsection, skip the parallel HEAD-check and reuse the prior probe results.

This is the critical sanity-check item. The problem is: **the LLM has no persistent memory of what it probed on the previous pass.**

When the user selects `[R]` in step-04 §5, the LLM re-loads and executes step-03.md fresh (step-04.md:152 — "Load, read entire file, then execute `{reviseStepFile}`"). There is no mechanism defined for the LLM to carry the previous probe results across that step boundary. Specifically:

- The `doc_urls` list is in conversation context — the LLM can compare the current list to what was discussed earlier.
- But "the prior probe results" (which specific URLs returned 2xx vs. 4xx) are only available if the LLM can recall them from earlier in the conversation thread.
- Shallow [R] revise loops are likely to happen within a single conversation turn, so context-window continuity means the LLM *can* scroll back to the step-03 §2b output from the prior pass and retrieve the per-URL results.
- However, the text says "byte-identical to the list that was probed on the **previous pass through this subsection**" — this phrasing assumes the LLM reliably knows which prior pass to compare against, and that it can reconstruct the exact results list from that pass. Long or multi-round conversations could push that context out of the effective working window.

**There is no cache storage mechanism defined.** The text directs the LLM to "reuse the prior probe results" but does not say where those results are stored or how to retrieve them. There is no write to a temp file, no field in `.brief-draft.json`, and no structured conversation artifact the LLM is told to reference. This means the optimization relies entirely on the LLM's ability to recall probe results from earlier in the same conversation thread. In long sessions this is unreliable, and even in short sessions there is nothing to stop the LLM from re-probing (falling back to the safe path) or incorrectly inferring a cache hit. The fix is directionally correct in framing this as a re-entry optimization rather than a first-entry fast-path, but it introduces an implicit dependency on LLM memory with no defined fallback if that memory is unavailable.

**Confidence: 88** — real correctness gap. If the LLM cannot recall the prior probe results, it either re-probes (which is the safe fallback and what it did before the fix) or skips the probe entirely on a false cache hit (the bad outcome). The text does not instruct it to fall back to re-probing when the prior results are not recoverable from context.

**File/line:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md:72`

**Suggested fix:** Either (a) persist the probe results into `.brief-draft.json` alongside the `doc_urls` list so the LLM can retrieve them from disk, or (b) add a fallback clause: "If prior probe results are not recoverable from conversation context, re-probe the current list." The simplest safe version: "…skip the parallel HEAD-check and reuse the prior probe results (from conversation context). If those results are no longer recoverable, re-probe."

---

## Fix 3 Verification — step-04 §1 schema anti-load guard vs. §4 conditional schema load

step-04.md:27:
> Use the values already accepted in steps 01-03 directly — do not re-load `{briefSchemaFile}` here. The 18 fields below are all in conversation; the schema is only consulted in §4 if an inline adjustment needs a specific field's validation rule cited.

step-04.md:141-142:
> If the adjustment requires explaining a field's validation rule or allowed values, load `{briefSchemaFile}` now (otherwise skip the read — the common path does not need it)

No conflict. §1 sets the default rule ("do not load the schema") and §4 provides the explicit override condition ("load it only if citing a validation rule"). The guard and the conditional load are consistent — one is the rule, the other is its named exception. No issue.

---

## SKILL.md On Activation move (commit 2562573a)

SKILL.md:33-38 — "On Activation" block is present and correctly positioned after "Workflow Rules" and before "Stages". It instructs the LLM to load config, resolve headless_mode, and chain to step-01. This is a structural placement check; the content is self-contained and nothing in the step files cross-references SKILL.md's section order. No issue introduced.

---

## Summary

**Critical issues: 0**

**Important issues: 1**

### Important — step-03 §2b re-entry cache has no defined retrieval mechanism or fallback

**Confidence: 88**
**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-03-scope-definition.md:72`

The cache condition ("byte-identical to the list that was probed on the previous pass") is checked against LLM conversation-context memory with no defined fallback. The text instructs the LLM to "reuse the prior probe results" but provides no storage mechanism (no `.brief-draft.json` field, no temp file, no explicit conversation artifact). In any [R] revise loop that is long enough to compress earlier context, or in an implementation that resets context on step re-entry, the LLM either (a) re-probes anyway — the safe fallback, which was the prior behavior — or (b) incorrectly infers a cache hit and suppresses the probe entirely. Path (b) is a silent correctness failure. The fix needs either a storage mechanism for the probe results or an explicit fallback instruction ("if prior results are not recoverable from context, re-probe").

All other review items verified clean:
- Fix 1 registry is accurate: 4 converted sites listed, 2 pre-existing gaps correctly attributed.
- Fix 2 first-entry behavior is correct: §2b still probes on first entry and only optimizes [R] re-entry.
- §4 schema conditional load does not conflict with §1 anti-load guard.
- SKILL.md On Activation placement is unaffected.

213	- Headless: log `"warn: skill name '{name}' collides with existing brief at {path}"` and proceed; the existing-brief overwrite policy in step-05 §2b is the canonical gate (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` unless `force` was supplied).
214	
215	**Portfolio-similarity check (Deep tier with QMD only, interactive only).** When the forge tier is `Deep` AND `tools.qmd` is true in `forge-tier.yaml`, the brief portfolio is already indexed in QMD collections (one `{skill-name}-brief` collection per existing brief, registered by step-05 §5 of every prior Deep-tier run). Run a similarity search against that portfolio so near-duplicates are caught before the user invests scope-definition time. The qmd CLI does not support glob-style collection selection, so enumerate first then query per collection in **a single message with N parallel Bash calls**:
216	
217	```bash
218	# 1. Enumerate brief collections (one per existing brief)
219	qmd collection list | awk '/-brief$/{print $1}'
220	
221	# 2. For each collection, query the proposed name + intent text:
222	qmd query "{name} {synthesized-or-intent-text}" -c {collection-name} -n 1 --min-score 0.6
223	```
224	
225	Aggregate the top hits across all `-brief` collections; keep the 3 highest-scoring across the union. If any results come back, surface them as a heads-up — *not* a HALT. Exact-name collision is already handled above; this check catches semantic near-duplicates that exact-match misses (e.g. `markdown-renderer` proposed when `marked` already exists, or `auth-gateway` when `auth-middleware` already exists). Present:
226	
227	```
228	**Heads up — these existing briefs look semantically close to `{name}`:**
229	  1. {existing-name} (similarity: {score})  — {existing-description}
230	  2. {existing-name} (similarity: {score})  — {existing-description}
231	
232	Continue with `{name}`, or pick a different name?
233	```
234	
235	On any QMD failure (binary missing, collection list empty, any per-collection query times out): log `"warn: portfolio-similarity check skipped — qmd query failed: {error}"` and continue silently — never HALT. Quick / Forge / Forge+ tiers do not run this check (qmd is Deep-tier-only per the canonical tier definition: `Deep = + ast-grep + gh + QMD` in `skf-forge-tier-rw.py`). Headless does not run this check either — it would either need to HALT on duplicates (over-aggressive for an automator) or silently log (no operator to act on it); leaving it interactive-only keeps the headless path side-effect-free against the QMD index.
236	
237	**Draft-resume check (interactive only).** After the name is confirmed, also check for an in-progress draft at `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/.brief-draft.json` (a step-01 §7 checkpoint from a previous run that did not reach step-05). If the file exists AND no `skill-brief.yaml` sits beside it (a finished brief uses the same dir), present:
238	
239	```
240	**An in-progress draft for `{name}` was found** (last updated: {mtime}).
241	  [Y] Resume from the saved draft (jump to §8 with prior answers restored)
242	  [N] Start fresh (delete the draft and re-gather)
243	```
244	
245	On `[Y]`: load the JSON, restore the captured fields (target_repo, source_type, source_authority, target_version, doc_urls, intent, scope_hint, description), and jump directly to §8 — **skip the rest of §6, §7, and §7b**. The restored `description` is authoritative and does not need re-synthesis (re-running §7b would overwrite the user's previously accepted description with a fresh candidate). The user can still revise any field at step-04. On `[N]`: delete `.brief-draft.json` and continue with §6 normally. In headless mode this check is skipped — drafts are an interactive-only convenience and the headless path runs to completion in a single invocation.
246	
247	### 7. Summarize Gathered Intent
248	
249	"**Here's what I've captured:**
250	
251	- **Target:** {repo URL or path}
252	- **Intent:** {user's intent summary}
253	- **Scope hints:** {any hints, or "None — we'll define scope after analysis"}
254	- **Skill name:** {confirmed name}
255	- **Source type:** {source or docs-only}
256	- **Source authority:** {official/community/internal}
257	{If target_version set:}

263	Ready to analyze the target repository?"

265	**Draft checkpoint (interactive only).** After the user confirms the summary, persist the captured state to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/.brief-draft.json` so a session interruption between here and step-05 doesn't force a full re-gather. Write a single JSON object with the captured fields (target_repo, source_type, source_authority, target_version, doc_urls, intent, scope_hint, description-after-§7b, the inferred forge_tier and tier_source) atomically (write to `.brief-draft.json.tmp` then rename). The file is removed by step-05 §4 after the final brief writes successfully (a draft only exists while the workflow is in flight). In headless mode skip this checkpoint — the run completes in a single invocation, no resume is meaningful.
266	
267	### 7b. Synthesize Skill Description
268	
269	The schema's `description` field is 1-3 sentences and surfaces in skill registries — it must exist by the time step-04 presents the brief. Synthesize it explicitly here, while the user's intent is fresh, instead of letting it fall out implicitly later.
270	
271	Compose a candidate 1-3 sentence description from the gathered material. **Write like a human library maintainer would** — what does an agent get from this skill, and when should it route here? Two facts must come through (what the skill is, when to use it); everything else is voice. Resist filling in the same skeleton every time.
272	
273	Load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` for the five voice examples (range of acceptable leads, structures, and trigger phrasings) and the "do not template-stamp" guidance, then compose in that spirit. The asset documents what "in that spirit" means; the gathered material to draw on is the target repo, the user's intent, the version if set, and any scope hints.

277	"**Proposed skill description:**
278	
279	> {synthesized description}
280	
281	This is what shows up when agents discover the skill. Edit it, replace it, or accept as-is."
282	
283	Wait for user confirmation or alternative. Store the accepted text as the brief's `description` field. The same field is re-presented in step-04 §3 for a final review pass — refinements there flow back to this value.
284	
285	**Headless:** if the `intent` argument was supplied, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and run the same synthesis against it (in `{document_output_language}`), then store the result. If `intent` was not supplied, fall back in priority order:
286	
287	1. **GitHub repo description** — when `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, fetch `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} --jq .description` (5-second timeout). If a non-empty description comes back, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and synthesize using the GitHub description as the seed in place of `intent`. Write the synthesized description in `{document_output_language}` regardless of the seed's language (the seed may be in any language; the output's language is dictated by the workflow's document-output configuration). Log `"info: description seeded from GitHub repo description"`. (The full `gh api repos` response is fetched again in step-02 §1; this lightweight `--jq .description` call only retrieves the one field.)
288	2. **Generic stub** — when no GitHub description is available (local-path target, GitHub repo with empty description, or `gh api` fails): derive from `target_repo` + `skill_name` (`"Use the {skill_name} skill to work with code or content from {target_repo}."`) — the generic fallback does not need the asset — and log `"warn: description synthesized without intent or repo description — narrow registry text."`
289	
290	### 8. Present MENU OPTIONS
291	
292	Display: "**Select:** [C] Continue to Target Analysis · [X] Cancel and exit"
293	
294	#### Menu Handling Logic:
295	
296	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
297	- IF X: Treat as user-cancellation. Display `"Cancelled — no brief was written."` and HALT (exit code 6, `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"`). When `{headless_mode}` is true the GATE auto-proceeds and never reaches this branch — `[X]` is interactive-only. Cancellation here is non-destructive: no files have been written yet by step-01.
298	- IF Any other: Help user, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#8-present-menu-options)
299	
300	#### EXECUTION RULES:
301	
302	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
303	- **GATE [default: use args]** — If `{headless_mode}`, consume pre-supplied arguments and auto-proceed. The full argument set (required/optional, defaults, halt codes, enum values) is documented in `{headlessArgsFile}` — load it now if you need to look up a specific argument. Validation is delegated to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`; the table is the canonical operator-facing documentation, the script enforces it.
304	
305	  **Preset merge (before validation).** If the headless args include a `preset` field, load `{sidecar_path}/brief-presets/{preset}.yaml` and merge its contents as defaults — explicit args override preset values, key by key. The preset file is YAML; if it does not exist, log `"warn: preset '{name}' not found at {path} — proceeding without preset"` and continue (do not HALT). If it parses but contains unknown fields, log per-field warnings and pass through unchanged (the validator's KNOWN_FIELDS check will catch any that survive). Drop the `preset` key itself from the merged dict before passing to the validator (it is consumed at this level and is not a brief field).
306	
307	  **Delegate validation to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`** instead of reasoning through the table rules in prose:
308	
309	  ```bash
310	  echo '<headless-args-as-json>' | uv run {validateBriefInputsScript}
311	  ```
312	
313	  The script returns a JSON envelope: `{valid, errors[], warnings[], normalized, halt_reason}`. Apply the result deterministically:
314	
315	  - **`valid: false`** — emit the error envelope per **step-05 §4b** with the script's `halt_reason` (`"input-missing"` for absent required args / docs-only without doc_urls; `"input-invalid"` for enum violations, malformed semver, malformed kebab-case skill_name). Surface `errors[]` to the operator log so the failure is debuggable. HALT.
316	  - **`valid: true`** — consume the `normalized` object as the source of truth (it has defaults applied per the table). Surface `warnings[]` to the operator log but do not HALT. Auto-proceed.
317	
318	  The script's `KNOWN_FIELDS` set must stay in sync with the table in `{headlessArgsFile}`.
319	
320	  **Headless source-authority detection** — the validator intentionally leaves `source_authority` ABSENT from `normalized` when not supplied (so detection can run here). After consuming `normalized`, if `source_authority` is absent AND `source_type=source` AND `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, run signal-driven detection:
321	
322	  ```bash
323	  gh api user --jq .login
324	  ```
325	
326	  Compare the result to the `owner` segment of `target_repo` (URL pattern `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>`) — **lower-case both values before comparing** (GitHub owner matching is case-insensitive but the API preserves case in responses). If they match, set `source_authority: "official"` — the operator is the repo's GitHub owner. Otherwise set `source_authority: "community"`. On any error from `gh api user` (unauthenticated, network failure, missing binary), log `"warn: source-authority detection skipped — gh api user failed"` and fall back to `"community"`. For local-path targets the comparison cannot apply; set `"community"` directly. (When `source_authority` was already supplied in `normalized`, the supplied value wins — no detection runs.)
327	
328	
329	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'

  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/references/headless-source-authority-detection.md",
  "content": "# Headless Source-Authority Detection\n\nLoaded by step-01 §8 only when **all three** preconditions hold:\n\n1. `{headless_mode}` is true\n2. `source_authority` is absent from the validator's `normalized` output (the validator intentionally leaves it absent so detection can run here — when it was supplied in args, the supplied value wins and detection does not run)\n3. `source_type=source` AND `target_repo` is a GitHub URL\n\nWhen any precondition is unmet, skip this entire procedure: docs-only forces `community` in §3.2; local-path targets default to `community` directly; non-GitHub source URLs are not classifiable from `gh api user` and also default to `community`.\n\n## Procedure\n\nProbe the operator's GitHub login:\n\n```bash\ngh api user --jq .login\n```\n\nCompare the result to the `owner` segment of `target_repo` (URL pattern `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>`) — **lower-case both values before comparing**. GitHub owner matching is case-insensitive but the API preserves case in responses, so a literal-string comparison would miss the match.\n\n| Outcome | Set | Rationale |\n|---------|-----|-----------|\n| login matches owner (case-insensitive) | `source_authority: \"official\"` | The operator is the repo's GitHub owner |\n| login does not match | `source_authority: \"community\"` | The operator is a downstream consumer |\n| `gh api user` errors (unauthenticated, network failure, missing binary) | `source_authority: \"community\"` (fallback) | Log `\"warn: source-authority detection skipped — gh api user failed\"` |\n| Local-path target | `source_authority: \"community\"` (no probe) | Comparison does not apply |\n"

After consuming `normalized`, if `source_authority` is absent AND `source_type=source` AND `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, run signal-driven detection:\n\n  ```bash\n  gh api user --jq .login\n  ```\n\n  Compare the result to the `owner` segment of `target_repo` (URL pattern `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>`) — **lower-case both values before comparing** (GitHub owner matching is case-insensitive but the API preserves case in responses). If they match, set `source_authority: \"official\"` — the operator is the repo's GitHub owner. Otherwise set `source_authority: \"community\"`. On any error from `gh api user` (unauthenticated, network failure, missing binary), log `\"warn: source-authority detection skipped — gh api user failed\"` and fall back to `\"community\"`. For local-path targets the comparison cannot apply; set `\"community\"` directly. (When `source_authority` was already supplied in `normalized`, the supplied value wins — no detection runs.)\n\n\n- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'",

Otherwise (precondition unmet, value already supplied, docs-only, or local-path) skip the load — `community` is the implicit default for the unmet branches.\n\n- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'"

  "content": "# Portfolio-Similarity Check\n\nLoaded by step-01 §6 only when **all three** preconditions hold:\n\n1. Forge tier is `Deep`\n2. `tools.qmd` is true in `forge-tier.yaml`\n3. The flow is interactive (the headless path skips this check entirely — it would either need to HALT on duplicates [over-aggressive for an automator] or silently log [no operator to act on it]; either choice has a side effect on the QMD index that is best avoided headlessly)\n\nThis check catches **semantic near-duplicates** that exact-name collision misses (e.g. `markdown-renderer` proposed when `marked` already exists, or `auth-gateway` when `auth-middleware` already exists). Exact-name collision is handled separately at §6 before this check runs.\n\n## Procedure\n\nThe brief portfolio is already indexed in QMD collections — one `{skill-name}-brief` collection per existing brief, registered by step-05 §5 of every prior Deep-tier run. The qmd CLI does not support glob-style collection selection, so enumerate first then query per collection in **a single message with N parallel Bash calls**:\n\n```bash\n# 1. Enumerate brief collections (one per existing brief)\nqmd collection list | awk '/-brief$/{print $1}'\n\n# 2. For each collection, query the proposed name + intent text:\nqmd query \"{name} {synthesized-or-intent-text}\" -c {collection-name} -n 1 --min-score 0.6\n```\n\nAggregate the top hits across all `-brief` collections; keep the 3 highest-scoring across the union. If any results come back, surface them as a heads-up — *not* a HALT:\n\n```\n**Heads up — these existing briefs look semantically close to `{name}`:**\n  1. {existing-name} (similarity: {score})  — {existing-description}\n  2. {existing-name} (similarity: {score})  — {existing-description}\n\nContinue with `{name}`, or pick a different name?\n```\n\n## Failure modes\n\nOn any QMD failure (binary missing, collection list empty, any per-collection query times out): log `\"warn: portfolio-similarity check skipped — qmd query failed: {error}\"` and continue silently — never HALT. Quick / Forge / Forge+ tiers do not run this check (qmd is Deep-tier-only per the canonical tier definition: `Deep = + ast-grep + gh + QMD` in `skf-forge-tier-rw.py`).\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/references/draft-checkpoint.md",
  "content": "# Draft Checkpoint Lifecycle\n\nThe `.brief-draft.json` file at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/.brief-draft.json` is a step-01 in-flight-state checkpoint. It exists only while the workflow has progressed past §7 but not yet completed step-05 — once the final brief writes successfully, step-05 §4 removes it.\n\n**Headless mode skips this entire lifecycle** — the run completes in a single invocation, so no resume is meaningful and no checkpoint is written.\n\nThe two halves of the lifecycle (resume on entry, write on §7 confirmation) form a pair. This file documents both so a single load covers them.\n\n## Half 1 — Resume Check (loaded from §6 after name confirmation)\n\nAfter the skill name is confirmed in §6, check for an in-progress draft at `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/.brief-draft.json`. **Only present the resume prompt** when the file exists AND no `skill-brief.yaml` sits beside it (a finished brief uses the same dir; if a finished brief exists, the draft is stale and step-05's overwrite gate is the right control point).\n\nWhen the precondition is met, present:\n\n```\n**An in-progress draft for `{name}` was found** (last updated: {mtime}).\n  [Y] Resume from the saved draft (jump to §8 with prior answers restored)\n  [N] Start fresh (delete the draft and re-gather)\n```\n\n### `[Y]` — Resume\n\nLoad the JSON and restore the captured fields: `target_repo`, `source_type`, `source_authority`, `target_version`, `doc_urls`, `intent`, `scope_hint`, `description`, `forge_tier`, `tier_source`. Then jump directly to §8 — **skip the rest of §6, all of §7, and all of §7b**.\n\nThe skip rule for §7b is load-bearing: re-running §7b would overwrite the user's previously accepted `description` with a fresh candidate synthesized from the seed material. The restored `description` is authoritative.\n\nThe user can still revise any field at step-04 §3 if a refinement is needed after the full brief is visible.\n\n### `[N]` — Start fresh\n\nDelete `.brief-draft.json` and continue with §6 normally (collision check, portfolio-similarity check if applicable, then §7 / §7b proceed in their normal order).\n\n## Half 2 — Checkpoint Write (loaded from §7 after summary confirmation)\n\nAfter the user confirms the §7 summary, persist the captured state atomically. Write a single JSON object with all of:\n\n- `target_repo`, `source_type`, `source_authority`\n- `target_version` (if set)\n- `doc_urls` (if collected)\n- `intent`, `scope_hint`\n- `description` (the §7b accepted text)\n- `forge_tier`, `tier_source` (for diagnostics)\n\nAtomic-write protocol: write to `.brief-draft.json.tmp` first, then `mv .brief-draft.json.tmp .brief-draft.json`. The rename is atomic on a single filesystem; a partial write never becomes visible as `.brief-draft.json`.\n\nThe file is removed by step-05 §4 after the final brief writes successfully.\n"
}

## Important Issues

### 1. Precondition mismatch: headless-source-authority ref lists `{headless_mode}` as an explicit precondition; the stub relies on structural implication only

Confidence: 80

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/references/headless-source-authority-detection.md`, line 5; compared with `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, lines 293-295.

The ref opens with three numbered preconditions, the first being "`{headless_mode}` is true". The §8 stub's detection paragraph does not restate this — it relies on the reader inferring that being inside the `GATE [default: use args]` block (which is already gated on `{headless_mode}`) covers precondition 1.

That inference is sound when an LLM reads step-01 top-to-bottom in one pass. However, the ref is written to be loadable in isolation (the precondition block at the top is meant to be a complete self-contained gate). If an LLM ever loads the ref directly — e.g., from the sidecar or a future step — and asks "should I run this?", precondition 1 would be checked correctly. If it loads the ref having entered §8 through the non-headless path (a bug elsewhere), the ref would correctly self-veto via precondition 1.

The ref is not wrong: precondition 1 adds a defense-in-depth safety check that the stub cannot provide (the stub can only prevent the load, but once the ref is loaded its own gate is the last line of defense). This is desirable, not a bug.

The actual concern is the inverse: could the stub's omission of `{headless_mode}` cause the load to happen incorrectly in interactive mode? Reading the stub:

> "**GATE [default: use args]** — If `{headless_mode}`, ... After consuming `normalized`, if `source_authority` is absent AND `source_type=source` AND `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, load `{headlessSourceAuthorityDetectionFile}`..."

The entire GATE bullet is scoped to `If {headless_mode}`. An LLM reading this linearly would not enter the GATE bullet in interactive mode. The ref load sentence is nested inside that block.

No behavior regression here, but the ref's self-documentation of `{headless_mode}` as precondition 1 is accurate and the stub's reliance on structural implication is a mild documentation asymmetry rather than a real issue. On reflection this is below the 80 threshold — lowering to informational.

Revised confidence: 72 — do not flag.

### 1 (revised). `[N]` start-fresh branch in Half 1 does not re-trigger the portfolio-similarity check

Confidence: 82

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/references/draft-checkpoint.md`, lines 29-31.

Half 1's `[N]` branch reads:

> "Delete `.brief-draft.json` and continue with §6 normally (collision check, portfolio-similarity check if applicable, then §7 / §7b proceed in their normal order)."

The stub in step-01 §6 (lines 219-220) says:

> "On `[Y]` resume, the procedure jumps directly to §8 with prior answers restored — **the rest of §6, all of §7, and all of §7b are skipped**. Otherwise (headless, or no draft file, or a finished brief sits beside the draft) skip the load and continue with §6 normally."

The stub's "continue with §6 normally" language matches the ref's `[N]` description. So far consistent.

The issue is sequencing. The §6 section executes in this order (reading the stub, lines 193-220):
1. Name proposal and confirmation
2. Collision check (interactive + headless)
3. Portfolio-similarity check (interactive + Deep + qmd)
4. Draft-resume check (interactive + draft exists + no finished brief)

When `[N]` is chosen at step 4, the ref says "continue with §6 normally (collision check, portfolio-similarity check if applicable...)". But collision check (step 2) and portfolio-similarity check (step 3) have already run before the draft-resume check (step 4) fired. The `[N]` branch cannot re-run them from within the ref — it can only proceed forward in §6. There are no further §6 actions after the draft-resume check before §7.

So "continue with §6 normally" in the `[N]` path effectively means: "continue to §7 / §7b" — the collision check and portfolio-similarity check already ran and are not re-executed after deleting the draft. The description in the ref is slightly misleading (implying those checks run after `[N]`, when they already ran before the resume prompt appeared), but it is not behaviorally wrong — the net effect of `[N]` is proceeding to §7 with the same name that was just collision-checked and similarity-checked.

The practical risk: an LLM reading the ref in isolation (having loaded it because the draft exists) might interpret "continue with §6 normally (collision check, portfolio-similarity check if applicable)" as an instruction to re-run those checks after choosing `[N]`. That would cause a redundant collision check and a second qmd similarity query with no user value, but would not cause incorrect behavior — it would just be wasteful.

This is low-harm (redundant checks, no state corruption) but is a potential behavior divergence from the inline original. Confidence: 82.

Fix: Change the `[N]` description in `draft-checkpoint.md` lines 29-31 to reflect the actual forward-continue:

```
### `[N]` — Start fresh

Delete `.brief-draft.json` and continue to §7 — collision and portfolio-similarity checks
already ran before the resume prompt appeared and do not repeat.
```

---

30	### 1. Discover Forge Tier
31	
32	**Pre-flight write probe.** Before any conversational state accumulates, verify `{forge_data_folder}` is writable. A read-only mount, full disk, or permissions-denied path otherwise only surfaces at step-05's atomic write — by then the user has invested 5–15 minutes. Run a single-byte write-and-remove probe:
33	
34	```bash
35	mkdir -p "{forge_data_folder}" && \
36	  printf 'probe' > "{forge_data_folder}/.skf-write-probe" && \
37	  rm "{forge_data_folder}/.skf-write-probe"
38	```
39	
40	`mkdir -p` succeeds on a pre-existing read-only mount, but the `printf > file` redirect actually attempts a write — that catches read-only, disk-full, and permissions-denied uniformly. **On any non-zero exit:** HALT (exit code 4, `halt_reason: "write-failed"`) — `"**Error:** {forge_data_folder} is not writable: {captured stderr}. Verify the path exists, the mount is writable, and there is free disk space, then re-run."` In headless mode, emit the error envelope per **step-05 §4b** with `halt_reason: "write-failed"` (skill_name is not yet resolved here — use the placeholder convention documented in §4b). 65	1. **Now:** Understand what you want to skill and why
66	2. **Next:** Analyze the target repo structure
67	3. **Then:** Define scope boundaries
68	4. **Finally:** Confirm and write the brief
69	
70	{If tier override was applied:}
71	**Your forge tier:** {override tier} (overridden from {original tier}) — {tier_gloss}
72	{Else:}
73	**Your forge tier:** {detected tier} — {tier_gloss}
74	
75	(Substitute `{tier_gloss}` with the matching one-liner so the user knows what the tier label means: `Quick` → "text-only extraction"; `Forge` → "AST-grep on, semantic discovery off"; `Forge+` → "AST-grep + ccc semantic discovery"; `Deep` → "full pipeline — AST + ccc + qmd portfolio search + LLM re-ranking". The tier sets the ceiling for what the downstream create-skill workflow can do; you can re-run setup later to change it.)
76	
77	Let's get started."
78	
79	### 3. Gather Target Repository
80	
81	This section has three sub-flows. Execute exactly one branch — 3.2 *or* 3.3 — based on the user's response in 3.1, then end with the shared confirmation. Do not mix branches.
82	
83	#### 3.1 Collect target
84	
85	"**What repository or documentation do you want to create a skill for?**
86	
87	Provide one of:
88	- A **GitHub URL** (e.g., `https://github.com/org/repo`)
89	- A **local path** (e.g., `/path/to/project`)
90	- **Documentation URLs** for a docs-only skill (e.g., `https://docs.stripe.com/api`) — use this when no source code is available (SaaS, closed-source)
91	
92	**Target:**"
93	
94	Wait for user response. Branch on the response:
95	
96	- Documentation URLs only (no source location) → §3.2
97	- GitHub URL or local filesystem path → §3.3
98	
99	#### 3.2 Branch — Documentation URLs (docs-only)
100	
101	- Set `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief data
102	- Collect one or more doc URLs with optional labels
103	- HEAD-check the collected URLs in parallel — do not loop sequentially. Issue all N `curl -sI {url}` (or equivalent) calls in a **single message with N parallel Bash calls**, then process the responses together. Each call must use a 5-second timeout (`curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`) to bound worst-case wall-time on hung hosts. Per response:
104	  - On 2xx/3xx: silently accept.
105	  - On 4xx/5xx, DNS failure, or timeout: warn `"Could not reach {url} — {status or error}. Confirm the URL is correct, or proceed anyway."` Interactive: re-prompt for a corrected URL or `[K] Keep anyway`. Headless: keep the URL and log the warning — the brief still records it but the failure is now visible at brief-creation time instead of materializing hours later in skf-create-skill.
106	- Set `source_authority: "community"` (forced for docs-only — T3 external documentation; the §3.3 source-authority prompt is skipped)
107	- Note: `source_repo` becomes optional (can be set to the main doc site URL for reference)
108	
109	Skip §3.3 and continue at "Confirm the target" below.
110	
111	#### 3.3 Branch — Source (GitHub URL or local path)
112	
113	- Set `source_type: "source"` (default)
114	- **Pre-validate the target before continuing.** Issue these probes in a single message with parallel Bash calls:
115	  - **GitHub URL:** `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`. On a 4xx (typically 404 for a typo'd repo or org), warn `"GitHub returned {status} for {url} — confirm the URL is correct."` and re-prompt. On 2xx, accept. (The full `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` check still runs in step-02 §1 to catch private-repo access issues — this HEAD probe is just for typo catch.)
116	  - **GitHub URL, in parallel with the above:** `gh auth status` — if it reports unauthenticated or the binary is missing, warn `"GitHub CLI not authenticated; step-02 will HALT when it tries to fetch the tree. Run 'gh auth login' before continuing, or supply a local clone path instead."` (Do not HALT here — let the user choose to fix or proceed; the canonical HALT still happens in step-02 §1's failure-class triage.)
117	  - **Local path:** verify the directory exists (`test -d {path}`). If not, warn `"Local path {path} does not exist."` and re-prompt.
118	- Optionally ask: "Are there any documentation URLs you'd like to include for supplemental context? (These will be fetched as T3 external references.)"
119	- If yes: collect doc URLs into `doc_urls`
120	
121	**Source authority (this branch only — docs-only forces `community` in §3.2):**
122	
123	**Interactive only** — skip this prompt entirely when `{headless_mode}` is true; the GATE in §8 resolves source_authority headlessly via the detection branch documented there.
124	
125	"**Are you the maintainer of this library, or creating a community skill?**"
126	- If maintainer: set `source_authority: "official"`
127	- If community user: set `source_authority: "community"` (default)
128	- If internal/proprietary: set `source_authority: "internal"`
129	
130	Default to `"community"` if user does not specify or skips.
131	
132	---
133	
134	Confirm the target.
135	
136	### 3b. Gather Target Version
137	
138	This step only collects `target_version` and validates its shape with the regex below — auto-detection runs in step-02 and precedence/invariant resolution lands in step-05's writer script. The canonical precedence rules live in `references/version-resolution.md`; load it from step-02 / step-05 only when the relevant section needs it.
139	
140	**Headless:** if `target_version` was supplied as an argument, store it and skip the interactive prompt below. If `doc_urls` were also supplied, treat the version-vs-doc-URL confirmation prompt as auto-confirmed (Y).
141	
142	"**Are you targeting a specific version of this library?**
143	(Leave blank to auto-detect from source)"
144	
145	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
146	"Since this is a docs-only skill with no source code, specifying the version is recommended — otherwise it defaults to 1.0.0."
147	
148	Wait for user response.
149	
150	**If user provides a version:** Validate the shape against `^v?\d+\.\d+\.\d+([.\-+][0-9A-Za-z][0-9A-Za-z.\-+]*)?$` (full X.Y.Z form, with optional `v` prefix and pre-release / build suffix; CalVer like `2024.04.01` accepted; partial forms like `1`, `1.2`, `v2`, `latest` rejected). On a match, store as `target_version` and set `version` to this value. On a non-match, warn `"'{value}' doesn't look like semver — write the explicit triple (e.g. 1.0.0). Fix it now or skip auto-detection?"` and re-prompt for a corrected value or blank to fall through to step-02 auto-detection.
151	**If blank:** Proceed without `target_version` — version will be auto-detected in step 02.
152	
153	{If target_version was set AND doc_urls are being collected (either docs-only primary or supplemental):}
154	
155	"**You're targeting version {target_version}. Do these documentation URLs correspond to that version?** [Y/N]"
156	
157	- **If Y:** Proceed.
158	- **If N:** "Provide the correct documentation URLs for version {target_version}." Re-collect doc_urls.
159	
160	### 4. Gather User Intent
161	
162	"**What's your intent for this skill?**
163	
164	Help me understand:
165	- **What** specifically do you want to skill from this repo?
166	- **Why** — what's the use case? How will an AI agent use this skill?
167	- **Any initial thoughts** on scope? (Full library? Specific modules? Public API only?)
168	
169	Take your time — the more context you share, the better the brief."
170	
171	Wait for user response. Ask follow-up questions if intent is unclear.
172	
173	### 5. Capture Scope Hints
174	
175	If the user mentioned scope preferences in their intent response, acknowledge them:
176	
177	"**I noted these scope hints from your response:**
178	- {list any scope hints mentioned}
179	
180	We'll refine these after analyzing the repo structure in the next step."
181	
182	If no scope hints were mentioned, that's fine — skip this acknowledgment.
183	
184	### 6. Derive Skill Name
185	
186	Based on the target repo and intent, propose a skill name:
187	
188	"**Suggested skill name:** `{derived-name}` (kebab-case)
189	
190	This will be used for the output directory and file naming. Want to use this name or suggest something different?"
191	
192	Wait for confirmation or alternative.
193	
194	**Collision check (interactive and headless):** before locking the name, check whether `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/skill-brief.yaml` already exists. If it does:
195	
196	- Interactive: generate 1–3 non-colliding candidate alternates by scanning sibling directories under `{forge_data_folder}/`. Apply each rule that fires; skip rules whose precondition isn't met:
197	  1. `{name}-v{N}` where `N` is the smallest positive integer that doesn't collide (e.g. `{name}-v2`, `{name}-v3`) — always applies
198	  2. `{name}-{target_version}` if `target_version` is set and the suffix wouldn't collide (e.g. `marked-1.2.3`)
199	  3. `{name}-{source_authority}` if `source_authority` is not `community` (e.g. `marked-internal` for an internal fork)
200	
201	  Number the surviving alternates `[1] [2] [3]…` in the order produced (1 alternate for a community-authority brief with no `target_version`; 2–3 otherwise). Then present:
202	
203	  ```
204	  **Heads up — a brief for `{name}` already exists at `{path}`.**
205	
206	  Suggested alternates (none collide):
207	    [1] {alternate-1}
208	    {if a second alternate was produced:} [2] {alternate-2}
209	    {if a third alternate was produced:} [3] {alternate-3}
210	
211	  Pick a number to use that name, type a different name, or press Enter to keep `{name}` and let step-05 §2b handle the overwrite prompt.
212	  ```
213	
214	  On a numbered choice, replace `{name}` with the chosen alternate. On Enter, fall through to step-05's overwrite gate. On any other input, treat as a new candidate name and re-run the collision check against it.
215	
216	- Headless: log `"warn: skill name '{name}' collides with existing brief at {path}"` and proceed; the existing-brief overwrite policy in step-05 §2b is the canonical gate (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` unless `force` was supplied).
217	
218	**Portfolio-similarity check.** When the flow is interactive AND forge tier is `Deep` AND `tools.qmd` is true in `forge-tier.yaml`, load `{portfolioSimilarityCheckFile}` and follow the procedure there to catch semantic near-duplicates that exact-name collision misses. Otherwise (headless, or tier below Deep, or qmd unavailable) skip the load — the check does not run.
219	
220	**Draft-resume check.** When the flow is interactive AND `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/.brief-draft.json` exists AND no `skill-brief.yaml` sits beside it, load `{draftCheckpointFile}` and follow Half 1 (Resume Check). On `[Y]` resume, the procedure jumps directly to §8 with prior answers restored — **the rest of §6, all of §7, and all of §7b are skipped**. Otherwise (headless, or no draft file, or a finished brief sits beside the draft) skip the load and continue with §6 normally.
221	
222	### 7. Summarize Gathered Intent
223	
224	"**Here's what I've captured:**
225	
226	- **Target:** {repo URL or path}
227	- **Intent:** {user's intent summary}
228	- **Scope hints:** {any hints, or "None — we'll define scope after analysis"}
229	- **Skill name:** {confirmed name}
230	- **Source type:** {source or docs-only}
231	- **Source authority:** {official/community/internal}
232	{If target_version set:}
233	- **Target version:** {target_version} (user-specified)
234	{If doc_urls collected:}
235	- **Doc URLs:** {count} supplemental documentation URLs
236	- **Forge tier:** {tier}
237	
238	Ready to analyze the target repository?"
239	
240	**Draft checkpoint.** When the flow is interactive, load `{draftCheckpointFile}` (or reuse it if already loaded for the §6 resume check) and follow Half 2 (Checkpoint Write) to persist the captured state atomically. Headless mode skips this — the run completes in a single invocation, no resume is meaningful.
241	
242	### 7b. Synthesize Skill Description
243	
244	The schema's `description` field is 1-3 sentences and surfaces in skill registries — it must exist by the time step-04 presents the brief. Synthesize it explicitly here, while the user's intent is fresh, instead of letting it fall out implicitly later.
245	
246	Compose a candidate 1-3 sentence description from the gathered material. **Write like a human library maintainer would** — what does an agent get from this skill, and when should it route here? Two facts must come through (what the skill is, when to use it); everything else is voice. Resist filling in the same skeleton every time.
247	
248	Load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` for the five voice examples (range of acceptable leads, structures, and trigger phrasings) and the "do not template-stamp" guidance, then compose in that spirit. The asset documents what "in that spirit" means; the gathered material to draw on is the target repo, the user's intent, the version if set, and any scope hints.
249	
250	Present:
251	
252	"**Proposed skill description:**
253	
254	> {synthesized description}
255	
256	This is what shows up when agents discover the skill. Edit it, replace it, or accept as-is."
257	
258	Wait for user confirmation or alternative. Store the accepted text as the brief's `description` field. The same field is re-presented in step-04 §3 for a final review pass — refinements there flow back to this value.
259	
260	**Headless:** if the `intent` argument was supplied, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and run the same synthesis against it (in `{document_output_language}`), then store the result. If `intent` was not supplied, fall back in priority order:
261	
262	1. **GitHub repo description** — when `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, fetch `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} --jq .description` (5-second timeout). If a non-empty description comes back, load `{descriptionVoiceExamplesFile}` and synthesize using the GitHub description as the seed in place of `intent`. Write the synthesized description in `{document_output_language}` regardless of the seed's language (the seed may be in any language; the output's language is dictated by the workflow's document-output configuration). Log `"info: description seeded from GitHub repo description"`. (The full `gh api repos` response is fetched again in step-02 §1; this lightweight `--jq .description` call only retrieves the one field.)
263	2. **Generic stub** — when no GitHub description is available (local-path target, GitHub repo with empty description, or `gh api` fails): derive from `target_repo` + `skill_name` (`"Use the {skill_name} skill to work with code or content from {target_repo}."`) — the generic fallback does not need the asset — and log `"warn: description synthesized without intent or repo description — narrow registry text."`
264	
265	### 8. Present MENU OPTIONS
266	
267	Display: "**Select:** [C] Continue to Target Analysis · [X] Cancel and exit"
268	
269	#### Menu Handling Logic:
270	
271	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
272	- IF X: Treat as user-cancellation. Display `"Cancelled — no brief was written."` and HALT (exit code 6, `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"`). When `{headless_mode}` is true the GATE auto-proceeds and never reaches this branch — `[X]` is interactive-only. Cancellation here is non-destructive: no files have been written yet by step-01.
273	- IF Any other: Help user, then [Redisplay Menu Options](#8-present-menu-options)
274	
275	#### EXECUTION RULES:
276	
277	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
278	- **GATE [default: use args]** — If `{headless_mode}`, consume pre-supplied arguments and auto-proceed. The full argument set (required/optional, defaults, halt codes, enum values) is documented in `{headlessArgsFile}` — load it now if you need to look up a specific argument. Validation is delegated to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`; the table is the canonical operator-facing documentation, the script enforces it.
279	
280	  **Preset merge (before validation).** If the headless args include a `preset` field, load `{sidecar_path}/brief-presets/{preset}.yaml` and merge its contents as defaults — explicit args override preset values, key by key. The preset file is YAML; if it does not exist, log `"warn: preset '{name}' not found at {path} — proceeding without preset"` and continue (do not HALT). If it parses but contains unknown fields, log per-field warnings and pass through unchanged (the validator's KNOWN_FIELDS check will catch any that survive). Drop the `preset` key itself from the merged dict before passing to the validator (it is consumed at this level and is not a brief field).
281	
282	  **Delegate validation to `{validateBriefInputsScript}`** instead of reasoning through the table rules in prose:
283	
284	  ```bash
285	  echo '<headless-args-as-json>' | uv run {validateBriefInputsScript}
286	  ```
287	
288	  The script returns a JSON envelope: `{valid, errors[], warnings[], normalized, halt_reason}`. Apply the result deterministically:
289	
290	  - **`valid: false`** — emit the error envelope per **step-05 §4b** with the script's `halt_reason` (`"input-missing"` for absent required args / docs-only without doc_urls; `"input-invalid"` for enum violations, malformed semver, malformed kebab-case skill_name). Surface `errors[]` to the operator log so the failure is debuggable. HALT.
291	  - **`valid: true`** — consume the `normalized` object as the source of truth (it has defaults applied per the table). Surface `warnings[]` to the operator log but do not HALT. Auto-proceed.
292	
293	  The script's `KNOWN_FIELDS` set must stay in sync with the table in `{headlessArgsFile}`.
294	
295	  **Headless source-authority detection.** After consuming `normalized`, if `source_authority` is absent AND `source_type=source` AND `target_repo` is a GitHub URL, load `{headlessSourceAuthorityDetectionFile}` and follow the procedure there. Otherwise (precondition unmet, value already supplied, docs-only, or local-path) skip the load — `community` is the implicit default for the unmet branches.
296	
297	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
298	
299	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
300	
301	ONLY WHEN C is selected and target repository is confirmed will you load and read fully `./step-02-analyze-target.md` to execute target analysis.
302	
303

1	# Description Voice Examples
2	
3	Loaded by step-01 §7b only. The five examples below show the *range* of acceptable voices for the `description` field — they vary in lead, structure, and trigger phrasing on purpose. The point is to anchor the LLM to "two facts must come through (what the skill is, when to use it); everything else is voice — do not template-stamp."
4	
5	## Examples
6	
7	> Render Markdown to HTML using the marked library. Use when the user pastes raw Markdown and wants formatted output, or asks how to convert MD files in a build pipeline.
8	
9	> Stripe API client for Node.js — payment intents, subscriptions, customer portal, webhooks. Triggers on tasks involving Stripe-managed payments, subscription billing, or webhook event handling.
10	
11	> Charts and visualizations powered by D3.js. Reach for this when the user asks to plot data, build interactive graphs, or wants bare D3 control instead of a React-charts abstraction.
12	
13	> Lint Python code with Ruff. Use when the user wants to add or configure Ruff in a Python project, debug rule selectors, or understand why a specific check fired.
14	
15	> Date and time arithmetic via Luxon — parsing, formatting, time zones, durations, intervals. Use when working with dates in ways that exceed `Date.toISOString()` but you don't want a full Moment.js footprint.
16	
17	## Notes on Voice
18	
19	Each example leads differently (verb / noun / "Charts and..." / verb / noun-phrase) and matches the trigger phrasing ("Use when...", "Triggers on...", "Reach for this when...") to the voice rather than copy-pasting a single template. Compose in that spirit using the gathered material — the target repo, the user's intent, the version if set, and any scope hints — but **do not template-stamp**.

1	# Scope Templates Reference
2	
3	## Scope Type Options
4	
5	Present these options to the user for selection:
6	
7	**[F] Full Library** — Include everything. Best for smaller, focused libraries.
8	- All public exports, all modules
9	- Exclude only tests, build artifacts, and internal utilities
10	
11	**[M] Specific Modules** — Select which modules to include. Best for large libraries where only some parts are relevant.
12	- You choose which modules/directories
13	- Fine-grained control over what's in and out
14	
15	**[P] Public API Only** — Include only the public-facing API surface. Best for libraries with a clear public/private boundary.
16	- Entry points and exported interfaces only
17	- Internal implementation excluded
18	
19	**[C] Component Library** — Optimized for UI component libraries with registries, props-based APIs, and design system variants.
20	- Component registry as primary API surface (not individual exports)
21	- Props interfaces as API contracts (not function signatures)
22	- Auto-exclude demo/example/story files (with user confirmation)
23	- Variant consolidation across design systems
24	
25	**[R] Reference App** — Whole-app pattern-reference skill. Use when the source is a working example app and the skill's value is **wiring patterns** (lifecycle, IPC, build-config, distribution) rather than a public library API.
26	- Pattern surface as primary API slot (not individual exports)
27	- Adoption Steps as primary workflow format (not API-call chains)
28	- Tier 2 organized as `references/pattern-*.md` groupings (not per-function)
29	- Export-count stats are pattern-surface proxies, not library exports
30	
31	## Boundary Definitions by Scope Type
32	
33	### Full Library Boundaries
34	
35	Default inclusions:
36	- All source files under {main source directory}
37	- All public modules: {list from analysis}
38	
39	Default exclusions:
40	- Test files (`**/*.test.*`, `**/*.spec.*`, `**/test/`, `**/tests/`)
41	- Build artifacts (`**/dist/`, `**/build/`, `**/target/`)
42	- Configuration files
43	- Documentation source files
44	
45	Prompt: "Any additional exclusions you'd like to add? Or adjustments to these defaults?"
46	
47	### Specific Modules Boundaries
48	
49	**Phase 1 — Module selection:**
50	
51	Present numbered list of modules from step 02 with brief descriptions.
52	Prompt: "Which modules would you like to include? (Enter numbers, comma-separated):"
53	
54	**Phase 2 — Granularity within selected modules:**
55	
56	For selected modules, ask:
57	- **A)** Everything in those modules (all files)
58	- **B)** Only public exports from those modules
59	
60	Prompt: "Any files or patterns to explicitly exclude within these modules?"
61	
62	### Public API Only Boundaries
63	
64	**Phase 1 — Export selection:**
65	
66	Present numbered list of exports/entry points from step 02.
67	Prompt: "Which of these would you like to include? (Enter numbers, or 'all'):"
68	
69	**Phase 2 — Confirm exclusions:**
70	
71	Exclusions will include all internal implementation files, tests, and utilities.
72	Prompt: "Any additional items you'd like to include or exclude?"
73	
74	### Reference App Boundaries
75	
76	**Phase 1 — Pattern surface intent:**
77	
78	Ask: "What is the authored pattern surface for this skill? List the files (or directories) the user must touch to adopt the pattern — entry points, config files, lifecycle hooks, build scripts."
79	
80	- Record the user's list as `scope.tier_a_include` when narrower than a broad `scope.include`. Reference-app briefs benefit strongly from `tier_a_include` because the denominator is small and precise.
81	- Prompt follow-up: "Any files outside that list that should still be in scope for completeness (tests, fixtures, supporting configs)?"
82	
83	**Phase 2 — Scope.include and exclusions:**
84	
85	Set `scope.include` to the pattern-surface file list (or broader union when the author flagged supporting files). Default exclusions mirror the Full Library defaults (tests, build artifacts, docs source). Record `scope.notes` with a one-sentence description of the pattern (e.g., "Embedded Python sidecar pattern for Electron apps — lifecycle orchestration, RPC proxy, build-copy wiring").
86	
87	**Phase 3 — Confirmation:**
88	
89	Summary showing: pattern surface count, `tier_a_include` vs `include` distinction, notes. Prompt: "Does this reference-app scope look right? Adjust before continuing."
90	
91	### Docs-Only Boundaries
92	
93	**No source code access.** Scope is defined by the `doc_urls` collected during intent gathering.
94	
95	- All content derived from external documentation
96	- No include/exclude patterns — coverage determined by fetched documentation
97	- All extractions labeled T3 (`[EXT:{url}]` citations)
98	
99	Prompt: "Any additional documentation URLs to include? Or URLs to exclude from the ones collected?"
100	
101	### Component Library Boundaries
102	
103	**Phase 1 — Registry Detection:**
104	
105	Auto-detect or accept explicit `registry_path` from user. Scan source tree for files matching common registry patterns:
106	- Files named `registry.ts`, `components.ts`, `index.ts` in `registry/`, `catalog/`, or `components/` directories
107	- Arrays of objects with `{ id, name, component }` structure and 10+ entries
108	- Files with `Component[]` type annotations
109	
110	Present detected registry candidate(s) to user for confirmation.
111	Prompt: "I found what looks like a component registry at {path} ({count} entries). Is this correct? Or provide the registry path:"
112	
113	**Phase 2 — Demo/Example Exclusion:**
114	
115	Auto-detect demo directories and file patterns:
116	- Directories: `demo/`, `demos/`, `stories/`, `examples/`, `__stories__/`, `storybook/`
117	- Files: `*.stories.*`, `*.story.*`, `*.example.*`, `*.demo.*`
118	
119	Present detected patterns to user before applying (do NOT exclude silently).
120	Prompt: "**Auto-detected {N} demo/example files** in {M} directories. Confirm exclusion? [Y/n] Or adjust patterns:"
121	
122	**Phase 3 — Variant Selection (if applicable):**
123	
124	If multiple design system variant directories detected (e.g., `react-shadcn/`, `react-baseui/`, `react-carbon/`):
125	- Present detected variants with component counts per variant
126	- User selects primary variant and which variants to include

src/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py
# /// script
# requires-python = ">=3.11"
# dependencies = []
# ///
"""SKF Extract Public API — pure parser for manifest + entry-point content.

Takes a JSON payload on stdin describing one logical package (one
manifest, one or more entry-point files) and emits a structured
extraction inventory: package name, version, description, public
exports, declared dependencies, and (for Maven/Gradle) discovered
sub-modules. The helper does NO file I/O — the caller fetches files
(via gh api / web browsing / local filesystem / wherever) and pipes
their contents in. Multi-module monorepos are caller-orchestrated:
invoke once per module, aggregate the results.

Supported languages (--language values; first listed is preferred):

  js, ts, javascript, typescript    package.json     index.{js,ts}, src/index.{js,ts}
  python                            pyproject.toml   __init__.py / setup.py
  rust                              Cargo.toml       src/lib.rs
  go                                go.mod           top-level *.go
  java                              pom.xml          src/main/java/**/*.java
  kotlin                            build.gradle*    src/main/kotlin/**/*.kt

Mode flag (currently `quick` only; `full` reserved for skf-create-skill):

  --mode quick    Top-level exports only (one entry file per language).

Input JSON shape (stdin):

  {
    "language": "python",
    "manifest": {"path": "pyproject.toml", "content": "..."},
    "entries":  [{"path": "src/foo/__init__.py", "content": "..."}, ...],
    "mode":     "quick"
  }

Output JSON shape (stdout):

  {
    "language":    "python",
    "package_name": "foo",
    "version":      "1.2.3",
    "description":  "...",
    "exports":      [{"name": "Bar", "type": "class", "source_file": "..."}, ...],
    "dependencies": ["requests", "pydantic", ...],
    "modules":      ["server", "client"],         (Maven/Gradle only)
    "extra":        {"group_id": "com.example"}, (Maven only)
    "warnings":     ["..."]                        (parse failures, fallbacks)
  }

Exit codes:

  0    success
  1    payload-level error (unknown language, no manifest content)
  2    stdin / argparse / JSON-decode error
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import re
import sys
import tomllib
import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
from typing import Callable

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Manifest parsers
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


def parse_package_json(content: str) -> dict:
    try:
        data = json.loads(content)
    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
        return {"_parse_error": f"package.json JSON parse error: {e}"}
    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        return {"_parse_error": "package.json root is not an object"}
    return {
        "name": data.get("name"),
        "version": data.get("version"),
        "description": data.get("description"),
        "main": data.get("main"),
        "exports": data.get("exports"),
        "dependencies": list((data.get("dependencies") or {}).keys()),
        "modules": [],
    }


def parse_pyproject_toml(content: str) -> dict:
    try:
        data = tomllib.loads(content)
    except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError as e:
        return {"_parse_error": f"pyproject.toml parse error: {e}"}
    project = data.get("project") or {}
    raw_deps = project.get("dependencies") or []
    dep_names: list[str] = []
    for d in raw_deps:

Branch on the response:\n\n- Empty input, `cancel`, `exit`, `[X]`, `q`, or `:q` → Display `\"Cancelled — no brief was written.\"` and HALT (exit code 6, `halt_reason: \"user-cancelled\"`). Cancellation here is non-destructive — no files have been written yet by step-01. Headless mode never reaches this branch (the GATE in §8 short-circuits the interactive sub-flows).\n- Documentation URLs only (no source location) → §3.2\n- GitHub URL or local filesystem path → §3.3\n- Any other free-form question (e.g. \"what is this?\", \"show me an example\", \"how does SKF work?\") → answer briefly, re-display the prompt"
}

How will an AI agent use this skill?\n- **Any initial thoughts** on scope? (Full library? Specific modules? Public API only?)\n\nTake your time — the more context you share, the better the brief.\"\n\nWait for user response. Ask follow-up questions if intent is unclear."
}

  "new_string": "- Set `source_type: \"source\"` (default)\n- **Pre-validate the target before continuing.** Issue these probes in a single message with parallel Bash calls:\n  - **GitHub URL:** `curl -sI --max-time 5 {url}`. On a 4xx (typically 404 for a typo'd repo or org), warn `\"GitHub returned {status} for {url} — confirm the URL is correct.\"` and re-prompt. On 2xx, accept.\n  - **GitHub URL, in parallel:** `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} --jq .name` (5-second timeout via `gh api --hostname github.com --method GET ... ` or just rely on default). On 403/404, warn `\"GitHub API returned {status} for {owner}/{repo} — the repo may be private or your token may not have access. Step-02 will HALT here if this is not resolved. Continue anyway, or fix and re-prompt?\"` and offer `[K] Keep anyway` / re-prompt for a corrected URL. Do not HALT — the canonical HALT still happens in step-02 §1, but surfacing access failures at URL-entry time prevents 5+ minutes of intent investment getting lost. On any other error (network failure, missing binary), log silently and let `gh auth status` below catch it. On 2xx, accept silently.\n  - **GitHub URL, in parallel:** `gh auth status` — if it reports unauthenticated or the binary is missing, warn `\"GitHub CLI not authenticated; step-02 will HALT when it tries to fetch the tree. Run 'gh auth login' before continuing, or supply a local clone path instead.\"` (Do not HALT here — let the user choose to fix or proceed; the canonical HALT still happens in step-02 §1's failure-class triage.)\n  - **Local path:** verify the directory exists (`test -d {path}`). If not, warn `\"Local path {path} does not exist.\"` and re-prompt."
}

  "old_string": "**[F] Full Library** — Include everything. Best for smaller, focused libraries.\n- All public exports, all modules\n- Exclude only tests, build artifacts, and internal utilities\n\n**[M] Specific Modules** — Select which modules to include. Best for large libraries where only some parts are relevant.\n- You choose which modules/directories\n- Fine-grained control over what's in and out\n\n**[P] Public API Only** — Include only the public-facing API surface. Best for libraries with a clear public/private boundary.\n- Entry points and exported interfaces only\n- Internal implementation excluded\n\n**[C] Component Library** — Optimized for UI component libraries with registries, props-based APIs, and design system variants.\n- Component registry as primary API surface (not individual exports)\n- Props interfaces as API contracts (not function signatures)\n- Auto-exclude demo/example/story files (with user confirmation)\n- Variant consolidation across design systems\n\n**[R] Reference App** — Whole-app pattern-reference skill. Use when the source is a working example app and the skill's value is **wiring patterns** (lifecycle, IPC, build-config, distribution) rather than a public library API.\n- Pattern surface as primary API slot (not individual exports)\n- Adoption Steps as primary workflow format (not API-call chains)\n- Tier 2 organized as `references/pattern-*.md` groupings (not per-function)\n- Export-count stats are pattern-surface proxies, not library exports",
  "new_string": "**[F] Full Library** — Include everything. Best for smaller, focused libraries.\n- All public exports, all modules\n- Exclude only tests, build artifacts, and internal utilities\n- *Looks like:* `marked` (single-purpose Markdown→HTML); `nanoid` (id generator); `zod` (validation library)\n\n**[M] Specific Modules** — Select which modules to include. Best for large libraries where only some parts are relevant.\n- You choose which modules/directories\n- Fine-grained control over what's in and out\n- *Looks like:* `lodash` skill scoped to just `lodash/array` and `lodash/string`; `aws-sdk` skill scoped to just S3 and DynamoDB\n\n**[P] Public API Only** — Include only the public-facing API surface. Best for libraries with a clear public/private boundary.\n- Entry points and exported interfaces only\n- Internal implementation excluded\n- *Looks like:* `stripe` (payment intents, subscriptions, webhooks — not internal HTTP plumbing); `redis` client (connection + commands, not protocol parsers)\n\n**[C] Component Library** — Optimized for UI component libraries with registries, props-based APIs, and design system variants.\n- Component registry as primary API surface (not individual exports)\n- Props interfaces as API contracts (not function signatures)\n- Auto-exclude demo/example/story files (with user confirmation)\n- Variant consolidation across design systems\n- *Looks like:* `shadcn-ui` (Button, Dialog, Form... 50+ components); Material-UI; Carbon Design System\n\n**[R] Reference App** — Whole-app pattern-reference skill. Use when the source is a working example app and the skill's value is **wiring patterns** (lifecycle, IPC, build-config, distribution) rather than a public library API.\n- Pattern surface as primary API slot (not individual exports)\n- Adoption Steps as primary workflow format (not API-call chains)\n- Tier 2 organized as `references/pattern-*.md` groupings (not per-function)\n- Export-count stats are pattern-surface proxies, not library exports\n- *Looks like:* a Tauri starter app (window setup + IPC bridge + build config); a Next.js auth example (route handlers + middleware + session storage wiring)"
}

  "new_string": "def _select_manifest_parser(language: str, manifest_path: str) -> ManifestParser:\n    \"\"\"Pick the right manifest parser, special-casing Python's setup.py.\"\"\"\n    if language == \"python\" and manifest_path.endswith(\"setup.py\"):\n        return parse_setup_py\n    return LANGUAGE_DISPATCH[language][0]\n\n\n# Release-time placeholder versions that appear in committed manifests but\n# resolve to a real version only at publish time. Briefs that silently inherit\n# these as the resolved version produce skills tagged with garbage version\n# strings; surface them at brief-creation instead.\n_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION_PREFIXES = (\"workspace:\",)\n_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION_EXACTS = frozenset({\"0.0.0-development\", \"0.0.0-semantically-released\"})\n\n\ndef _detect_placeholder_version(version: str | None) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the original placeholder string if `version` is a known release-time sentinel, else None.\"\"\"\n    if not version or not isinstance(version, str):\n        return None\n    v = version.strip().lower()\n    if v in _PLACEHOLDER_VERSION_EXACTS:\n        return version\n    if any(v.startswith(prefix) for prefix in _PLACEHOLDER_VERSION_PREFIXES):\n        return version\n    return None\n\n\ndef extract(payload: dict) -> dict:"
}

But wait — does the headless GATE in §8 run BEFORE §4? No — §8 is the LAST section before transitioning to step-02. The §3.1 cancel is in the MIDDLE of the flow, before §4. And the headless mode note on §3.1 cancel says "Headless mode never reaches this branch (the GATE in §8 short-circuits the interactive sub-flows)."

But wait — if headless mode "short-circuits the interactive sub-flows" via the §8 GATE, does that mean §3.1 through §7b are skipped entirely in headless mode? That doesn't make sense — the GATE in §8 auto-proceeds with pre-supplied args, but steps 3-7 must still run to collect the intent, gather the target repo, etc.

Let me re-read §8 more carefully:

Line 299: "**GATE [default: use args]** — If `{headless_mode}`, consume pre-supplied arguments and auto-proceed."

The GATE is at §8 (the final menu). In headless mode, the GATE auto-proceeds at §8. But the preceding sections (§3.1-§7b) still execute — they're just the interactive data-gathering steps. The GATE in §8 short-circuits only the FINAL confirmation menu, not the earlier sections.

But then: §3.1 says "Headless mode never reaches this branch (the GATE in §8 short-circuits the interactive sub-flows)." This is confusing — if the GATE in §8 only short-circuits §8's menu, why would §3.1's cancel branch be unreachable in headless mode?

Looking more carefully: in headless mode, the `target` is pre-supplied via args. The user never types anything interactively. So §3.1 displays the prompt but the LLM should auto-consume the pre-supplied `target` arg rather than waiting for user input. The cancel branch requires a user to actively type `cancel` — which would never happen in headless mode since there's no human interaction. The note "Headless mode never reaches this branch" means: in headless mode, the target arg is always consumed programmatically without displaying the interactive prompt, so cancel/empty input from a user is structurally impossible. The phrasing "the GATE in §8 short-circuits the interactive sub-flows" is slightly misleading — it implies the GATE somehow reaches back to control §3.1, which it doesn't. Rather, it's the nature of headless mode (no interactive prompts) that makes §3.1's cancel branch unreachable.

This imprecise phrasing is pre-existing in the skill's headless logic — it's not introduced by this PR. But the note correctly conveys that cancel at §3.1 won't happen in headless mode.

## Important

### Finding 1 — Missing test for `0.0.0-development` exact match (C8)

Confidence: 85

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/test/test-skf-extract-public-api.py`

`_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION_EXACTS` at `skf-extract-public-api.py:423` is defined as:

```python
_PLACEHOLDER_VERSION_EXACTS = frozenset({"0.0.0-development", "0.0.0-semantically-released"})
```

The test suite has `test_semantic_release_sentinel_warns_and_nulls` (line 372) which exercises `"0.0.0-semantically-released"`, and `test_workspace_placeholder_version_warns_and_nulls` (line 358) which exercises `"workspace:*"`. There is no test covering `"0.0.0-development"` or its uppercase variant `"0.0.0-DEVELOPMENT"` (which is case-insensitively matched via `.lower()` at line 431).

The commit description claims "Three new pytest cases verify the workspace match, semantic-release match, and no-false-positive on real versions" — the semantic-release match only covers one of the two exact-match sentinels. If the `frozenset` were accidentally missing `"0.0.0-development"` (or if the string were misspelled), no test would catch it.

Fix: add a fourth test case:

```python
def test_development_sentinel_warns_and_nulls(self):
    result = mod.extract(
        {
            "language": "js",
            "manifest": {
                "path": "package.json",
                "content": '{"name":"foo","version":"0.0.0-development"}',
            },
            "entries": [],
        }
    )
    assert result["version"] is None
    assert any("placeholder" in w and "0.0.0-development" in w for w in result["warnings"])
```

---

### Finding 2 — First-timer `{forge_data_folder}` empty-check has no concrete command (C3)

Confidence: 82

File: `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-01-gather-intent.md`, line 169

The §4 first-timer rail says: "check whether `{forge_data_folder}/` is empty (no subdirectories containing `skill-brief.yaml`)". This is a filesystem traversal. Every other filesystem operation in step-01 that could go wrong has an explicit bash snippet — the write-probe in §1 (line 34) has:

```bash
mkdir -p "{forge_data_folder}" && \
  printf 'probe' > "{forge_data_folder}/.skf-write-probe" && \
  rm "{forge_data_folder}/.skf-write-probe"
```

The collision check in §6 (line 207) says "check whether `{forge_data_folder}/{name}/skill-brief.yaml` already exists" — a single-path existence check that is trivially unambiguous. The §4 check, by contrast, requires traversing an unknown directory tree, which a LLM may implement as `ls {forge_data_folder}` (only catches top-level entries), `find {forge_data_folder} -name skill-brief.yaml` (correct), or even `ls -R` (correct but wasteful). Without a concrete command, LLMs under time pressure tend to pick the cheapest option, which is `ls`, and that fails silently if briefs live one directory deeper.

Since the rail is soft (worst case: a first-timer skips the example, or a repeat user sees it on their second run), this is not safety-critical. But it is the one place in the step where a missing concrete command could cause consistent mis-behavior rather than a one-off judgment call.

Fix: add the suggested command inline, mirroring the §1 pattern:

```
Check whether `{forge_data_folder}/` is empty (no subdirectories containing `skill-brief.yaml`):

```bash
find "{forge_data_folder}" -maxdepth 2 -name "skill-brief.yaml" | head -1
```

If the command produces no output, offer the example rail. If it produces any output, skip the rail silently.
```
