## Cleared items (no issue found)

**Focus 1 — Behavior preservation at §3.1.** The cancel branch (line 100) is additive before the existing docs/source/free-form routing. All three pre-existing branches remain reachable. No instruction displaced.

**Focus 2 — Cancel contract consistency.** §3.1 (line 100) and §8 (line 293) both emit exit code 6, `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"`, message `"Cancelled — no brief was written."`, and both carry the note that headless mode never reaches the branch. Exact match.

**Focus 4 — `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo} --jq .name` correctness.** GET is the implicit default for `gh api`; `--method GET` is not required. The `.name` field is the correct jq path for the GitHub `/repos/{owner}/{repo}` response. URL parsing of `{owner}` and `{repo}` from a `https://github.com/{owner}/{repo}` URL is unambiguous, and step-02 §1 already uses the same `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` pattern without documenting the extraction. No regression introduced.

**Focus 5 — Ellipsis edge case in sentence-count heuristic.** The heuristic counts `.`/`!`/`?` followed by whitespace or end-of-string. In `...`, the first two dots are followed by `.`, not whitespace, so they do not match. Only the final dot matches if the string ends there. The count for an ellipsis-terminated sentence is 1, not 3. The prompt's concern about triple-counting is a false positive.

**Focus 6 — `_detect_placeholder_version` coverage across all languages.** All parsers in `LANGUAGE_DISPATCH` return a dict with a `"version"` key. `parse_go_mod` hardcodes `"version": None`, which is handled by the `if not version` guard at line 429. The Cargo workspace `version.workspace = true` pattern returns a dict (not a string) which is filtered by `not isinstance(version, str)`. Both pass through silently and correctly.

**Focus 7 — `workspace-foo` false positive.** `"workspace-foo".startswith("workspace:")` is `False` (the colon is not present). No false positive. Case-insensitive matching via `.lower()` correctly catches `"0.0.0-DEVELOPMENT"` and `"WORKSPACE:*"`.

**Focus 8 — No regression on existing tests.** Accepted per pre-commit attestation.

1	---
2	nextStepFile: './step-03-scope-definition.md'
3	versionResolutionFile: 'references/version-resolution.md'
4	extractPublicApiScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-extract-public-api.py'
5	detectWorkspacesScript: '{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-workspaces.py'
6	---
7	
8	# Step 2: Analyze Target
9	
10	## STEP GOAL:
11	
12	To analyze the target repository by resolving its location, reading its structure, detecting the primary language, and listing top-level modules and exports — providing the user with a factual foundation for scoping decisions.
13	
14	## Rules
15	
16	- Focus only on analysis — do not define scope yet (Step 03)
17	- Do not make scoping decisions or recommendations
18	- Do not hallucinate or guess about repository contents
19	- All user-facing output in `{communication_language}`
20	
21	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
22	
23	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
24	
25	### 1. Resolve Target Location
26	
27	**For GitHub URLs:**
28	- Issue both probes in **one message with two parallel Bash calls** — they are independent:
29	  - `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` (verify repo exists)
30	  - `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/HEAD?recursive=1` (fetch file tree)
31	- If the repo-existence probe fails, fall through to the failure-class triage below; the tree response from the parallel call is discarded in that case.
32	
33	**Truncation detection:** After receiving the tree response, check the `truncated` field in the JSON output. If `truncated: true`:
34	- Display: "Note: GitHub API returned a truncated tree response ({count} items). Full analysis may require a local clone."
35	- Record in analysis summary: "Tree listing is partial — some files may not appear in the analysis."
36	- For very large repos (>1000 files in tree response): offer a recovery path instead of just warning. Interactive — present:
37	  ```
38	  Tree is truncated. How would you like to proceed?
39	    [L] Clone locally and re-analyze (slower but complete)
40	    [P] Proceed with the partial tree (faster, may miss exports under deeper paths)
41	  ```
42	  On `[L]`: shallow-clone (`git clone --depth 1 {url} {tmp_dir}`), restart this section against the local path, and remove `{tmp_dir}` after the analysis summary in §5. On `[P]` (or under headless): record `tree_truncated: true` in the analysis summary and continue without HALT.
43	
44	**On API failure (non-200 from `gh api`):**
45	
46	Distinguish the failure class before reporting:
47	- Auto-run `gh auth status` and capture its output. If it reports an unauthenticated state or expired token: HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"`) — "**Error:** GitHub CLI is not authenticated. `gh auth status` says: `{captured output}`. Run `gh auth login` and retry."
48	- If `gh auth status` reports authenticated but the call still failed (404/403): HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "target-inaccessible"`) — "**Error:** Cannot access repository at `{url}`. The CLI is authenticated but the API returned `{status}`. Check the URL and that the account has access to private repositories if applicable."
49	- If `gh auth status` itself fails to run (binary missing): HALT (exit code 3, `halt_reason: "gh-auth-failed"`) — "**Error:** `gh` CLI not found on PATH. Install it from <https://cli.github.com> and re-run."
50	
51	**For local paths:**
52	- Verify the directory exists
53	- List the directory tree
54	- If path doesn't exist: **HALT** — "**Error:** Directory not found at {path}. Verify the path is correct."
55	
56	Display: "**Resolving target...**"
57	
58	### 1b. Detect Monorepo / Workspace Layout
59	
60	Delegate workspace detection to `{detectWorkspacesScript}` instead of reasoning through manifest rules in prose. Build a payload from the tree fetched in §1 plus the small set of root manifests the detector needs, then invoke the script:
61	
62	```bash
63	echo '{"tree": [<flat list of repo-relative file paths>], "manifests": {"package.json": "<raw text>", "Cargo.toml": "<raw text>", "pnpm-workspace.yaml": "<raw text>", "lerna.json": "<raw text>"}}' | \
64	  uv run {detectWorkspacesScript}
65	```
66	
67	- **`tree`** — pass the flat list of repo-relative file paths already fetched in §1 (for GitHub: the `path` values from the `gh api .../git/trees/HEAD?recursive=1` response; for local: the equivalent listing).
68	- **`manifests`** — only the root manifests need contents; child-workspace manifests are looked up from the tree by the script. Include any of `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `lerna.json` that appears at the repo root. Fetch them in **one message with N parallel Bash calls** (`gh api .../contents/{path}` for GitHub, file reads for local), then base64-decode together. Per-workspace manifest contents (e.g. `packages/foo/package.json`) are optional — including them populates the workspace `name` field with the manifest's declared package name; omitting them falls back to the directory basename.
69	
70	The script returns a JSON envelope: `{is_monorepo, manifest_kind, workspaces[], warnings[]}`. Apply the result deterministically — see `src/shared/scripts/schemas/workspace-detection.v1.json` for the full contract.
71	
72	**If `is_monorepo: false`** — skip this section silently and continue to §2.
73	
74	**If `is_monorepo: true`** — present the discovered workspaces and prompt:
75	
76	```
77	This looks like a monorepo ({manifest_kind}) with these workspaces:
78	  1. {workspaces[0].name} ({workspaces[0].path})
79	  2. {workspaces[1].name} ({workspaces[1].path})
80	  ...
81	Which one should the skill cover? Pick a number, type 'all' to scope at the repo root, or 'list' to keep listing more.
82	```
83	
84	Interactive: wait for the user choice. On a numbered choice, store `monorepo_workspace: {path}` and rebase §2-§4b against that path. On `'all'`, leave `monorepo_workspace` unset and proceed at the repo root with a note in the analysis summary that scope is unfiltered.
85	
86	Headless: if the input contract supplied an `include` glob that begins with one of the workspace paths, auto-select that workspace (log `"headless: auto-selected workspace {name} from include glob"`). Otherwise default to repo root and log `"warn: monorepo detected ({manifest_kind}) but no workspace pre-selected — analyzing at repo root"`.
87	
88	Surface any non-empty `warnings[]` from the script to the operator log so a malformed root manifest is debuggable; the workflow does not HALT — falling back to repo-root analysis is always safe.
89	
90	### 2. Read Repository Structure
91	
92	List the top-level directory structure:
93	
94	"**Repository Structure:**
95	```
96	{repo-name}/
97	├── {top-level files}
98	├── {top-level directories}/
99	│   └── ...
100	└── ...
101	```
102	**Total:** {file count} files, {directory count} directories"
103	
104	### 3. Detect Primary Language
105	
106	Examine file extensions and configuration files to detect the primary language:
107	
108	**Detection signals (check in order):**
109	1. `package.json` → JavaScript/TypeScript
110	2. `tsconfig.json` → TypeScript
111	3. `Cargo.toml` → Rust
112	4. `pyproject.toml` or `setup.py` or `setup.cfg` → Python
113	5. `go.mod` → Go
114	6. `pom.xml` or `build.gradle` → Java
115	7. `*.csproj` or `*.sln` → C#
116	8. `Gemfile` → Ruby
117	9. File extension frequency analysis as fallback
118	
119	"**Detected language:** {language}
120	**Confidence:** {high/medium/low}
121	**Detection source:** {what config file or pattern confirmed it}"
122	
123	If confidence is low or ambiguous: flag for user override in step 03.
124	
125	### 4. List Top-Level Modules and Exports
126	
127	Identify the public API surface. **Delegate the parsing to `{extractPublicApiScript}` whenever the detected language is supported** — the script is the single source of truth for manifest parsing, export discovery, and version detection across the whole SKF pipeline. Hand-rolling these in prose creates drift seams the LLM cannot fully close.
128	
129	**Script-supported languages** (use the script): `js`, `ts`, `javascript`, `typescript`, `python`, `rust`, `go`, `java`, `kotlin`.
130	
131	This section runs exactly one of §4.1 (script path) or §4.2 (fallback path) based on the detected language, then always emits §4.3 (output format) and conditionally §4.4 (semantic signals).
132	
133	#### 4.1 Procedure — script-supported languages
134	
135	1. Read the relevant files into memory (no parsing yet — just collect content). For GitHub sources, issue **all N `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` calls in a single message with N parallel Bash calls** (one per manifest + each entry point), then base64-decode the responses together — these are 2-4 independent fetches per typical run. For local sources read directly (also parallelisable, but local reads are fast enough that serial Read tool calls are acceptable).
136	
137	   | Language | Manifest | Entry points (mode=quick) |
138	   |----------|----------|--------------------------|
139	   | js / ts / javascript / typescript | `package.json` (root, or primary workspace package per `references/version-resolution.md`) | `index.{ts,js}` and/or `src/index.{ts,js}` if present |
140	   | python | `pyproject.toml` (or `setup.py` / `setup.cfg` if no `pyproject.toml`) | top-level `__init__.py` of the package, plus `_version.py` if present |
141	   | rust | `Cargo.toml` (`[package]` — workspace root if `version = { workspace = true }`) | `src/lib.rs` |
142	   | go | `go.mod` | top-level `*.go` exporting the package surface |
143	   | java | `pom.xml` | (manifest alone is sufficient for the modules listing) |
144	   | kotlin | `build.gradle` / `build.gradle.kts` | (manifest alone) |
145	
146	2. Build a JSON payload matching the script contract:
147	
148	   ```json
149	   {
150	     "language": "<one of the supported values>",
151	     "manifest": {"path": "<relative path>", "content": "<file contents>"},
152	     "entries":  [{"path": "<relative path>", "content": "<file contents>"}, ...],
153	     "mode":     "quick"
154	   }
155	   ```
156	
157	3. Invoke the script and parse its JSON stdout:
158	
159	   ```bash
160	   echo '<payload-json>' | uv run {extractPublicApiScript} --language <lang> --mode quick
161	   ```
162	
163	   On a non-zero exit (codes 1 or 2 per the script's docstring), capture stderr, log it, and fall through to §4.2 (the prose-fallback path) — never HALT just because the script choked on an unusual manifest.
164	
165	4. Render the returned `package_name`, `exports` (each entry's `name`/`type`/`source_file`), `dependencies`, and any `warnings` to the user. The script also returns `version` — feed that into §4b instead of re-deriving.
166	
167	5. The script does not enumerate directories under `src/`. The LLM still lists those as "Top-Level Modules/Directories" so the user sees structural context (Maven and Gradle are the exception — for those, the script returns a `modules` array which IS the list).
168	
169	#### 4.2 Procedure — fallback (not script-supported)
170	
171	Languages outside the script coverage (Ruby / C# / Swift / etc.) take this path. The §4.1 fall-through on script error also lands here.
172	
173	Fall back to ad-hoc inspection — `Gemfile` / `*.csproj` / `*.sln` / `Package.swift` / file extension frequency. List top-level source directories as potential modules and note any obvious entry points. Flag the limitation in the analysis summary so the user knows scoping is on coarser signals.
174	
175	#### 4.3 Output format (both paths)
176	
177	"**Top-Level Modules/Directories:**
178	{numbered list of modules with brief description of each}
179	
180	**Detected Exports/Entry Points:**
181	{numbered list of public-facing items found — from script output when available, ad-hoc inspection otherwise}"
182	
183	#### 4.4 Semantic Signals (Forge+/Deep with ccc only)
184	
185	**Remote source guard:** If the target source was resolved via GitHub API (remote URL, not a local file path), skip this CCC subsection — CCC requires a local source index and cannot operate on remote-only sources. Note: "CCC semantic discovery skipped — target is remote. CCC discovery will run automatically during create-skill after the source is cloned."
186	
187	If `tools.ccc` is true in forge-tier.yaml, supplement the module listing with a semantic discovery pass:
188	
189	**CCC Semantic Discovery:**
190	- **Claude Code:** Use `/ccc search "{repo_name} public API exports modules" {source_path}`
191	- **Cursor:** Use `ccc` MCP server `search` tool with query `"{repo_name} public API exports modules"` and path `{source_path}`
192	- **CLI fallback:** `ccc search "{repo_name} public API exports modules" --path {source_path} --limit 10`
193	
194	See `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` for full bridge-to-tool mapping.
195	
196	If results are returned, display:
197	
198	"**Semantic Signals (ccc):**
199	{numbered list of file:snippet pairs from CCC results — top 5 most relevant}"
200	
201	This supplements — never replaces — the explicit module list above. CCC may surface non-obvious entry points (dynamically constructed exports, re-export chains) that static directory analysis misses.
202	
203	If CCC is unavailable or returns no results: skip this subsection silently.
204	
205	### 4b. Detect Source Version
206	
207	**When the language was script-supported (§4 took the script path):** the `version` field returned by `{extractPublicApiScript}` IS the detected version — do not re-derive it and do not load `{versionResolutionFile}`. The script already implements the language-specific lookups documented in that reference, so loading the reference here only burns context.
208	
209	**When the language was not script-supported:** load `{versionResolutionFile}` and follow the prose Detection Algorithm directly (Ruby / C# / Swift / etc. fall outside the script's coverage).
210	
211	Surface the result regardless of which path produced it:
212	
213	**If `target_version` was provided in step 01:**
214	- Display: "**Target version:** {target_version} (user-specified)"
215	
216	Display: "**Detected version:** {version or 'Not detected — will default to 1.0.0'}"
217	
218	{If target_version was provided AND auto-detected version differs:}
219	"**Note:** Detected version ({detected_version}) differs from your target version ({target_version}). Using target version (per `references/version-resolution.md` precedence rules)."
220	
221	If detection fails or returns a non-semver value: note that version will default to `"1.0.0"` and the user can override in step 04. The actual write happens in step 05.
222	
223	### 5. Report Analysis Summary
224	
225	Present the complete analysis:
226	
227	"**Analysis Complete**
228	
229	---
230	
231	**Target:** {repo URL or path}
232	**Language:** {detected language} ({confidence})
233	**Structure:** {file count} files across {directory count} directories
234	
235	**Key Modules ({count}):**
236	{bulleted list of modules}
237	
238	**Public Exports/Entry Points ({count}):**
239	{bulleted list of exports}
240	
241	**Notable Files:**
242	- README: {found/not found}
243	- Tests: {found/not found — location}
244	- Docs: {found/not found — location}
245	- Config: {list of config files found}
246	- Version: {detected version or "Not detected — defaulting to 1.0.0"}
247	
248	---
249	
250	{If language confidence is low:}
251	**Note:** Language detection confidence is low. You'll be able to override this in the next step.
252	
253	Moving to scope definition where you'll choose what to include and exclude."
254	
255	### 6. Auto-Proceed to Scope Definition
256	
257	Display: "**Proceeding to scope definition...**
258	
259	Review the analysis above. If anything looks wrong, let me know now — otherwise I'll proceed to scope definition."
260	
261	Pause briefly for user input. If the user provides corrections or asks questions, address them and re-present any updated analysis findings. Then proceed.
262	
263	#### Menu Handling Logic:
264	
265	- After analysis report is presented to user and any corrections addressed, load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
266	
267	#### EXECUTION RULES:
268	
269	- This is a soft auto-proceed step — present the pause prompt, wait briefly for user input
270	- If user provides corrections: address them, then proceed
271	- If no user input after a brief pause: proceed directly to step 03
272	
273	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
274	
275	ONLY WHEN the analysis is complete and the summary has been presented to the user will you load and read fully `./step-03-scope-definition.md` to begin scope definition.
276	
277	
```

75	
76	**Scope guidance for first-time users:** A well-scoped skill covers one cohesive capability with 3-8 primary functions. If the scope includes unrelated concerns (e.g., authentication AND data visualization), suggest splitting into separate briefs. If the scope is too narrow (single utility function), suggest expanding to the surrounding capability surface.
77	
78	### 2c. Offer Scope Templates
79	
80	Load `{scopeTemplatesFile}` for the scope type options ([F], [M], [P], [C], [R]) and their descriptions.
81	
82	**Recommend a scope type — don't present the five options as equal weight.** SKILL.md states this workflow "steers toward the smaller, sharper version when scope is unclear" — surface that opinion at decision time. Use the analysis from step-02 and the user's intent from step-01 to pick the best-fit recommendation, then present the menu with that option marked as the suggested default.
83	
84	Heuristics for the recommendation (apply in order, pick the first that matches):
85	
86	- **Component registry detected** (file matching `registry.ts` / `components.ts` with 10+ entries, or `Component[]` type annotation) → **[C] Component Library**
87	- **User intent mentions "wiring", "integration example", "starter", "lifecycle", "build config"** OR the analysis identified the source as an example/demo app → **[R] Reference App**
88	- **Intent names specific module(s)** (e.g. "just the auth module", "only the streaming part") OR the analysis surfaced a large library (≥6 top-level modules with weakly-related concerns) → **[M] Specific Modules**
89	- **Library has a clear, narrow public API** (≤8 named exports from the manifest, intent points at "the API" / "the SDK") → **[P] Public API Only**
90	- **Otherwise** (small focused library, intent says "everything", or no strong signal either way) → **[F] Full Library**
91	
92	Present:
93	
94	"**Recommended scope type: [{letter}] {Name}** — {one-sentence rationale tying the recommendation to a specific signal from step-01/02, e.g. 'because the analysis found a 47-entry component registry under src/components/registry.ts'}.
95	
96	How broadly should this skill cover the library?
97	
98	{full menu from `{scopeTemplatesFile}` with the recommended letter marked, e.g. '[F] Full Library', '[M] Specific Modules', '[P] Public API Only ← recommended', '[C] Component Library', '[R] Reference App'}
99	
100	Press Enter to accept the recommendation, or pick a different letter."
101	
102	Wait for user selection. Empty input or just Enter accepts the recommendation; any of the five letters overrides.
103	
104	**Headless:** if `scope_type` was supplied, use it (consumed at the GATE in §6). If not supplied, run the same five heuristics above against the data already gathered (intent text + scope_hint from step-01, module count + exports + file paths from step-02) and use the first matching recommendation as the scope_type. Log `"headless: scope_type auto-selected as {value} from {heuristic-name}"` so the choice is debuggable.
105	
106	For the **component-registry** heuristic, the entry-count threshold (`10+ entries`) and `Component[]` type-annotation check require file *contents* — those are only available when step-02 §4.1 included `registry.ts` / `components.ts` as an entry point in the script payload (rare for the public-API mode), or when the target is a local path. In headless mode without that visibility, treat **the presence of `registry.ts` or `components.ts` anywhere in the file tree** as a sufficient match for the component-library recommendation — content inspection is interactive-only.
107	
108	The `docs-only` short-circuit still applies when `source_type=docs-only` (heuristic ranking is skipped — there is no source surface to scope). When no heuristic matches, fall back to `full-library` and log `"headless: scope_type defaulted to full-library — no signal matched"`.
109

111	
112	Using the boundary definitions from `{scopeTemplatesFile}`, present the appropriate flow for the user's selected scope type ([F], [M], [P], [C], or [R]). Follow each type's prompts and wait for user input at each phase before proceeding.

115	
116	{If language detection confidence was low from step 02:}
117	
118	"**Language confirmation needed.**

145	### 5b. Scripts & Assets Intent (Optional)
146	
147	**Only ask when `scope.type` is `full-library`, `specific-modules`, `component-library`, or `reference-app` (skip for `public-api` and `docs-only`). Reference apps routinely ship wiring scripts and build-config assets — prompt for them.**
148	
149	"Does this library include executable scripts (CLI tools, validation scripts, setup helpers) or static assets (config templates, JSON schemas, example configs) that should be packaged with the skill?"
150	
151	- **[D] Auto-detect** from source (default) — SKF will scan for `scripts/`, `bin/`, `assets/`, `templates/`, `schemas/` directories
152	- **[N] None expected** — skip script/asset detection
153	- Or describe what you expect (free text)
154	
155	Record the response as `scripts_intent` and `assets_intent` in the brief. Default to `detect` if user does not respond or skips.
156	
157	### 6. Present MENU OPTIONS
158	
159	Display: **Select an Option:** [A] Advanced Elicitation [P] Party Mode [C] Continue to Brief Confirmation [X] Cancel and exit
160	
161	#### Menu Handling Logic:
162	
163	- IF A: Invoke {advancedElicitationSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
164	- IF P: Invoke {partyModeSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu
165	- IF C: Load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
166	- IF X: Treat as user-cancellation. Display `"Cancelled — no brief was written."` and HALT (exit code 6, `halt_reason: "user-cancelled"`). Cancellation here is non-destructive — no files have been written yet. `[X]` is interactive-only; the headless GATE never reaches this branch.
167	- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond then [Redisplay Menu Options](#6-present-menu-options)
168	
169	#### EXECUTION RULES:
170	
171	- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
172	- **GATE [default: C]** — If `{headless_mode}`: consume the headless inputs from step-01 in priority order:
173	  - If `scope_type` was supplied, use it (must match one of the six valid types) and skip the §2c template menu.
174	  - Otherwise auto-select: `source_type=docs-only` → `scope.type: "docs-only"`; `source_type=source` → run the §2c heuristic-driven recommendation (component-registry / reference-app keywords / specific-modules / public-api / full-library) against the step-01/02 signals and use the first match. Falls back to `full-library` only when no heuristic matches.
175	  - If `include`/`exclude` were supplied, use them verbatim (split on comma) instead of running the boundary prompts in §3.
176	  - If `scripts_intent`/`assets_intent` were supplied, record them and skip §5b; otherwise default to `detect`.
177	  - Log: `"headless: scope_type={value} include={n} exclude={n} scripts_intent={value} assets_intent={value}"`.
178	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
179	- After other menu items execution, return to this menu
180	- User can chat or ask questions — always respond and then redisplay menu
181	
182	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
183	
184	ONLY WHEN C is selected and scope boundaries are confirmed will you load and read fully `./step-04-confirm-brief.md` to present the complete brief for confirmation.

# /// script
# requires-python = ">=3.9"
# dependencies = []
# ///
"""SKF Validate Brief Inputs — pre-pass validation for brief-skill headless invocation.

Validates and normalizes the headless argument set passed to skf-brief-skill
before the workflow's interactive sequence runs. Catches malformed inputs at
point of capture instead of letting them surface 5+ minutes later in step-02
or step-05.

CLI:
  uv run skf-validate-brief-inputs.py --json '{...}'
  echo '{...}' | uv run skf-validate-brief-inputs.py

Input (JSON object on stdin or via --json):
  Required:
    target_repo  — string (URL or path); error if absent
    skill_name   — string (kebab-case); error if absent or malformed

  Optional with enum constraints:
    source_type      — "source" | "docs-only" (default "source")
    source_authority — "official" | "community" | "internal" (default "community")
    scope_type       — "full-library" | "specific-modules" | "public-api"
                       | "component-library" | "reference-app" | "docs-only"
    scripts_intent   — "detect" | "none" | free-text (default "detect")
    assets_intent    — "detect" | "none" | free-text (default "detect")

  Optional with format constraints:
    target_version — loose semver string (matches 1.2.3, 1.2.3-rc.1, 1.2.3+build.5)

  Conditional:
    doc_urls — required when source_type == "docs-only"

  Free-text / pass-through:
    scope_hint, language_hint, intent, include, exclude, force

  Unrecognized keys are passed through unchanged but flagged as warnings.

Output (JSON on stdout):
  {
    "valid": bool,
    "errors":   [{"field": "...", "message": "..."}, ...],
    "warnings": [{"field": "...", "message": "..."}, ...],
    "normalized": { ...input dict with defaults applied... },
    "halt_reason": "input-missing" | "input-invalid" | null
  }

Exit codes:
  0 — valid (errors empty)
  1 — invalid (errors present); halt_reason is set
  2 — internal error (bad JSON input, IO failure)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import re
import sys
from typing import Any

KNOWN_FIELDS = {
    "target_repo",
    "skill_name",
    "source_type",
    "source_authority",
    "scope_type",
    "target_version",
    "doc_urls",
    "scope_hint",
    "language_hint",
    "intent",
    "scripts_intent",
    "assets_intent",
    "include",
    "exclude",
    "force",
}
# `preset` is intentionally NOT in KNOWN_FIELDS — it is consumed at the step-01 §8 GATE
```

'{...}'\n\nInput (JSON object on stdin or via --json):\n  intent          — string (combined intent + scope_hint), default \"\"\n  module_count    — integer (top-level modules from step-02), default 0\n  export_count    — integer (named exports from manifest), default 0\n  tree            — list of repo-relative file paths, default []\n  entry_files     — optional [{path, content}], default null\n  source_type     — \"source\" | \"docs-only\" | null, default null\n  mode            — \"interactive\" | \"headless\", default \"headless\"\n\nOutput (JSON on stdout):\n  scope_type        — one of full-library | specific-modules | public-api\n                      | component-library | reference-app | docs-only\n  matched_heuristic — \"component-registry\" | \"reference-app-keywords\"\n                      | \"specific-modules-naming\" | \"specific-modules-count\"\n                      | \"narrow-public-api\" | \"default-full-library\"\n                      | \"docs-only-shortcircuit\"\n  signals           — object naming the signals that fired (path, count, etc.)\n  rationale         — one-sentence explanation referencing the signals\n\nExit codes:\n  0 — recommendation produced\n  2 — internal error (bad JSON input, IO failure)\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport re\nimport sys\nfrom typing import Any\n\nREFERENCE_APP_KEYWORDS = (\n    \"wiring\",\n    \"integration example\",\n    \"integration sample\",\n    \"starter\",\n    \"lifecycle\",\n    \"build config\",\n    \"build-config\",\n    \"demo app\",\n    \"example app\",\n    \"reference app\",\n)\n\nNARROW_PUBLIC_API_KEYWORDS = (\n    \"the api\",\n    \"the sdk\",\n    \"public api\",\n    \"client library\",\n    \"client sdk\",\n)\n\n# Phrases that indicate the user wants only a specific subset of modules\n# rather than the whole library. The match is substring + lowercase, so\n# \"just the auth module\" / \"only the streaming part\" / \"specifically the\n# parser\" all match.\nSPECIFIC_MODULE_NAMING_PATTERNS = (\n    re.compile(r\"\\bjust the\\s+\\w+\", re.IGNORECASE),\n    re.compile(r\"\\bonly the\\s+\\w+\", re.IGNORECASE),\n    re.compile(r\"\\bspecifically the\\s+\\w+\", re.IGNORECASE),\n    re.compile(r\"\\bjust\\s+\\w+\\s+module\\b\", re.IGNORECASE),\n    re.compile(r\"\\bonly\\s+\\w+\\s+module\\b\", re.IGNORECASE),\n)\n\nVALID_MODES = {\"interactive\", \"headless\"}\nVALID_SOURCE_TYPES = {None, \"source\", \"docs-only\"}\n\n\ndef _die(message: str, code: int = 2) -> None:\n    sys.stderr.write(f\"skf-recommend-scope-type: {message}\\n\")\n    sys.exit(code)\n\n\ndef _find_registry_files(tree: list[str]) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Return repo-relative paths matching registry.ts / components.ts (any depth).\"\"\"\n    hits: list[str] = []\n    for path in tree:\n        if not isinstance(path, str):\n            continue\n        basename = path.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[-1]\n        if basename in {\"registry.ts\", \"components.ts\", \"registry.tsx\", \"components.tsx\"}:\n            hits.append(path)\n    return hits\n\n\ndef _registry_entry_count(content: str) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Approximate the number of entries in a registry array literal.\n\n    Counts top-level `{ ... }` objects within the first array-like body\n    we encounter. Approximate by design — used only as a >=10 threshold.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Strip line comments to reduce noise; do not bother with block comments.\n    cleaned = re.sub(r\"//.*\", \"\", content)\n    # Find array literals — match `[` followed by whitespace then `{`.\n    array_match = re.search(r\"\\[\\s*\\{\", cleaned)\n    if not array_match:\n        return 0\n    body_start = array_match.start()\n    # Walk forward, count balanced top-level `{` matches up to the closing `]`.\n    depth = 0\n    in_string: str | None = None\n    count = 0\n    for ch in cleaned[body_start:]:\n        if in_string:\n            if ch == in_string:\n                in_string = None\n            continue\n        if ch in (\"'\", '\"', \"`\"):\n            in_string = ch\n            continue\n        if ch == \"{\":\n            if depth == 0:\n                count += 1\n            depth += 1\n        elif ch == \"}\":\n            depth = max(depth - 1, 0)\n        elif ch == \"]\" and depth == 0:\n            break\n    return count\n\n\ndef _has_component_array_annotation(content: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Detect a `Component[]` type annotation, allowing trivial whitespace and generics.\"\"\"\n    return bool(re.search(r\"\\bComponent(?:\\s*<[^>]*>)?\\s*\\[\\s*\\]\", content))\n\n\ndef _component_registry_match(\n    tree: list[str],\n    entry_files: list[dict] | None,\n    mode: str,\n) -> dict | None:\n    \"\"\"Apply rule 1 — component-library.\n\n    Returns a signals dict on match, or None.\n    \"\"\"\n    registry_paths = _find_registry_files(tree)\n    if not registry_paths:\n        return None\n\n    # Index entry-file contents by path for O(1) lookup\n    contents: dict[str, str] = {}\n    if entry_files:\n        for ef in entry_files:\n            if not isinstance(ef, dict):\n                continue\n            path = ef.get(\"path\")\n            content = ef.get(\"content\")\n            if isinstance(path, str) and isinstance(content, str):\n                contents[path] = content\n\n    # When we have contents for any registry file, do the deep check\n    for path in registry_paths:\n        if path in contents:\n            entry_count = _registry_entry_count(contents[path])\n            has_annotation = _has_component_array_annotation(contents[path])\n            if entry_count >= 10 or has_annotation:\n                return {\n                    \"registry_path\": path,\n                    \"entry_count\": entry_count if entry_count >= 10 else None,\n                    \"component_array_annotation\": has_annotation,\n                    \"contents_inspected\": True,\n                }\n            # File present but neither threshold met — content disqualifies\n            continue\n\n    # No contents available for any registry file\n    if mode == \"headless\":\n        return {\n            \"registry_path\": registry_paths[0],\n            \"entry_count\": None,\n            \"component_array_annotation\": False,\n            \"contents_inspected\": False,\n        }\n    # Interactive without contents — fall through; the rule does not match\n    return None\n\n\ndef _reference_app_match(intent_lower: str) -> dict | None:\n    matches = [kw for kw in REFERENCE_APP_KEYWORDS if kw in intent_lower]\n    if matches:\n        return {\"keywords\": matches}\n    return None\n\n\ndef _specific_modules_match(intent: str, module_count: int) -> tuple[str, dict] | None:\n    naming_hits: list[str] = []\n    for pattern in SPECIFIC_MODULE_NAMING_PATTERNS:\n        m = pattern.search(intent)\n        if m:\n            naming_hits.append(m.group(0).strip())\n    if naming_hits:\n        return (\"specific-modules-naming\", {\"phrases\": naming_hits})\n    if module_count >= 6:\n        return (\"specific-modules-count\", {\"module_count\": module_count})\n    return None\n\n\ndef _narrow_public_api_match(intent_lower: str, export_count: int) -> dict | None:\n    keyword_hits = [kw for kw in NARROW_PUBLIC_API_KEYWORDS if kw in intent_lower]\n    if keyword_hits and 0 < export_count <= 8:\n        return {\"keywords\": keyword_hits, \"export_count\": export_count}\n    return None\n\n\ndef recommend(payload: dict) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Apply the five-rule ladder. Always returns a recommendation.\"\"\"\n    intent = (payload.get(\"intent\") or \"\").strip()\n    intent_lower = intent.lower()\n    module_count = int(payload.get(\"module_count\") or 0)\n    export_count = int(payload.get(\"export_count\") or 0)\n    tree = payload.get(\"tree\") or []\n    entry_files = payload.get(\"entry_files\")\n    source_type = payload.get(\"source_type\")\n    mode = payload.get(\"mode\") or \"headless\"\n\n    if source_type not in VALID_SOURCE_TYPES:\n        _die(f\"source_type must be one of {sorted(t for t in VALID_SOURCE_TYPES if t)} or null; got {source_type!r}\")\n    if mode not in VALID_MODES:\n        _die(f\"mode must be one of {sorted(VALID_MODES)}; got {mode!r}\")\n\n    # Short-circuit: docs-only has no source surface to scope\n    if source_type == \"docs-only\":\n        return {\n            \"scope_type\": \"docs-only\",\n            \"matched_heuristic\": \"docs-only-shortcircuit\",\n            \"signals\": {\"source_type\": \"docs-only\"},\n            \"rationale\": \"source_type is docs-only — there is no source surface to scope, so the brief uses the docs-only template.\",\n        }\n\n    # Rule 1 — component-library\n    cr = _component_registry_match(tree, entry_files, mode)\n    if cr:\n        rationale_bits = [f\"a component registry was detected at {cr['registry_path']}\"]\n        if cr.get(\"entry_count\"):\n            rationale_bits.append(f\"with {cr['entry_count']} entries\")\n        if cr.get(\"component_array_annotation\"):\n            rationale_bits.append(\"and a Component[] type annotation\")\n        if not cr.get(\"contents_inspected\"):\n            rationale_bits.append(\"(presence-only match — file contents not inspected in headless mode)\")\n        return {\n            \"scope_type\": \"component-library\",\n            \"matched_heuristic\": \"component-registry\",\n            \"signals\": cr,\n            \"rationale\": \"Component Library because \" + \" \".join(rationale_bits) + \".\",\n        }\n\n    # Rule 2 — reference-app\n    ra = _reference_app_match(intent_lower)\n    if ra:\n        kw_str = \", \".join(f\"'{k}'\" for k in ra[\"keywords\"])\n        return {\n            \"scope_type\": \"reference-app\",\n            \"matched_heuristic\": \"reference-app-keywords\",\n            \"signals\": ra,\n            \"rationale\": f\"Reference App because the intent mentions {kw_str} — language consistent with a wiring-pattern skill rather than a library API.\",\n        }\n\n    # Rule 3 — specific-modules\n    sm = _specific_modules_match(intent, module_count)\n    if sm:\n        heuristic, signals = sm\n        if heuristic == \"specific-modules-naming\":\n            phrases = \", \".join(f\"'{p}'\" for p in signals[\"phrases\"])\n            rationale = f\"Specific Modules because the intent names a subset ({phrases}).\"\n        else:\n            rationale = f\"Specific Modules because the analysis surfaced {signals['module_count']} top-level modules — likely too many for a single cohesive scope.\"\n        return {\n            \"scope_type\": \"specific-modules\",\n            \"matched_heuristic\": heuristic,\n            \"signals\": signals,\n            \"rationale\": rationale,\n        }\n\n    # Rule 4 — narrow public API\n    pa = _narrow_public_api_match(intent_lower, export_count)\n    if pa:\n        kw_str = \", \".join(f\"'{k}'\" for k in pa[\"keywords\"])\n        return {\n            \"scope_type\": \"public-api\",\n            \"matched_heuristic\": \"narrow-public-api\",\n            \"signals\": pa,\n            \"rationale\": f\"Public API Only because the intent mentions {kw_str} and the manifest exposes {pa['export_count']} named exports — a clear narrow public surface.\",\n        }\n\n    # Rule 5 — fallback\n    return {\n        \"scope_type\": \"full-library\",\n        \"matched_heuristic\": \"default-full-library\",\n        \"signals\": {\n            \"module_count\": module_count,\n            \"export_count\": export_count,\n        },\n        \"rationale\": \"Full Library — no signal matched a narrower scope, so the default is to cover everything.\",\n    }\n\n\ndef _parse_argv(argv: list[str]) -> dict:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description=\"Recommend a scope type by applying the documented 5-rule heuristic ladder.\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--json\",\n        help=\"JSON payload (alternative to stdin)\",\n    )\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n    if args.json is not None:\n        raw = args.json\n    else:\n        raw = sys.stdin.read()\n    if not raw or not raw.strip():\n        _die(\"empty input (expected JSON payload on stdin or via --json)\")\n    try:\n        payload = json.loads(raw)\n    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n        _die(f\"invalid JSON input: {e}\")\n    if not isinstance(payload, dict):\n        _die(\"payload must be a JSON object\")\n    return payload\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str]) -> int:\n    payload = _parse_argv(argv)\n    result = recommend(payload)\n    json.dump(result, sys.stdout, ensure_ascii=False)\n    sys.stdout.write(\"\\n\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))\n"
}

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Tests for skf-detect-workspaces.py.

The detector is pure — it parses a payload (tree + manifests) and returns
a result envelope. Tests build payloads inline and call detect() directly,
plus a few subprocess-level CLI tests for stdin/argv/exit-code wiring.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import importlib.util
import json
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

SCRIPT_PATH = (
    Path(__file__).parent.parent
    / "src"
    / "shared"
    / "scripts"
    / "skf-detect-workspaces.py"
)

fire, reference-app wins (rule 2 before rule 3).\"\"\"\n    result = mod.recommend(\n        {\n            \"intent\": \"starter wiring with just the auth module\",\n            \"tree\": [],\n            \"module_count\": 99,\n            \"mode\": \"interactive\",\n        }\n    )\n    assert result[\"scope_type\"] == \"reference-app\"\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Validation\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_invalid_source_type_dies():\n    with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as exc_info:\n        mod.recommend({\"source_type\": \"weird\", \"tree\": [], \"mode\": \"interactive\"})\n    assert exc_info.value.code == 2\n\n\ndef test_invalid_mode_dies():\n    with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as exc_info:\n        mod.recommend({\"tree\": [], \"mode\": \"background\"})\n    assert exc_info.value.code == 2\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# CLI wiring\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_cli_stdin_payload_round_trip():\n    payload = {\n        \"intent\": \"starter integration example\",\n        \"tree\": [],\n        \"mode\": \"headless\",\n    }\n    proc = subprocess.run(\n        [sys.executable, str(SCRIPT_PATH)],\n        input=json.dumps(payload),\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        timeout=10,\n    )\n    assert proc.returncode == 0, proc.stderr\n    out = json.loads(proc.stdout)\n    assert out[\"scope_type\"] == \"reference-app\"\n\n\ndef test_cli_json_arg():\n    payload = {\"source_type\": \"docs-only\", \"tree\": [], \"mode\": \"headless\"}\n    proc = subprocess.run(\n        [sys.executable, str(SCRIPT_PATH), \"--json\", json.dumps(payload)],\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        timeout=10,\n    )\n    assert proc.returncode == 0\n    out = json.loads(proc.stdout)\n    assert out[\"scope_type\"] == \"docs-only\"\n\n\ndef test_cli_empty_stdin_dies_with_2():\n    proc = subprocess.run(\n        [sys.executable, str(SCRIPT_PATH)],\n        input=\"\",\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        timeout=10,\n    )\n    assert proc.returncode == 2\n    assert \"empty input\" in proc.stderr\n\n\ndef test_cli_invalid_json_dies_with_2():\n    proc = subprocess.run(\n        [sys.executable, str(SCRIPT_PATH)],\n        input=\"{not valid\",\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        timeout=10,\n    )\n    assert proc.returncode == 2\n    assert \"invalid JSON\" in proc.stderr\n"

Empty input or just Enter accepts the recommendation; any of the five letters overrides.\n\n**Headless:** if `scope_type` was supplied, use it (consumed at the GATE in §6). If not supplied, run the same five heuristics above against the data already gathered (intent text + scope_hint from step-01, module count + exports + file paths from step-02) and use the first matching recommendation as the scope_type. Log `\"headless: scope_type auto-selected as {value} from {heuristic-name}\"` so the choice is debuggable.\n\nFor the **component-registry** heuristic, the entry-count threshold (`10+ entries`) and `Component[]` type-annotation check require file *contents* — those are only available when step-02 §4.1 included `registry.ts` / `components.ts` as an entry point in the script payload (rare for the public-API mode), or when the target is a local path. In headless mode without that visibility, treat **the presence of `registry.ts` or `components.ts` anywhere in the file tree** as a sufficient match for the component-library recommendation — content inspection is interactive-only.\n\nThe `docs-only` short-circuit still applies when `source_type=docs-only` (heuristic ranking is skipped — there is no source surface to scope). ## Critical Issues

None found. No rule from the original prose ladder was dropped or inverted.

---

## Important Issues

### 1. `test-skf-recommend-scope-type.py` not registered in `test:python` — 27 tests never run in CI

**Confidence: 97**

`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/package.json` line 56 lists 21 explicit test file paths in `test:python`. `test/test-skf-recommend-scope-type.py` is not among them. Every prior shared-script test suite (`test-skf-detect-workspaces.py`, `test-skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`, `test-skf-emit-brief-result-envelope.py`, etc.) appears in that list. The omission means all 27 tests — including the CLI round-trip tests and the precedence tests — will never be executed by `npm test`, `npm run quality`, or CI.

Fix: add `test/test-skf-recommend-scope-type.py` to the space-separated list in `test:python` between `test-skf-detect-workspaces.py` and `-v`.

---

### 2. Interactive path cannot detect component-library when `registry.ts` was not fetched in step-02

**Confidence: 85**

The step-03 §2c payload note says "supply the contents of any `registry.ts` / `components.ts` **already fetched in step-02** … omit otherwise." The step-02 §4.1 entry-point table for `js/ts` lists only `package.json` + `index.{ts,js}` / `src/index.{ts,js}`. `registry.ts` and `components.ts` are not in that table and will not be in memory after step-02.

Consequence: in interactive mode, `entry_files` for the component-registry rule will almost always be empty. With `mode: "interactive"`, `_component_registry_match` at `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-recommend-scope-type.py` line 206–214 returns `None` when no file contents are available — it deliberately does not fall back to presence-only. The **headless** path with `mode: "headless"` correctly fires on presence alone (lines 206–212). The result: a component-library repo visited interactively classifies as whatever the next lower rule produces, while the same repo in headless mode correctly classifies as `component-library`.

The PR description acknowledges the distinction ("interactive mode for 10+ entries / `Component[]` annotation; presence-only fallback in headless when no entry_files supplied") but the workflow file does not instruct the LLM to fetch registry files proactively at the step-03 §2c invocation site.

Fix option A (minimal): in step-03 §2c, before building the payload, add: "Fetch the contents of any `registry.ts` / `components.ts` paths visible in the tree — issue all N fetches in one parallel batch." This gives the interactive path the same information headless infers from presence.

Fix option B (alternative): change the interactive fallback to also use presence-only when no contents are available, consistent with the headless path. This would require updating the script and removing the interactive/headless distinction in `_component_registry_match`, but it's a one-line change at line 213 and eliminates the asymmetry entirely.

---

## Findings That Were Investigated and Cleared

**Focus area 2 — `//` comment strip corrupting URL strings in `_registry_entry_count`:** The concern is real but self-contained. For a multi-line registry (the universal real-world layout), the `{` and `}` that form entry boundaries sit on their own lines, unaffected by URL-line stripping. The walker misparsed the truncated string literal but still correctly counts depth transitions because the braces that matter are on separate lines. For a single-line-per-entry layout, the brace after the URL would be lost — but the function is explicitly "Approximate by design" (docstring, line 129), the threshold is >=10, and the `Component[]` annotation path is the primary guard for small registries. Below 80 confidence for a real-world failure.

**Focus area 3 — `"ButtonComponent[]"` false match:** `r"\bComponent(?:\s*<[^>]*>)?\s*\[\s*\]"` — the `\b` asserts a word boundary. `ButtonComponent` has a word character (`n`) immediately before `C`, so no boundary exists and the regex does not match. Correct.

**Focus area 1 — behavior preservation of all five rules:**
- Component-registry: four basenames covered (lines 120–121), headless presence-only confirmed (lines 206–212), interactive content-inspection confirmed (lines 192–202). ✓
- Reference-app: "wiring", "integration example", "starter", "lifecycle", "build config", "build-config" all present, plus added "demo app", "example app", "reference app", "integration sample". The additions are strictly synonymous and do not widen the rule to catch false positives — "demo app", "example app", and "reference app" are explicit signal phrases; "integration sample" is a synonym for the existing "integration example". Safe. ✓
- Specific-modules: `\bjust the\s+\w+`, `\bonly the\s+\w+` cover the prose examples; `module_count >= 6` preserved. ✓
- Narrow-public-api: <=8 exports with keyword, `0 < export_count` correctly excludes zero (line 239). Added "client library", "client sdk", "public api" are natural synonyms. Safe. ✓
- Default fallback unchanged. ✓

**Focus area 6 — include/exclude order in headless §6 GATE:** Step-03 §6 GATE (lines 179–184) applies scope_type first, then applies `include`/`exclude` verbatim if supplied. Order is preserved. ✓

**Focus area 7 — JSON schema:** The script follows the lighter pattern (no schema file) established by `skf-validate-brief-inputs.py`. The `skf-detect-workspaces.py` pattern (with schema) was chosen because its contract is consumed programmatically by step-02 §1b. This script's output is consumed by the LLM directly, so schema-less is consistent. ✓

**Focus area 8 — exit code 2 for empty stdin and bad JSON:** Lines 349 and 354 both call `_die(...)` which exits with code 2. Tests `test_cli_empty_stdin_dies_with_2` and `test_cli_invalid_json_dies_with_2` verify both cases. ✓

---

## Summary

Two findings meet the >=80 threshold:

1. **The test file is not wired into CI** (`test:python` in `package.json`) — this is a definitive gap; the 27 tests are silently skipped on every CI run.

2. **Interactive mode will almost never have `entry_files` for the component-registry rule**, producing a systematic divergence from the headless path for component-library repos. The fix is to either instruct the LLM to fetch registry files proactively at the §2c invocation site in `step-03-scope-definition.md`, or to make the interactive path also fall back to presence-only when no contents are available (a one-line change in `skf-recommend-scope-type.py` at line 213).

  "new_string": "**Fetch registry-file contents before building the payload.** Step-02 §4.1 fetches `package.json` plus the entry-point files but does not fetch `registry.ts` / `components.ts` — the deep-match branch of the component-registry rule needs those contents. For each match, fetch its contents in **one message with N parallel Bash calls** (`gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` for GitHub, file reads for local), then base64-decode the responses together. Skip the fetch if the tree contains no registry files.\n\nBuild the payload and invoke:\n\n```bash\necho '{\n  \"intent\": \"<combined intent + scope_hint text from step-01>\",\n  \"module_count\": <count from step-02 §4.3>,\n  \"export_count\": <count from step-02 §4.3>,\n  \"tree\": [<flat list of repo-relative file paths from step-02 §1>],\n  \"entry_files\": [{\"path\": \"<registry path>\", \"content\": \"<contents>\"}, ...],\n  \"source_type\": \"source\",\n  \"mode\": \"interactive\"\n}' | uv run {recommendScopeTypeScript}\n```\n\n`entry_files` carries the registry contents fetched above; omit when no registry files exist in the tree. `mode: \"interactive\"` activates the content-inspection branch of the component-registry rule (10+ entries or `Component[]` annotation); the headless GATE in §6 uses `mode: \"headless\"` which falls back to presence-only matching. `source_type: \"docs-only\"` short-circuits to `docs-only` regardless of the other signals."

1	# /// script
2	# requires-python = ">=3.10"
3	# dependencies = ["pyyaml"]
4	# ///
5	"""SKF Write Skill Brief — Schema-validated atomic writer for skill-brief.yaml.
6	
7	Replaces the prose-driven YAML emission, version-precedence resolution,
8	conditional optional-field rendering, and non-atomic file write currently
9	inlined in `src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md` §3-§4.
10	
11	Each of those operations is purely deterministic: there is no LLM
12	judgement required to render the YAML, decide which optional fields
13	appear, resolve target_version vs. detected vs. default, or write the
14	file atomically. Keeping that work in prose creates four schema-drift
15	seams (key order, YAML formatting, conditional field inclusion rules,
16	atomic-write behaviour) that the LLM cannot fully close on every
17	invocation. This script is the single source of truth.
18	
19	Subcommand:
20	
21	  write   Read brief context as JSON on stdin, validate against
22	          src/shared/scripts/schemas/skill-brief.v1.json, apply
23	          version-precedence rules, render the canonical YAML, and
24	          atomically write to --target. Emits a JSON success envelope
25	          on stdout.
26	
27	Context payload shape (consumed by `write`):
28	
29	  {
30	    "name":             "marked",
31	    "version_resolved": "1.2.3",   # OR omit and provide:
32	    "target_version":   "1.2.3" | null,
33	    "detected_version": "1.2.3" | null,
34	
35	    "source_type":      "source" | "docs-only",
36	    "source_repo":      "https://github.com/...",
37	    "language":         "javascript",
38	    "description":      "...",
39	    "forge_tier":       "Quick" | "Forge" | "Forge+" | "Deep",
40	    "created":          "2026-05-02",     # ISO date
41	    "created_by":       "armel",
42	
43	    "scope": {
44	      "type":    "full-library" | ...,
45	      "include": ["src/**/*.ts"],
46	      "exclude": ["**/*.test.*"],
47	      "notes":   ""
48	    },
49	
50	    # Conditionally present:
51	    "doc_urls":         [{"url": "...", "label": "..."}],
52	    "scripts_intent":   "detect" | "none" | free-text,
53	    "assets_intent":    "detect" | "none" | free-text,
54	    "source_authority": "official" | "community" | "internal"
55	  }
56	
57	Version precedence (resolved into the rendered YAML's `version` field):
58	  1. version_resolved if explicitly supplied (caller already ran the
59	     precedence rule). Used by step-05 when it has confirmed values.
60	  2. Otherwise: target_version if non-null.
61	  3. Otherwise: detected_version if non-null.
62	  4. Otherwise: "1.0.0".
63	
64	When target_version is set, the rendered YAML includes a `target_version`
65	field whose value MUST match `version` (the script enforces this
66	invariant before write — refuses to emit a brief that violates it).
67	
68	Output (success):
69	
70	  {
71	    "status":     "ok",
72	    "brief_path": "/abs/path/skill-brief.yaml",
73	    "version":    "<resolved version>",
74	    "bytes":      <integer>,
75	    "warnings":   ["string", ...]
76	  }
77	
78	Errors emit `{"status": "error", "message": "...", "field": "..."|null}`
79	to stderr and exit non-zero.
80

111	VALID_SOURCE_TYPES = {"source", "docs-only"}
112	VALID_SOURCE_AUTHORITIES = {"official", "community", "internal"}
113	VALID_FORGE_TIERS = {"Quick", "Forge", "Forge+", "Deep"}
114	VALID_SCOPE_TYPES = {
115	    "full-library",
116	    "specific-modules",
117	    "public-api",
118	    "component-library",
119	    "reference-app",
120	    "docs-only",
121	}
122	
123	
124	def _die(message: str, field: str | None = None, code: int = 1) -> None:
125	    payload = {"status": "error", "message": message}
126	    if field is not None:
127	        payload["field"] = field
128	    sys.stderr.write(json.dumps(payload) + "\n")
129	    sys.exit(code)
130	
131	
132	def resolve_version(ctx: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
133	    """Apply the version-precedence rule. Returns the resolved version string.
134	
135	    Uses `is not None` checks (not truthiness) so an explicitly-supplied
136	    empty string surfaces as a SEMVER_RE validation failure downstream
137	    rather than silently falling through to the next precedence level.
138	    """
139	    vr = ctx.get("version_resolved")
140	    if vr is not None:
141	        return vr
142	    tv = ctx.get("target_version")
143	    if tv is not None:
144	        return tv
145	    dv = ctx.get("detected_version")
146	    if dv is not None:
147	        return dv
148	    return "1.0.0"
149	
150	
151	def validate_context(ctx: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
152	    """Validate the context payload. Raises via _die on hard errors; returns warnings."""
153	    warnings: list[str] = []
154	
155	    # Required string fields
156	    for field in ("name", "source_repo", "language", "description",
157	                  "forge_tier", "created", "created_by"):
158	        v = ctx.get(field)
159	        if not v or not isinstance(v, str):
160	            _die(f"required field {field!r} missing or not a non-empty string", field=field)
161	
162	    # Name must be kebab
163	    if not KEBAB_RE.match(ctx["name"]):
164	        _die(
165	            f"name must be kebab-case (lowercase letters/digits/hyphens, no leading/trailing hyphen). "
166	            f"Got: {ctx['name']!r}",
167	            field="name",
168	        )
169	
170	    # forge_tier enum
171	    if ctx["forge_tier"] not in VALID_FORGE_TIERS:
172	        _die(
173	            f"forge_tier must be one of {sorted(VALID_FORGE_TIERS)}. Got: {ctx['forge_tier']!r}",
174	            field="forge_tier",
175	        )
176	
177	    # created ISO date
178	    if not ISO_DATE_RE.match(ctx["created"]):
179	        _die(
180	            f"created must be an ISO date (YYYY-MM-DD). Got: {ctx['created']!r}",
181	            field="created",
182	        )
183	
184	    # source_type (default 'source') and conditional doc_urls
185	    source_type = ctx.get("source_type", "source")
186	    if source_type not in VALID_SOURCE_TYPES:
187	        _die(
188	            f"source_type must be one of {sorted(VALID_SOURCE_TYPES)}. Got: {source_type!r}",
189	            field="source_type",
190	        )
191	
192	    doc_urls = ctx.get("doc_urls")
193	    if source_type == "docs-only":
194	        if not doc_urls or not isinstance(doc_urls, list) or len(doc_urls) == 0:
195	            _die("source_type=docs-only requires at least one entry in doc_urls", field="doc_urls")
196	    if doc_urls is not None:
197	        if not isinstance(doc_urls, list):
198	            _die("doc_urls must be an array of objects", field="doc_urls")
199	        for i, entry in enumerate(doc_urls):
200	            if not isinstance(entry, dict):
201	                _die(f"doc_urls[{i}] must be an object with at least a 'url' field", field="doc_urls")
202	            url = entry.get("url")
203	            if not url or not isinstance(url, str):
204	                _die(f"doc_urls[{i}].url is required and must be a non-empty string", field="doc_urls")
205	
206	    # source_authority (default 'community') with docs-only force rule
207	    source_authority = ctx.get("source_authority", "community")
208	    if source_authority not in VALID_SOURCE_AUTHORITIES:
209	        _die(
210	            f"source_authority must be one of {sorted(VALID_SOURCE_AUTHORITIES)}. Got: {source_authority!r}",
211	            field="source_authority",
212	        )
213	    if source_type == "docs-only" and source_authority != "community":
214	        warnings.append(
215	            f"source_authority forced to 'community' for docs-only (was {source_authority!r})"
216	        )
217	
218	    # scope object
219	    scope = ctx.get("scope")
220	    if not isinstance(scope, dict):
221	        _die("scope must be an object", field="scope")
222	    for sf in ("type", "include", "exclude", "notes"):
223	        if sf not in scope:
224	            _die(f"scope.{sf} is required", field=f"scope.{sf}")
225	    if scope["type"] not in VALID_SCOPE_TYPES:
226	        _die(
227	            f"scope.type must be one of {sorted(VALID_SCOPE_TYPES)}. Got: {scope['type']!r}",
228	            field="scope.type",
229	        )
230	    if not isinstance(scope["include"], list):
231	        _die("scope.include must be an array of glob strings", field="scope.include")
232	    if not isinstance(scope["exclude"], list):
233	        _die("scope.exclude must be an array of glob strings", field="scope.exclude")
234	    if not isinstance(scope["notes"], str):
235	        _die("scope.notes must be a string (use empty string when no notes)", field="scope.notes")
236	
237	    # target_version semver shape (when present)
238	    tv = ctx.get("target_version")
239	    if tv is not None:
240	        if not isinstance(tv, str) or not SEMVER_RE.match(tv):
241	            _die(
242	                f"target_version must be full X.Y.Z semver (with optional v prefix and pre-release/build). "
243	                f"Got: {tv!r}",
244	                field="target_version",
245	            )
246	
247	    detected = ctx.get("detected_version")
248	    if detected is not None and (not isinstance(detected, str) or not SEMVER_RE.match(detected)):
249	        # Warn rather than HALT — auto-detection upstream may surface odd shapes
250	        warnings.append(
251	            f"detected_version {detected!r} is not full X.Y.Z semver — falling through to default 1.0.0"
252	        )
253	
254	    return warnings
255	
256	
257	def assemble_brief(ctx: dict[str, Any], resolved_version: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
258	    """Build the final brief dict that will be YAML-dumped, in canonical key order."""
259	    source_type = ctx.get("source_type", "source")
260	    source_authority = ctx.get("source_authority", "community")
261	    if source_type == "docs-only":
262	        source_authority = "community"  # forced
263	
264	    brief: dict[str, Any] = {
265	        "name": ctx["name"],
266	        "version": resolved_version,
267	        "source_type": source_type,
268	        "source_repo": ctx["source_repo"],
269	        "language": ctx["language"],
270	        "description": ctx["description"],
271	        "forge_tier": ctx["forge_tier"],
272	        "created": ctx["created"],
273	        "created_by": ctx["created_by"],
274	        "scope": {
275	            "type": ctx["scope"]["type"],
276	            "include": list(ctx["scope"]["include"]),
277	            "exclude": list(ctx["scope"]["exclude"]),
278	            "notes": ctx["scope"]["notes"],
279	        },

66	### 3. Write the Brief
67	
68	Assemble the brief context as a JSON object containing the approved field values from steps 01-04:
69	
70	```json
71	{
72	  "name":             "{approved skill name}",
73	  "target_version":   "{target_version from step 01, or null}",
74	  "detected_version": "{auto-detected version from step 02, or null}",
75	  "source_type":      "{source or docs-only}",
76	  "source_repo":      "{approved source repo or doc site URL}",
77	  "language":         "{approved language}",
78	  "description":      "{approved description}",
79	  "forge_tier":       "{Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep}",
80	  "created":          "{current ISO date YYYY-MM-DD}",
81	  "created_by":       "{user_name}",
82	  "scope": {
83	    "type":    "{approved scope type}",
84	    "include": ["{approved include patterns}"],
85	    "exclude": ["{approved exclude patterns}"],
86	    "notes":   "{approved scope notes or empty string}"
87	  },
88	  "doc_urls":         [{"url": "...", "label": "..."}],   // omit when not applicable
89	  "scripts_intent":   "{detect|none|free-text}",          // omit if "detect"
90	  "assets_intent":    "{detect|none|free-text}",          // omit if "detect"
91	  "source_authority": "{official|community|internal}"     // optional; defaults to "community" and is forced to "community" when source_type=docs-only
92	}
93	```
94	
95	Pipe it into the writer script:
96	
97	```bash
98	echo '<context-json>' | uv run {writeSkillBriefScript} write --target {resolved-target-path}
99	```
100	
101	The script:
102	- Validates the context against `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skill-brief.v1.json`
103	- Applies the version-precedence rule from `{versionResolutionFile}`
104	- Enforces the `target_version == version` invariant (refuses to write a brief that violates it)
105	- Renders YAML in canonical key order (byte-stable across runs)
106	- Atomically writes the file via temp + fsync + rename (no half-written file ever visible)
107	- Emits a JSON success envelope on stdout: `{"status":"ok","brief_path":"…","version":"…","bytes":…,"warnings":[…]}`
108	
109	**On script failure (non-zero exit):**
110	- Exit 1 (validation/invariant): The error JSON on stderr names the offending field. This indicates a context-assembly bug, not a user error — surface the message to the user, log it, then HALT.
111	  - Interactive: **HALT** — display the error JSON's `message` field.
112	  - Headless: emit the error envelope per §4b with `halt_reason: "input-invalid"`, then `exit 2`.
113	- Exit 2 (I/O failure): The atomic write failed (target unwritable, disk full, etc.).
114	  - Interactive: **HALT** — "**Error:** Failed to write skill-brief.yaml. Check that the directory is writable and try again."
115	  - Headless: emit the error envelope per §4b with `halt_reason: "write-failed"`, then `exit 4`.
116	
117	**On success:** capture `brief_path` and `version` from the response envelope — both are needed for §4b and §6.
118	
119	**Draft cleanup.** After a successful write, remove `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/.brief-draft.json` if it exists (`rm -f` — silent on absent). The draft was a step-01 §7 checkpoint covering the in-flight workflow window; once the brief is written it is no longer meaningful. In headless mode this rm is a no-op (drafts are only written interactively).
120	
121	### 4b. Headless Result Envelope (Canonical)
122	
123	This section is the canonical envelope-emission reference for the workflow. Every headless emission — the success terminal here and every HARD HALT in step-01/02/05 — uses this contract. Remote sites point here instead of restating it.
124	
125	**Success (this call site only — emitted from §3 directly):**

306	    # Emit source_authority only when non-default — schema lists it as Optional
307	    # in src/skf-brief-skill/assets/skill-brief-schema.md, and unconditional
308	    # emission would inject the field into round-tripped briefs that previously
309	    # omitted it (false-drift signal for diff tooling). Consumers default to
310	    # "community" when the field is absent.
311	    if source_authority != "community":
312	        brief["source_authority"] = source_authority
313	
314	    return brief

316	
317	def render_yaml(brief: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
318	    """Dump the brief dict as YAML in canonical key order with a leading document marker.
319	
320	    The step-05 §3 template shows leading and trailing `---` markers, but those were
321	    wrapping the example YAML for documentation purposes — actual on-disk YAML uses
322	    only the leading `---` (or none). A trailing `---` would start a second empty
323	    document and break callers that use `yaml.safe_load` (which expects a single
324	    document) — and skf-create-skill / audit-skill / update-skill all use
325	    `yaml.safe_load`, so consistency with their loaders matters.
326	    """
327	    body = yaml.safe_dump(
328	        brief,
329	        default_flow_style=False,
330	        sort_keys=False,
331	        allow_unicode=True,
332	    )
333	    return "---\n" + body
334	
335	
336	def atomic_write(target: Path, content: str) -> int:
337	    """Crash-safe write via temp + fsync + rename. Returns bytes written."""
338	    target.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
339	    tmp = target.with_name(target.name + ".skf-tmp")
340	    encoded = content.encode("utf-8")
341	    try:
342	        fd = os.open(tmp, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC, 0o644)
343	        try:
344	            os.write(fd, encoded)
345	            os.fsync(fd)
346	        finally:
347	            os.close(fd)
348	        os.replace(tmp, target)
349	    except OSError as e:
350	        if tmp.exists():
351	            try:
352	                tmp.unlink()
353	            except OSError:
354	                pass
355	        _die(f"atomic write failed for {target}: {e}", code=2)
356	    return len(encoded)
357	
358	
359	def cmd_write(target: Path) -> int:
360	    raw = sys.stdin.read()
361	    if not raw or not raw.strip():
362	        _die("write: empty stdin (expected JSON brief context)")
363	    try:
364	        ctx = json.loads(raw)
365	    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
366	        _die(f"write: invalid JSON on stdin: {e}")
367	    if not isinstance(ctx, dict):
368	        _die("write: context payload must be a JSON object")
369	
370	    warnings = validate_context(ctx)
371	    resolved_version = resolve_version(ctx)
372	    if not SEMVER_RE.match(resolved_version):
373	        _die(
374	            f"resolved version {resolved_version!r} is not full X.Y.Z semver — "
375	            f"check version_resolved / target_version / detected_version inputs",
376	            field="version",
377	        )
378	
379	    brief = assemble_brief(ctx, resolved_version)
380	    content = render_yaml(brief)
381	    bytes_written = atomic_write(target, content)
382	
383	    response = {
384	        "status": "ok",
385	        "brief_path": str(target.resolve()),
386	        "version": resolved_version,
387	        "bytes": bytes_written,
388	        "warnings": warnings,
389	    }
390	    print(json.dumps(response))
391	    return 0
392	
393	
394	def main() -> int:
395	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
396	        prog="skf-write-skill-brief",
397	        description="Schema-validated atomic writer for skill-brief.yaml.",
398	    )
399	    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="cmd", required=True)

  "new_string": "# Top-level keys accepted by the flat input form. Any other top-level key\n# is silently passed through to the nested context so callers can still\n# include version_resolved or other fields the validator understands.\n_FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS = (\"scope_type\", \"scope_include\", \"scope_exclude\", \"scope_notes\")\n_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS = (\n    \"name\",\n    \"version_resolved\",\n    \"target_version\",\n    \"detected_version\",\n    \"source_type\",\n    \"source_repo\",\n    \"language\",\n    \"description\",\n    \"forge_tier\",\n    \"created\",\n    \"created_by\",\n    \"doc_urls\",\n    \"scripts_intent\",\n    \"assets_intent\",\n    \"source_authority\",\n)\n\n\ndef flat_to_nested(flat: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Translate the flat brief-context shape into the nested shape consumed by validate_context.\n\n    Flat shape: scope is split across four top-level keys (`scope_type`,\n    `scope_include`, `scope_exclude`, `scope_notes`) instead of nested\n    under a `scope` object. Optional top-level fields (`doc_urls`,\n    `scripts_intent`, `assets_intent`, `source_authority`,\n    `target_version`, `detected_version`) may be absent or null —\n    they are simply dropped from the nested output, which matches the\n    existing validator's `ctx.get(...) is None` semantics.\n\n    Any unknown top-level keys are passed through unchanged so future\n    additions don't require a translator update.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not isinstance(flat, dict):\n        _die(\"write --from-flat: payload must be a JSON object\")\n\n    nested: dict[str, Any] = {}\n\n    # Pass through every top-level key that isn't part of the flat-scope\n    # split. Drop None values so optional fields behave as \"absent\" in\n    # the nested form (validate_context uses `is not None` checks).\n    for key, value in flat.items():\n        if key in _FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS:\n            continue\n        if value is None:\n            continue\n        nested[key] = value\n\n    # Build the nested scope object. All four scope_* keys must be supplied\n    # by the caller (the schema requires scope.type, scope.include,\n    # scope.exclude, scope.notes); we surface a clear error if any is\n    # missing rather than silently substituting defaults that mask a bug.\n    if any(k in flat for k in _FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS):\n        scope: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        if \"scope_type\" in flat and flat[\"scope_type\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"type\"] = flat[\"scope_type\"]\n        if \"scope_include\" in flat and flat[\"scope_include\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"include\"] = flat[\"scope_include\"]\n        if \"scope_exclude\" in flat and flat[\"scope_exclude\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"exclude\"] = flat[\"scope_exclude\"]\n        if \"scope_notes\" in flat and flat[\"scope_notes\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"notes\"] = flat[\"scope_notes\"]\n        nested[\"scope\"] = scope\n    return nested\n\n\ndef cmd_write(target: Path, from_flat: bool = False) -> int:\n    raw = sys.stdin.read()\n    if not raw or not raw.strip():\n        _die(\"write: empty stdin (expected JSON brief context)\")\n    try:\n        ctx = json.loads(raw)\n    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n        _die(f\"write: invalid JSON on stdin: {e}\")\n    if not isinstance(ctx, dict):\n        _die(\"write: context payload must be a JSON object\")\n\n    if from_flat:\n        ctx = flat_to_nested(ctx)\n\n    warnings = validate_context(ctx)"
}

        assert tmp_target.exists()

    def test_write_rejects_invalid_context(self, tmp_target):
        ctx = _baseline_ctx()
        ctx["forge_tier"] = "InvalidTier"
        code, _, stderr = self._write(tmp_target, ctx)
        assert code == 1
        err = json.loads(stderr.strip())
        assert err["status"] == "error"
        assert err["field"] == "forge_tier"
        assert not tmp_target.exists()  # nothing written

    def test_write_rejects_target_version_invariant_violation(self, tmp_target):
        ctx = _baseline_ctx()
        ctx["version_resolved"] = "9.9.9"  # forces this
        ctx["target_version"] = "1.0.0"  # disagrees
        code, _, stderr = self._write(tmp_target, ctx)
        assert code == 1
        err = json.loads(stderr.strip())
        assert err["field"] == "target_version"
        assert "invariant" in err["message"]

    def test_write_rejects_empty_stdin(self, tmp_target):
        proc = subprocess.run(
            [sys.executable, str(SCRIPT_PATH), "write", "--target", str(tmp_target)],
            input="", capture_output=True, text=True,
        )
        assert proc.returncode == 1
        err = json.loads(proc.stderr.strip())
        assert "empty stdin" in err["message"]

    def test_write_atomic_on_replace(self, tmp_target):
        # First write
        ctx_a = _baseline_ctx()
        ctx_a["description"] = "Description A"
        self._write(tmp_target, ctx_a)
        first_content = tmp_target.read_text()

        # Second write with different description
        ctx_b = _baseline_ctx()
        ctx_b["description"] = "Description B"
        self._write(tmp_target, ctx_b)
        second_content = tmp_target.read_text()

        # File was replaced cleanly
        assert "Description B" in second_content
        assert "Description A" not in second_content
        assert first_content != second_content
        # No leftover .skf-tmp file
        assert not tmp_target.with_name(tmp_target.name + ".skf-tmp").exists()

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CLI: write subcommand (subprocess)
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------


class TestCLIWrite:
    def _write(self, target: Path, ctx: dict) -> tuple[int, dict, str]:
        proc = subprocess.run(
            [sys.executable, str(SCRIPT_PATH), "write", "--target", str(target)],
            input=json.dumps(ctx),
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
        )
        out = json.loads(proc.stdout) if proc.stdout.strip() else {}
        return proc.returncode, out, proc.stderr

    def test_write_creates_file_and_returns_path(self, tmp_target):
        code, response, _ = self._write(tmp_target, _baseline_ctx())
        assert code == 0
        assert response["status"] == "ok"
        assert response["brief_path"] == str(tmp_target.resolve())

{
  "command": "git add src/shared/scripts/skf-write-skill-brief.py test/test-skf-write-skill-brief.py && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfeat(write-skill-brief): add --from-flat input mode for prose-driven callers\n\nThe writer previously accepted only a nested context shape with scope as\na sub-object and the LLM at step-05 §3 had to know which optional fields\nto omit (doc_urls when not applicable, scripts_intent/assets_intent when\n\"detect\", source_authority when \"community\", target_version when not\nset). This conditional-omit logic is the most expensive HALT-typo site\nin the brief workflow because the writer rejects malformed input.\n\nAdd a flat-input mode behind --from-flat where:\n- scope is split across four top-level keys (scope_type, scope_include,\n  scope_exclude, scope_notes) instead of a nested sub-object\n- every optional top-level field can be passed as null without the\n  caller deciding whether to include it; the translator drops them\n- a flat_to_nested() helper converts to the nested form, then the\n  existing validate / resolve / assemble / render / atomic-write\n  pipeline runs unchanged\n\nBackwards compatible — the original `write` (nested) path is unchanged\nand all 52 prior tests still pass. 23 new tests cover the flat\ntranslator (pure-function) and the --from-flat CLI subcommand\n(end-to-end), including a byte-identical-output assertion that\nguarantees flat and nested produce the same YAML for equivalent inputs.\n\nThe step-05 §3 workflow swap to use --from-flat lands in the next\ncommit.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit D3-1: writer + tests"
}
