Write the Brief\n\nAssemble the brief context as a **flat** JSON object — every approved value is a top-level key, scope is split across four `scope_*` keys instead of nested, and every optional field is passed as `null` when not set rather than conditionally omitted. This eliminates the \"decide what to omit\" cognitive load that previously made this the most expensive HALT-typo site in the workflow:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"name\":             \"{approved skill name}\",\n  \"target_version\":   \"{target_version from step 01, or null}\",\n  \"detected_version\": \"{auto-detected version from step 02, or null}\",\n  \"source_type\":      \"{source or docs-only}\",\n  \"source_repo\":      \"{approved source repo or doc site URL}\",\n  \"language\":         \"{approved language}\",\n  \"description\":      \"{approved description}\",\n  \"forge_tier\":       \"{Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep}\",\n  \"created\":          \"{current ISO date YYYY-MM-DD}\",\n  \"created_by\":       \"{user_name}\",\n  \"scope_type\":       \"{approved scope type}\",\n  \"scope_include\":    [\"{approved include patterns}\"],\n  \"scope_exclude\":    [\"{approved exclude patterns}\"],\n  \"scope_notes\":      \"{approved scope notes or empty string}\",\n  \"doc_urls\":         null | [{\"url\": \"...\", \"label\": \"...\"}],\n  \"scripts_intent\":   null | \"{detect|none|free-text}\",\n  \"assets_intent\":    null | \"{detect|none|free-text}\",\n  \"source_authority\": null | \"{official|community|internal}\"\n}\n```\n\nPipe it into the writer script with the `--from-flat` flag:\n\n```bash\necho '<context-json>' | uv run {writeSkillBriefScript} write --target {resolved-target-path} --from-flat\n```\n\nThe script translates flat → nested internally, drops the null optional fields, and runs the same schema validation and atomic write as before — pass every key always, the writer decides what reaches the YAML."

The JSON schema is confirmed to validate the output YAML shape (the brief artifact), not the input context. The schema has no flat keys (`scope_type`, `scope_include`, etc.) — it only has the nested `scope` object. No schema update is needed for this change.

Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile the findings.

---

Reviewing `src/shared/scripts/skf-write-skill-brief.py` and `src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-05-write-brief.md` and `test/test-skf-write-skill-brief.py` on branch `refactor/skf-brief-writer-flat-input`.

All eight focus areas have been walked in full. Here is the complete findings report.

---

## Behavior Preservation (Focus Area 1) — No Issue

The null-to-absent equivalence holds for every optional field. `validate_context` uses `ctx.get(field)` (returns `None` for absent keys) and `ctx.get(field, default)` (uses default for absent keys) throughout. The translator drops null values from the nested dict, so a null-passed field and an absent field produce identical `ctx.get(...)` outcomes:

- `source_type` (line 214): `ctx.get("source_type", "source")` — absent and null-dropped both fall back to `"source"`.
- `doc_urls` (line 221): `ctx.get("doc_urls")` — absent and null-dropped both return `None`; the `if doc_urls is not None:` guard skips validation for both.
- `source_authority` (line 236): `ctx.get("source_authority", "community")` — same pattern.
- `target_version` (line 267) and `detected_version` (line 276): `ctx.get(...)` returning `None` causes the `is not None` guard to skip validation in both cases.

Behavior is identical across both paths. No issue.

---

## Critical Issues

None.

---

## Important Issues

### Issue 1: `_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` is defined but never referenced — silent forward-compat divergence risk

**Confidence: 82**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-write-skill-brief.py`, lines 392–408

`_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` is defined as a module-level constant but is never used anywhere in the implementation. The actual passthrough logic in `flat_to_nested` (lines 433–438) works by iterating over all keys in `flat` and excluding only `_FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS` — which is correct. The `_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` constant is documentation-only dead code.

The risk: a future contributor adds a new passthrough key to `_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` thinking that list gates what passes through, when it has no effect on runtime behavior. The actual gate is `_FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS`. This creates a maintenance trap with no test to catch the divergence.

The fix is either: (a) delete `_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` and rely on the comment at line 388–390 to document forward compat, or (b) add a test that calls `flat_to_nested` with a key not in `_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` and verifies it passes through — which `test_passes_through_unknown_keys` (line 470) already does, but a reader wouldn't connect it to `_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` since the test doesn't reference that constant.

---

### Issue 2: `scope_notes: ""` is silently treated as a scope-key-absent indicator, making the translator inconsistent with the other scope_* fields

**Confidence: 80**

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-write-skill-brief.py`, line 452

```python
if "scope_notes" in flat and flat["scope_notes"] is not None:
    scope["notes"] = flat["scope_notes"]
```

The condition `is not None` is correct for optional top-level fields (where null means "omit"), but `scope_notes` is a **required** scope subkey — `validate_context` at line 263 requires `scope.notes` to be a string, with `""` being the documented valid minimum. If an LLM passes `"scope_notes": null` in the flat JSON, the translator silently drops the key, and `validate_context` then dies with `scope.notes is required` (line 253) — which is a correct error, but the step-05 template at line 85 only shows `"{approved scope notes or empty string}"`, not `null`, so this path requires the LLM to have misread the template.

This is not a bug in the happy path (the step-05 template correctly shows scope_notes as non-nullable), but the three sibling scope_* fields (`scope_type`, `scope_include`, `scope_exclude`) use the same `is not None` guard. If an LLM passes any of them as null, the error message is `scope.<field> is required` — which is accurate. The inconsistency is that `scope_notes` has a meaningful falsy valid value (`""`), so a future reader might wonder why the guard isn't `if "scope_notes" in flat:` (without the null check). A brief comment on line 452 clarifying that `scope_notes: null` is intentionally treated as absent (causing a validator error) would remove ambiguity.

This is a documentation/clarity issue rather than a behavior bug — functionality is correct.

---

## Focus Areas Confirmed Clean

**Focus Area 3 (byte-identical assertion):** `test_flat_write_produces_identical_yaml_to_nested_write` (line 495) compares actual file bytes via `nested_target.read_text() == flat_target.read_text()`. Both subprocesses run against the same baseline data. The assertion is on the written file content, not the JSON success envelope. Correct.

**Focus Area 4 (step-05 §3 instruction clarity):** The `null | "..."` pseudo-syntax at step-05 lines 86–89 reads unambiguously as "pass null OR pass one of these values." The instruction "pass every key always, the writer decides what reaches the YAML" is consistent with the template showing all optional fields with null as a valid value. `scope_notes` correctly shows no null option. No contradictions with other guidance in the file.

**Focus Area 5 (backwards compatibility):** `TestCLIWrite._write` (line 321–329) uses the nested path (no `--from-flat` flag) and is unchanged. All prior nested-path tests continue to run against the original code path.

**Focus Area 6 (JSON Schema sync):** `skill-brief.v1.json` validates the output YAML artifact (the rendered `brief` dict). It contains no flat keys (`scope_type`, etc.) — only the nested `scope` object. The input shape change is entirely pre-validation and requires no schema update.

**Focus Area 7 (docstring accuracy):** The flat input example at lines 76–95 accurately reflects the implementation: `scope_notes: ""` (not nullable), optional fields shown as `null | <value>` (translator drops null), and `version_resolved` is absent from the flat example but the comment on `_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS` documents that it still passes through (forward compat). The docstring is accurate.

**Focus Area 8 (incomplete scope test coverage):** `test_flat_write_rejects_missing_scope_type` (line 545) covers the partial-scope path (scope_type absent while other scope_* are present). The runtime error message (`scope.type is required`, field `scope.type`) reaches the user correctly. No silent failure. The absence of individual tests for missing `scope_include`, `scope_exclude`, or `scope_notes` is a minor coverage gap, but behavior is correct and the error messages are usable.

  "old_string": "# Top-level keys accepted by the flat input form. Any other top-level key\n# is silently passed through to the nested context so callers can still\n# include version_resolved or other fields the validator understands.\n_FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS = (\"scope_type\", \"scope_include\", \"scope_exclude\", \"scope_notes\")\n_FLAT_PASSTHROUGH_KEYS = (\n    \"name\",\n    \"version_resolved\",\n    \"target_version\",\n    \"detected_version\",\n    \"source_type\",\n    \"source_repo\",\n    \"language\",\n    \"description\",\n    \"forge_tier\",\n    \"created\",\n    \"created_by\",\n    \"doc_urls\",\n    \"scripts_intent\",\n    \"assets_intent\",\n    \"source_authority\",\n)\n\n\ndef flat_to_nested(flat: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Translate the flat brief-context shape into the nested shape consumed by validate_context.\n\n    Flat shape: scope is split across four top-level keys (`scope_type`,\n    `scope_include`, `scope_exclude`, `scope_notes`) instead of nested\n    under a `scope` object. Optional top-level fields (`doc_urls`,\n    `scripts_intent`, `assets_intent`, `source_authority`,\n    `target_version`, `detected_version`) may be absent or null —\n    they are simply dropped from the nested output, which matches the\n    existing validator's `ctx.get(...) is None` semantics.\n\n    Any unknown top-level keys are passed through unchanged so future\n    additions don't require a translator update.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not isinstance(flat, dict):\n        _die(\"write --from-flat: payload must be a JSON object\")\n\n    nested: dict[str, Any] = {}\n\n    # Pass through every top-level key that isn't part of the flat-scope\n    # split. Drop None values so optional fields behave as \"absent\" in\n    # the nested form (validate_context uses `is not None` checks).\n    for key, value in flat.items():\n        if key in _FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS:\n            continue\n        if value is None:\n            continue\n        nested[key] = value\n\n    # Build the nested scope object. All four scope_* keys must be supplied\n    # by the caller (the schema requires scope.type, scope.include,\n    # scope.exclude, scope.notes); we surface a clear error if any is\n    # missing rather than silently substituting defaults that mask a bug.\n    if any(k in flat for k in _FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS):\n        scope: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        if \"scope_type\" in flat and flat[\"scope_type\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"type\"] = flat[\"scope_type\"]\n        if \"scope_include\" in flat and flat[\"scope_include\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"include\"] = flat[\"scope_include\"]\n        if \"scope_exclude\" in flat and flat[\"scope_exclude\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"exclude\"] = flat[\"scope_exclude\"]\n        if \"scope_notes\" in flat and flat[\"scope_notes\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"notes\"] = flat[\"scope_notes\"]\n        nested[\"scope\"] = scope\n    return nested",
  "new_string": "# Top-level keys that get folded into the nested `scope` sub-object — every\n# other top-level key passes through unchanged so future additions to the\n# schema don't require a translator update.\n_FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS = (\"scope_type\", \"scope_include\", \"scope_exclude\", \"scope_notes\")\n\n\ndef flat_to_nested(flat: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Translate the flat brief-context shape into the nested shape consumed by validate_context.\n\n    Flat shape: scope is split across four top-level keys (`scope_type`,\n    `scope_include`, `scope_exclude`, `scope_notes`) instead of nested\n    under a `scope` object. Optional top-level fields (`doc_urls`,\n    `scripts_intent`, `assets_intent`, `source_authority`,\n    `target_version`, `detected_version`) may be absent or null —\n    they are simply dropped from the nested output, which matches the\n    existing validator's `ctx.get(...) is None` semantics.\n\n    Any unknown top-level keys are passed through unchanged so future\n    additions don't require a translator update.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not isinstance(flat, dict):\n        _die(\"write --from-flat: payload must be a JSON object\")\n\n    nested: dict[str, Any] = {}\n\n    # Pass through every top-level key that isn't part of the flat-scope\n    # split. Drop None values so optional fields behave as \"absent\" in\n    # the nested form (validate_context uses `is not None` checks).\n    for key, value in flat.items():\n        if key in _FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS:\n            continue\n        if value is None:\n            continue\n        nested[key] = value\n\n    # Build the nested scope object. All four scope_* keys (type, include,\n    # exclude, notes) are required at the schema level — null is\n    # intentionally treated as \"absent\" here (same as omitting the key)\n    # so validate_context surfaces `scope.<field> is required` rather\n    # than e.g. `scope.notes must be a string`. The `\"\"` valid minimum\n    # for scope_notes must therefore be passed as the literal empty\n    # string, not null.\n    if any(k in flat for k in _FLAT_SCOPE_KEYS):\n        scope: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        if \"scope_type\" in flat and flat[\"scope_type\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"type\"] = flat[\"scope_type\"]\n        if \"scope_include\" in flat and flat[\"scope_include\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"include\"] = flat[\"scope_include\"]\n        if \"scope_exclude\" in flat and flat[\"scope_exclude\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"exclude\"] = flat[\"scope_exclude\"]\n        if \"scope_notes\" in flat and flat[\"scope_notes\"] is not None:\n            scope[\"notes\"] = flat[\"scope_notes\"]\n        nested[\"scope\"] = scope\n    return nested"
}
```

4c920b94 feat(write-skill-brief): add --from-flat input mode for prose-driven callers
---
commit 4c920b94651cdad425c022b0b7bbfeeee5ded3cc
Author: armel <armelhbobdad@gmail.com>
Date:   Sun May 3 00:11:57 2026 +0400

    feat(write-skill-brief): add --from-flat input mode for prose-driven callers
    
    The writer previously accepted only a nested context shape with scope as
    a sub-object and the LLM at step-05 §3 had to know which optional fields
    to omit (doc_urls when not applicable, scripts_intent/assets_intent when
    "detect", source_authority when "community", target_version when not
    set). This conditional-omit logic is the most expensive HALT-typo site
    in the brief workflow because the writer rejects malformed input.
    
    Add a flat-input mode behind --from-flat where:
    - scope is split across four top-level keys (scope_type, scope_include,
      scope_exclude, scope_notes) instead of a nested sub-object
    - every optional top-level field can be passed as null without the
      caller deciding whether to include it; the translator drops them
    - a flat_to_nested() helper converts to the nested form, then the
      existing validate / resolve / assemble / render / atomic-write
      pipeline runs unchanged
    
    Backwards compatible — the original `write` (nested) path is unchanged
    and all 52 prior tests still pass. 23 new tests cover the flat
    translator (pure-function) and the --from-flat CLI subcommand
    (end-to-end), including a byte-identical-output assertion that
    guarantees flat and nested produce the same YAML for equivalent inputs.
    
    The step-05 §3 workflow swap to use --from-flat lands in the next
    commit.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Entire-Checkpoint: 60bd216d85e0

commit 6bd79f3a95f6f2365d82da1b64a1cca3b85214e7
Author: armel <armelhbobdad@gmail.com>
Date:   Sun May 3 00:13:01 2026 +0400

    refactor(skf-brief-skill): step-05 §3 uses --from-flat — eliminates conditional-omit logic at the JSON-typo HALT site
    
    The original §3 had the LLM hand-stitch a 14-key nested JSON with four
    'omit when not applicable' conditionals around the optional fields.
    That conditional logic plus the nested scope sub-object made this the
    most expensive HALT-typo site in the workflow — a missed comma or
    mis-omitted field reaches the writer's schema validator and produces
    an input-invalid HALT after the user already approved the brief at
    step-04.
    
    Switch to the writer's flat input form: every field is top-level,
    scope is split across four scope_* keys, and every optional field is
    passed as null rather than conditionally omitted. The writer's
    flat_to_nested() translator drops the nulls before validation, so
    the LLM's only job is to assemble all 18 keys uniformly — no
    conditional omit, no nested object.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

  "content": "# /// script\n# requires-python = \">=3.9\"\n# dependencies = []\n# ///\n\"\"\"SKF Detect Language — deterministic primary-language detection from a flat file tree.\n\nSingle source of truth for the language-detection rule table that\nskf-brief-skill step-02 §3 applies. The rule walk is purely deterministic:\nmanifest-file presence first (Cargo.toml → rust, package.json → js/ts,\netc.), then extension-frequency fallback. Moving it into a shared script\nsaves ~150-250 tokens per workflow invocation and removes a quiet drift\nseam — the JS-vs-TS disambiguation in particular benefits from being\nco-located with a deterministic rule rather than restated in prose.\n\nDetection rules (apply in order, first match wins):\n\n  1. package.json (with optional tsconfig.json companion):\n       tsconfig.json present → typescript (high)\n       tsconfig.json absent  → javascript (high)\n  2. Cargo.toml          → rust (high)\n  3. pyproject.toml | setup.py | setup.cfg → python (high)\n  4. go.mod              → go (high)\n  5. pom.xml             → java (high)\n  6. build.gradle.kts    → kotlin (high)\n  7. build.gradle (Groovy) — check tree:\n       src/main/kotlin/  → kotlin (medium)\n       else              → java (medium)\n  8. *.csproj | *.sln    → csharp (high)\n  9. Gemfile             → ruby (high)\n 10. Extension-frequency fallback over the full tree.\n       dominant extension >= 50% of code files → that language (medium)\n       no clear winner                        → unknown (low)\n\nCLI:\n  echo '{\"tree\": [\"path1\", \"path2\", ...]}' | uv run skf-detect-language.py\n  uv run skf-detect-language.py --json '{\"tree\": [...]}'\n\nInput (JSON object on stdin or via --json):\n  tree — list of repo-relative file paths (required, non-empty)\n\nOutput (JSON on stdout):\n  language          — javascript | typescript | rust | python | go\n                       | java | kotlin | csharp | ruby | swift | php\n                       | unknown\n  confidence        — \"high\" | \"medium\" | \"low\"\n  detection_source  — human-readable string naming what fired (manifest\n                       basename, extension share, etc.)\n  fallback_to_extension_frequency — bool (true when rule 10 fired)\n\nExit codes:\n  0 — recommendation produced (even when language is \"unknown\")\n  2 — internal error (bad JSON input, IO failure)\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom collections import Counter\nfrom typing import Any\n\n# Manifest basenames the rule table checks. Kept tight — tree-wide pattern\n# matches (e.g. *.csproj) are handled separately to avoid false positives\n# from generated artifacts under build/ or dist/.\n_MANIFEST_RULES: list[tuple[str, str, str]] = [\n    # (basename, language, detection_source)\n    (\"Cargo.toml\", \"rust\", \"Cargo.toml present\"),\n    (\"pyproject.toml\", \"python\", \"pyproject.toml present\"),\n    (\"setup.py\", \"python\", \"setup.py present\"),\n    (\"setup.cfg\", \"python\", \"setup.cfg present\"),\n    (\"go.mod\", \"go\", \"go.mod present\"),\n    (\"pom.xml\", \"java\", \"pom.xml present\"),\n    (\"build.gradle.kts\", \"kotlin\", \"build.gradle.kts present\"),\n    (\"Gemfile\", \"ruby\", \"Gemfile present\"),\n]\n\n# Glob-style suffix rules — matches anywhere in the tree. csproj/sln are\n# C#-specific. Ordered so the first hit wins.\n_SUFFIX_RULES: list[tuple[str, str, str]] = [\n    (\".csproj\", \"csharp\", \".csproj project file present\"),\n    (\".sln\", \"csharp\", \".sln solution file present\"),\n]\n\n# Extension → language for the frequency fallback. Tightly limited to source\n# extensions; keeping this small avoids the fallback being skewed by\n# auto-generated assets (.json, .md, .yaml, etc.).\n_EXTENSION_TO_LANGUAGE: dict[str, str] = {\n    \".py\": \"python\",\n    \".js\": \"javascript\",\n    \".jsx\": \"javascript\",\n    \".mjs\": \"javascript\",\n    \".cjs\": \"javascript\",\n    \".ts\": \"typescript\",\n    \".tsx\": \"typescript\",\n    \".rs\": \"rust\",\n    \".go\": \"go\",\n    \".java\": \"java\",\n    \".kt\": \"kotlin\",\n    \".kts\": \"kotlin\",\n    \".cs\": \"csharp\",\n    \".rb\": \"ruby\",\n    \".swift\": \"swift\",\n    \".php\": \"php\",\n}\n\n_DOMINANCE_THRESHOLD = 0.50\n\n\ndef _die(message: str, code: int = 2) -> None:\n    sys.stderr.write(f\"skf-detect-language: {message}\\n\")\n    sys.exit(code)\n\n\ndef _basename(path: str) -> str:\n    return path.rsplit(\"/\", 1)[-1]\n\n\ndef _has_basename(tree: list[str], basename: str) -> bool:\n    return any(_basename(p) == basename for p in tree)\n\n\ndef _has_suffix(tree: list[str], suffix: str) -> bool:\n    return any(p.endswith(suffix) for p in tree)\n\n\ndef _has_path_segment(tree: list[str], segment: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True when any path contains the given segment (e.g. 'src/main/kotlin/').\"\"\"\n    return any(segment in p for p in tree)\n\n\ndef _extension(path: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the lowercased file extension including the leading dot, or empty string.\"\"\"\n    base = _basename(path)\n    if \".\" not in base or base.startswith(\".\"):\n        return \"\"\n    return \".\" + base.rsplit(\".\", 1)[-1].lower()\n\n\ndef _frequency_fallback(tree: list[str]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Score the tree by source-extension frequency. Returns the result envelope.\"\"\"\n    counter: Counter[str] = Counter()\n    for path in tree:\n        ext = _extension(path)\n        if ext in _EXTENSION_TO_LANGUAGE:\n            counter[ext] += 1\n\n    total = sum(counter.values())\n    if total == 0:\n        return {\n            \"language\": \"unknown\",\n            \"confidence\": \"low\",\n            \"detection_source\": \"no recognized source extensions in tree\",\n            \"fallback_to_extension_frequency\": True,\n        }\n\n    # Largest-share extension wins. Ties (e.g. equal counts) broken by\n    # the natural order of _EXTENSION_TO_LANGUAGE — Counter.most_common\n    # is stable for equal counts and we don't rely on which loses.\n    top_ext, top_count = counter.most_common(1)[0]\n    share = top_count / total\n    language = _EXTENSION_TO_LANGUAGE[top_ext]\n\n    if share >= _DOMINANCE_THRESHOLD:\n        confidence = \"medium\"\n        source = f\"extension frequency: {top_ext} is {top_count}/{total} of source files ({share:.0%})\"\n    else:\n        confidence = \"low\"\n        source = f\"no dominant extension: top is {top_ext} at {top_count}/{total} ({share:.0%}, threshold {int(_DOMINANCE_THRESHOLD * 100)}%)\"\n        # Still surface the best-guess language; caller / step-03 §4 lets\n        # the user override on low confidence.\n    return {\n        \"language\": language,\n        \"confidence\": confidence,\n        \"detection_source\": source,\n        \"fallback_to_extension_frequency\": True,\n    }\n\n\ndef detect(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Apply the documented rule walk. Always returns a recommendation.\"\"\"\n    tree = payload.get(\"tree\")\n    if not isinstance(tree, list):\n        _die(\"payload.tree must be an array of repo-relative file paths\")\n    if len(tree) == 0:\n        _die(\"payload.tree must be non-empty\")\n\n    # Rule 1 — package.json (with tsconfig.json disambiguation)\n    if _has_basename(tree, \"package.json\"):\n        if _has_basename(tree, \"tsconfig.json\"):\n            return {\n                \"language\": \"typescript\",\n                \"confidence\": \"high\",\n                \"detection_source\": \"package.json + tsconfig.json present\",\n                \"fallback_to_extension_frequency\": False,\n            }\n        return {\n            \"language\": \"javascript\",\n            \"confidence\": \"high\",\n            \"detection_source\": \"package.json present (no tsconfig.json)\",\n            \"fallback_to_extension_frequency\": False,\n        }\n\n    # Rules 2-9 — single-basename manifests\n    for basename, language, source in _MANIFEST_RULES:\n        if _has_basename(tree, basename):\n            # Rule 7 — build.gradle (Groovy) needs tree disambiguation between\n            # Java and Kotlin. The rule fires for build.gradle.kts via the\n            # earlier _MANIFEST_RULES entry; this path is reached only for\n            # the Groovy variant (no .kts suffix).\n            if basename == \"pom.xml\":\n                # Plain pom.xml — no Kotlin signal needed\n                pass\n            return {\n                \"language\": language,\n                \"confidence\": \"high\",\n                \"detection_source\": source,\n                \"fallback_to_extension_frequency\": False,\n            }\n\n    # Rule 7b — build.gradle (Groovy DSL): check src/main/kotlin/ to disambiguate.\n    # This rule sits *after* the basename loop because build.gradle.kts is\n    # already covered by the loop and we don't want it to pre-empt that match.\n    if _has_basename(tree, \"build.gradle\"):\n        if _has_path_segment(tree, \"src/main/kotlin/\"):\n            return {\n                \"language\": \"kotlin\",\n                \"confidence\": \"medium\",\n                \"detection_source\": \"build.gradle (Groovy) + src/main/kotlin/ present\",\n                \"fallback_to_extension_frequency\": False,\n            }\n        return {\n            \"language\": \"java\",\n            \"confidence\": \"medium\",\n            \"detection_source\": \"build.gradle (Groovy) present without src/main/kotlin/ — defaulting to java\",\n            \"fallback_to_extension_frequency\": False,\n        }\n\n    # Rules 8-9 — suffix-based (csproj, sln)\n    for suffix, language, source in _SUFFIX_RULES:\n        if _has_suffix(tree, suffix):\n            return {\n                \"language\": language,\n                \"confidence\": \"high\",\n                \"detection_source\": source,\n                \"fallback_to_extension_frequency\": False,\n            }\n\n    # Rule 10 — extension-frequency fallback\n    return _frequency_fallback(tree)\n\n\ndef _parse_argv(argv: list[str]) -> dict:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\n        description=\"Detect primary language from a flat repo file tree by walking the documented rule table.\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\"--json\", help=\"JSON payload (alternative to stdin)\")\n    args = parser.parse_args(argv)\n    raw = args.json if args.json is not None else sys.stdin.read()\n    if not raw or not raw.strip():\n        _die(\"empty input (expected JSON payload on stdin or via --json)\")\n    try:\n        payload = json.loads(raw)\n    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:\n        _die(f\"invalid JSON input: {e}\")\n    if not isinstance(payload, dict):\n        _die(\"payload must be a JSON object\")\n    return payload\n\n\ndef main(argv: list[str]) -> int:\n    payload = _parse_argv(argv)\n    result = detect(payload)\n    json.dump(result, sys.stdout, ensure_ascii=False)\n    sys.stdout.write(\"\\n\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))\n"
}

Walking it in prose costs ~150-250\ntokens per workflow invocation and leaves a quiet drift seam (the\nJS-vs-TS disambiguation is implicit in the prose ordering).\n\nMove the rule walk into a shared script with full test coverage. Rule 7\n(build.gradle Groovy DSL) gets the same treatment — checks the tree\nfor src/main/kotlin/ to disambiguate java vs kotlin at medium\nconfidence.\n\nReturns {language, confidence, detection_source,\nfallback_to_extension_frequency}. The workflow swap to invoke the\nscript from step-02 §3 lands in the next commit.\n\n29 pytest cases cover all 9 manifest rules, both rule-7 disambiguation\nbranches, the rule-precedence ordering, the extension-frequency\nfallback (dominant / no-winner / no-recognized-extensions), validation\nerrors, and CLI wiring. Wired into test:python.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",

The comment on line 64 says manifest basenames are "kept tight" to "avoid false positives from generated artifacts under build/ or dist/". But _SUFFIX_RULES uses `p.endswith(suffix)` (line 123-124), which matches anywhere in the tree including paths like `vendor/deps/something.csproj` — the comment says "matches anywhere in the tree" which is intentional for C# (all .csproj files are C#, so any depth is fine). This is correct by design.

Let me check whether `.sln` could cause false positives with other file types:

`.sln` as a file extension is exclusively used by Visual Studio Solution files in practice. The risk is acceptably low, and the comment explicitly documents the "anywhere in tree" behavior. Not an issue above the threshold.

Reviewing the diff between `main` and `refactor/skf-brief-detect-language-script`. The scope is `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-language.py`, `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/test/test-skf-detect-language.py`, `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-brief-skill/steps-c/step-02-analyze-target.md`, and `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/package.json`.

---

## Review Results

Two issues meet the >= 80 confidence threshold. Both are in the script itself.

---

## Critical

None.

---

## Important

### Issue 1 — Dead code with a completely wrong comment (confidence: 95)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-language.py`, lines 207-213

The comment block at lines 207-210 says:

```
# Rule 7 — build.gradle (Groovy) needs tree disambiguation between
# Java and Kotlin. The rule fires for build.gradle.kts via the
# earlier _MANIFEST_RULES entry; this path is reached only for
# the Groovy variant (no .kts suffix).
```

This comment is factually wrong about what code it is annotating. `build.gradle` appears nowhere in `_MANIFEST_RULES` (lines 66-76). The loop at line 205 iterates over that table, so `basename` can never equal `"build.gradle"` inside it. The comment describes code that does not exist at this location.

Immediately under the comment, lines 211-213 are dead code:

```python
if basename == "pom.xml":
    # Plain pom.xml — no Kotlin signal needed
    pass
```

`basename == "pom.xml"` is true only when `pom.xml` was found in the tree, but the `if` body is `pass`, and the `return` on line 214 runs unconditionally immediately after — the `if`/`pass` has no effect whatsoever on control flow or output. This was clearly scaffolded for disambiguation logic that was subsequently moved to the post-loop block at line 224, and the scaffold was never cleaned up. The comment above it was copied from that post-loop context and now misdirects the reader.

**Fix:** Remove the dead block and replace the comment with one that accurately describes the loop invariant:

```python
    # Rules 2-6 and 8-9 — single-basename manifests walked in priority order.
    # build.gradle is NOT in this table; it requires post-loop disambiguation (Rule 7b below).
    for basename, language, source in _MANIFEST_RULES:
        if _has_basename(tree, basename):
            return {
                "language": language,
                "confidence": "high",
                "detection_source": source,
                "fallback_to_extension_frequency": False,
            }
```

---

### Issue 2 — Missing test for `pom.xml` + `build.gradle` co-presence (confidence: 82)

**File:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/test/test-skf-detect-language.py`

The code path where `pom.xml` and `build.gradle` coexist in the same tree is a non-obvious interaction. `pom.xml` is in `_MANIFEST_RULES` at index 5 (after Cargo.toml, pyproject.toml, setup.py, setup.cfg, go.mod) and causes the loop to return `java` (high) before Rule 7b (`build.gradle`) is ever evaluated. `build.gradle` itself is absent from `_MANIFEST_RULES` entirely — this is by design, but readers of the code (and future maintainers) will not expect that `build.gradle` in a Maven project is silently ignored.

This is the one realistic "two manifests at once" scenario that the documented rule table calls out implicitly ("pom.xml or build.gradle → Java"), and there is currently no test asserting that `pom.xml` wins when both are present. The existing tests cover manifest-vs-extension-frequency precedence (`test_package_json_takes_precedence_over_dominant_python_extensions`, `test_cargo_toml_takes_precedence_over_python_files`) but not manifest-vs-manifest.

**Fix:** Add one test covering the interaction:

```python
def test_pom_xml_takes_precedence_over_build_gradle():
    """pom.xml fires in the manifest loop (java, high); build.gradle is post-loop and never reached."""
    result = mod.detect({"tree": ["pom.xml", "build.gradle", "src/main/java/com/example/App.java"]})
    assert result["language"] == "java"
    assert result["confidence"] == "high"
    assert result["fallback_to_extension_frequency"] is False
```

---

## Confirmed Correct (all other focus areas)

**Behavior preservation (focus 1a):** For any tree where the original prose would have produced an ambiguous "JavaScript/TypeScript" signal, the script returns either `javascript` (high) when only `package.json` is present, or `typescript` (high) when both `package.json` and `tsconfig.json` are present. No ambiguous return is possible from Rule 1.

**pom.xml + build.gradle (focus 1b):** As traced above — `pom.xml` is at index 5 of `_MANIFEST_RULES`, fires first, returns java (high), and the function exits before Rule 7b is evaluated. Correct behavior; the result matches the original prose's "pom.xml or build.gradle → Java" intent.

**`_basename` correctness (focus 3):** `rsplit("/", 1)[-1]` correctly handles `"Cargo.toml"` (no slash → returns full string), `"src/main.unknown"` (returns `"main.unknown"`), and `""` (returns `""`). Leading slashes (absolute paths like `/etc/foo`) return `"foo"` — correct. The repo exclusively uses forward-slash repo-relative paths, so Windows backslash paths are out of scope.

**`_extension` edge cases (focus 4):** `"package.lock.json"` → `".json"` (correct). `".gitignore"` → `""` (correct, dotfile guard at line 135). `"foo."` → `"."` which is not in `_EXTENSION_TO_LANGUAGE` (treated as no extension — acceptable).

**Extension dominance threshold (focus 5):** The `>=` comparison at line 164 means exactly 50% (3-of-6 files) returns `medium`. The comment and test case at lines 157-161 using 2/2/1 (40%) correctly return `low`. The boundary behavior is intentional and correct.

**Low-confidence + unknown (focus 6):** When no recognized source extensions exist, `language: "unknown"` is returned with `confidence: "low"`. When extensions exist but no clear winner, the best-guess language is returned with `confidence: "low"`. Step-02 §3's instruction "if confidence is low (or unknown is returned for language)" covers both cases correctly.

**CI wiring (focus 7):** `package.json` line 56 correctly appends `test/test-skf-detect-language.py` as the last positional argument before the `-v` flag. Placement is consistent with all other shared-script tests in the list.

**Workflow integration (focus 8):** Step-02 §3 passes only `tree` to the script. The script's `detect()` reads only `payload["tree"]`. No mismatch; no missing or extra fields.

**Gemfile test coverage (focus 9):** Covered by the parametrized `test_manifest_rules_high_confidence` at line 73-92.
